L322: **Reviewer's Comments:** In Study MG0003, the Applicant enrolled adult gMG subjects who were primarily anti-AChR antibody positive (89.5%). A smaller number of subjects were anti-MuSK antibody positive (10.5%; 8% based on repeat testing at the baseline visit). The primary endpoint, change from baseline in MG-ADL score to day 43, and the key secondary endpoints, change from baseline to day 43 in MG-C and QMG scores were evaluated in the overall study population that combined both anti-AChR and anti-MuSK antibody positive gMG subjects. The analysis of the overall study population showed a statistically significant difference favoring rozanolixizumab at ≈7 mg/kg and ≈10 mg/kg doses in the mean change from baseline to day 43 in the MG-ADL total scores (-3.4 points in the rozanolixizumab treated groups compared with -0.8 in the placebo group, p&lt;0.001). A statistically significant difference favoring rozanolixizumab was also observed in the mean change from baseline to day 43 in the QMG and MG-C scores (p&lt;0.001). The subjects receiving rozanolixizumab had a greater than 5-point improvement in the MG-C score and QMG score over the same period.
L323: In MG0003 study, a clinical response was defined by the Applicant in the MG-ADL total score as at least a 2-point improvement, and in the QMG and the MG-C total scores as at least a 3-point improvement. The proportion of clinical responders was numerically higher for rozanolixizumab compared to the placebo for the change in the MG-ADL total score at day 43 from the baseline (68.2% and 61.2% in the ≈7 mg/kg and ≈10 mg/kg treatment groups compared with 28.4% in the placebo group, p&lt;0.001) and the odds ratios (95% CI) were 5.8 (2.1, 14.9) and 4.3 (1.6, 11.8) for the rozanolixizumab ≈7 mg/kg and ≈10 mg/kg groups, respectively.
L324: *Clinical improvement in the MG-ADL total score was seen as early as day 8 (first postbaseline assessment) in the rozanolixizumab treatment groups compared to the placebo group and the median time to first MG-ADL response was estimated at 16 days and 22 days in the \approx 7 mg/kg and \approx 10 mg/kg treatment groups (Figure 3). A similar pattern of clinical response was seen in the favor of rozanolixizumab in the QMG score and the MG-C score during the same treatment period.*
L325: *Changes in the secondary endpoints of MGSPRO domain scores support the overall primary and key secondary endpoint responses favoring rozanolixizumab in Study MG0003, indicating clinical benefit of rozanolixizumab that was perceived by subjects with gMG as meaningfully impacting their daily life. In the pre-IND meeting minutes dated December 9, 2016, the Type B end-of-phase 2 meeting minutes dated June 14, 2019, and the Type C written responses dated November 3, 2020 (IND132407), the Division of Neurology 1 (DNI) asked the Applicant to make additional edits to MGSPRO including items reduction, removal of redundant and overlapping items, and reducing the length of the item and recall period to optimize the PRO. The DNI recommended that the Applicant conduct exit interviews which may provide an opportunity to obtain a subject's input on what they consider to be a meaningful change. However, the recall period, the instrument item redundancy, and the instrument items length were unchanged, and no exit interviews were conducted for the phase 3 study. [REDACTED] (b) (4), additional work is needed for MGSPRO to be fit-for-purpose including optimizing the length of the instrument, reducing the concepts overlap with those assessed by other scales (MG-ADL and MG-C), determining the content validity at the item-level, and obtaining patient's input by exit interviews among others. The reviewer notes that with further edits and additional analyses, this instrument may become fit-for-purpose and be useful to support a secondary endpoint in future studies of gMG.*
L326: *None of the subjects on rozanolixizumab required rescue therapy, whereas three subjects (4.5%) in the placebo group needed rescue therapy (IVIg) during the treatment period. One of these subjects also needed IVIg therapy during the observation period. Three subjects in the rozanolixizumab treatment groups received IVIg during the observation period including two in the \approx 10 mg/kg group and one in the \approx 7 mg/kg group 24, 41, and 37 days after the last infusion, respectively.*
L327: *Note that a large majority (n=180 out of 191; 94.2%) of the subjects who were on concomitant medications including steroids, nonsteroidal immunosuppressant drugs, and acetylcholine esterase inhibitor drugs at baseline visit did not require any changes in the dose, frequency, addition, or removal for the MG standard care during the double-blind treatment period. Three subjects receiving the placebo infusions required addition of a concomitant drug in the same time period (Table 23).*
L328: *These results indicate an improvement in the activities of daily living, motor function, and disease severity in a greater number of subjects treated with rozanolixizumab compared to those treated with placebo, supporting the clinical efficacy of rozanolixizumab.*

L339: ## 6.2.2.4.3. Subgroup Analyses for the Primary Efficacy Endpoint
L340: Analyses were conducted to assess the treatment effect for subgroups defined by various demographics and clinical characteristics. The subgroup analyses of age, sex, region and weight
L341: to evaluate the efficacy of primary endpoint in Table 24 were generated by the FDA reviewer. To be consistent with the methodologies applied in the primary efficacy analyses of the primary endpoint and the key secondary endpoints, the output's results on subgroups of age, sex and region were from the combined mixed model repeated measures (MMRM) model using an unstructured covariance matrix except for the 95% CIs, which were based on the same method used to produce the multiplicity adjusted 95% CIs for the primary endpoint and key secondary endpoints. The output's results of subgroup analysis of weight including 97.5% CI were from the MMRM model using a covariance matrix of first order autoregressive structure. Of note, the sample size of the subgroup of weight less than 50 kg was small, thus limiting the ability to produce the multiplicity adjusted 95% CIs with the method used for the primary endpoint and limiting the ability to use an MMRM model with an unstructured covariance matrix.
L342: Changes from baseline in MG-ADL score to day 43 of rozanolixizumab of \approx7 mg/kg dose level group and rozanolixizumab of \approx10 mg/kg dose level group compared to placebo group in study subjects with gMG were generally consistent across subgroups of age, sex, region, and weight.

L347: **Reviewer Assessment:** There was an improvement (decrease) in MG-ADL score from baseline at day 43 across the subgroups of age, sex, geographical region, and bodyweight in the subjects treated with rozanolixizumab compared to placebo in the phase 3 controlled Study MG0003. Therefore, it appears that rozanolixizumab is effective in both women and men across the range of bodyweight and in different geographical region.
L348: For age greater than 65, the reviewer notes that it is difficult to adequately determine efficacy in this age group due to small sample size. There were a total of 49 (24.8%) geriatric subjects enrolled in the study. Of these 49 subjects, 46 (93.9%) subjects had anti-AChR-MG and the remaining 3 (6.1%) subjects had anti-MuSK-MG. Only one subject with anti-MuSK-MG was randomized to rozanolixizumab. The reviewer recommends including in the label the following language for geriatric use: "Clinical studies of rozanolixizumab did not include sufficient number of patients aged 65 years and older to determine whether they respond differently from younger adults."
L349: ## 6.3. Key Efficacy Review Issues
L350: ## 6.3.1. Efficacy in Anti-AChR and Anti-MuSK Antibody Sero-positive Myasthenia Gravis
L351: ## Issue
L352: Evaluate whether the evidence provided by the Applicant supports the proposed indication "Adult (b) (4) with generalized myasthenia gravis who are anti-AChR or anti-MuSK antibody positive"
L353: ## **Background**
L354: In the label, the Applicant proposed the following indication, “for the treatment of generalized myasthenia gravis (gMG) in adult (b)(4) who are anti-acetylcholine receptor (AChR) or antimuscle-specific tyrosine kinase (MuSK) antibody positive”.
L355: There are no approved treatments for patients with gMG who are anti-MuSK antibody seropositive (anti-MuSK-gMG). Eculizumab, ravulizumab, and efgartigimod have been approved for the treatment of patients with gMG who are anti-AChR antibody seropositive (anti-AChR-gMG). Steroids and nonsteroidal immunosuppressant drugs (azathioprine, mycophenolate, tacrolimus, methotrexate, and cyclosporine, rituximab) have been used in anti-MuSK gMG patients; however, it is often challenging to achieve a long-term effective remission. About 10 to 15% of patients do not respond to steroids or other immunosuppressant drugs.
L356: ## **Assessment**
L357: ## **Baseline Demographics of the Subjects With Anti-MuSK Antibody Positive gMG, Study MG0003**
L358: In Study MG0003, randomization into treatment groups was stratified by the presence of MG-specific anti-AChR or anti-MuSK autoantibody. A total of 16 gMG subjects who tested positive for anti-MuSK antibody at baseline were evaluated during the Study MG0003 (Table 25). This subgroup comprised 8% of total subjects in the study. Of 16 subjects, 8 were assigned to rozanolixizumab treatment arms and 8 were assigned to placebo. One subject in the placebo group also tested positive for AChR antibody. Twelve (75%) subjects were female with balanced distribution between the rozanolixizumab and placebo treatment arms. The subjects had comparable ages and weight-tier distribution at baseline between the groups. There were three subjects (18.7%) who were age 65 years and older. As indicated by the MGFA classification, the subjects had moderate to severe gMG with a larger proportion of the randomized subjects in the MGFA Class III (43.7%) followed by Class II (37.4%), and the rest in the MGFA class IV (18.7%) which reflects the protocol eligibility criteria. All three subjects with severe gMG (MGFA Class IV) were assigned to the placebo group. At baseline, the MG-ADL and QMG scores were balanced between the placebo and rozanolixizumab groups. The mean MG-ADL score was 9.3 (compared to total 200 subjects: 8.3) and the mean QMG score was 15.9 (compared to total 200 subjects: 15.6). The mean disease duration was 11.1 years (compared to total 200 subjects: 8.6 years), and 56.2% (compared to total subjects: 29.5%) of the randomized subjects had a history of MG crisis in the past. Relatively fewer randomized subjects had undergone thymectomy (18.7%). All treatment groups were balanced in regard to standard of care including the use of steroids, nonsteroidal immunosuppressant drugs, and acetylcholine esterase inhibitor drugs.
L359: **Reviewer's Comments:** The subjects with anti-MuSK antibodies comprised 8% of the study subjects based on the testing at baseline visit, which is consistent with the disease prevalence in the intended patient population (Gilhus et al. 2019). Overall, there appears to be acceptable balance of demographic characteristics between the control and treatment groups that adequately represents the demographics of the intended subject population with moderate to severe gMG who are seropositive for anti-MuSK antibodies. The sex imbalance and the majority of the randomized subjects with the age younger than 65 years are consistent with the natural
L360: *history of the anti-MuSK gMG disease which tends to occur in the 30's with a strong female predominance.*

L386: ## Analysis
L387: In Study MG0003, the primary endpoint, change from baseline in MG-ADL score to day 43, and the key secondary endpoints, change from baseline to day 43 in MG-C and QMG scores were evaluated in the overall study population that combined both anti-AChR-gMG and anti-MuSK-gMG subjects. The tables (25, 26, and 27) show comparisons in the primary and the key secondary endpoints between the anti-AChR-gMG and anti-MuSK-gMG subjects. As expected from the disease prevalence, only a small number of subjects with anti-MuSK-gMG (n=16; 8% of the total 200 subjects) were evaluated in the study. Two anti-MuSK-gMG subjects discontinued treatment prematurely one each in the placebo (subject ID: MG0003-[REDACTED](b) (6); day 22) and rozanolixizumab ≈10 mg/kg treatment arms (subject ID: MG0003-[REDACTED](b) (6); received the first two infusions only) due to the lack of efficacy and TEAE of upper abdominal pain and vomiting, respectively. For subject MG0003-[REDACTED](b) (6), the primary endpoint analysis of the MG-ADL change from baseline to the day 85 visit (time interval 43 days) was used according to the prespecified statistical analysis plan (SAP) criterion. The key secondary endpoints QMG and MG-C assessments were not available for the day 85 visit. The subject assigned to placebo did not continue the study during the observation period after treatment discontinuation and therefore had missing data for the primary and key secondary endpoints.
L388: Table 26 shows a difference favoring rozanolixizumab at \approx 7 mg/kg and \approx 10 mg/kg doses in the mean change from baseline to day 43 in the MG-ADL total scores (difference from placebo, -6.27 and -4.21 points, respectively).
L389: Table 27 and Table 28 show a difference favoring rozanolixizumab in the mean change from baseline to day 43 in the MG-C and QMG scores in the anti-MuSK-MG subjects similar to the anti-AChR-MG subjects over the same period.
L390: None of the subjects receiving rozanolixizumab required rescue therapy. The double-blind treatment period in Study MG0003 consisted of a single treatment cycle. In Study MG0007 OLE study, a consistent improvement from baseline to day 43 in the MG-ADL scores was observed in the subjects with anti-MuSK-gMG during the first 4 treatment cycles (cycle 1, -5.1; cycle 2, -5; cycle 3, -3.8; and cycle 4, -3; n \le 8 per cycle). Similar consistent improvements in the MG-C score and QMG score were observed in the first 4 treatment cycles (Source: MG0007 CSR dated August 17, 2022). However, these MG0007 findings are limited by the absence of placebo-controlled data and the few anti-MuSK-MG subjects.
L391: ## Pathogenic IgG Levels in Anti AChR-ab and Anti-MuSK-ab Subjects in Study MG0003
L392: The Applicant collected pathogenic IgG data in Study MG0003. Based on the Applicant's analysis, levels of anti-AChR autoantibodies decreased from baseline for both the rozanolixizumab \approx 7 mg/kg and \approx 10 mg/kg groups in a dose-dependent manner. At day 15 (1 week after the second dose), there was a median reduction in anti-AChR autoantibodies of 53.0% for the rozanolixizumab \approx 7 mg/kg group and 69.9% for the \approx 10 mg/kg group. The median maximum reduction from baseline in anti-AChR autoantibodies was 73.0% for the rozanolixizumab \approx 7 mg/kg group and 81.4% for the rozanolixizumab \approx 10 mg/kg group.
L393: For anti-MuSK autoantibodies, 16 subjects had detectable levels (4, 4, and 8 in subjects in the rozanolixizumab \approx 7 mg/kg, \approx 10 mg/kg, and placebo groups, respectively). Per the Applicant, the median maximum reduction from baseline in anti-MuSK autoantibodies was 36.6% for the rozanolixizumab \approx 7 mg/kg group and 57.8% for the rozanolixizumab \approx 10 mg/kg group. The review team confirmed these data which can be seen in the plots below (Figure 4, Figure 5). The number inside the box represents the actual number of subjects in that treatment group.

L398: ## **Conclusion**
L399: These results indicate an improvement in the activities of daily living, motor function, and disease severity in a greater number of subjects treated with rozanolixizumab compared to those treated with the placebo, supporting the clinical efficacy of rozanolixizumab in the anti-AChR-gMG as well as anti-MuSK-gMG subjects. These data are limited by the limited number of anti-
L400: MuSK-gMG subjects. The reviewer notes that the placebo group had more subjects with severe anti-MuSK-gMG (MGFA Class III and IV) compared to those randomized to rozanolixizumab which may have contributed to a more magnified treatment response in the rozanolixizumab treatment groups. However, the mean MG-ADL scores were similar at baseline among the treatment and placebo groups. The data from this study support the Applicant's proposed indication to include anti-AChR-gMG and anti-MuSK-gMG in the product labeling.
L401: ## 6.3.2. Length of Treatment Cycles and the Time Between Treatment Cycles
L402: ## Issue
L403: Determine the minimum length of time between treatment cycles of rozanolixizumab that is supported by the available efficacy and safety data.
L404: ## Background
L405: The Applicant's proposed prescribing information provides the following information about the treatment cycle of rozanolixizumab and retreatment following completion of a treatment cycle:
L406: “(b) (4)

[REDACTED]

[REDACTED]

[REDACTED]

”
L407: The Applicant is proposing the dosing regimen of rozanolixizumab as a subcutaneous infusion once weekly for 6 weeks and the subsequent treatment cycles based on clinical evaluation in the draft label. The double-blind, placebo-controlled treatment period in Study MG0003 included 6-weekly infusions, representing one treatment cycle. The dosing regimen in the open-label Study MG0004 consisted weekly infusions up to 52 weeks and the safety data from the first 6 weeks representing a treatment cycle were included in the Safety Pool S1. In the ongoing open-label Study MG0007, subjects are receiving treatment cycles of a subcutaneous infusion once weekly for 6 weeks with repeat treatment cycles administered based on MG symptom worsening. The subjects are assessed every 4 weeks between treatment cycles. The criteria for starting a new treatment cycle are defined as an increase of 2 points on MG-ADL or 3 points on QMG scale between assessments, based on investigator's assessments. It is not usual clinical practice to conduct MG-ADL or QMG scoring during standard clinical care office visits. Prescribing physicians and patients would need to decide on both initial treatment and retreatment for myasthenia gravis based on clinical examination and the patients' perception of their clinical status.
L408: As per the protocol study design, the minimum time to start the next treatment cycle is 4 weeks following the last dose of the previous 6-weekly infusions treatment which translates to treatment cycles of 9 weeks. Therefore, the maximum number of completed treatment cycles would be 5 within one year. Refer to Section 7.5 for the detailed review of rozanolixizumab exposure and adequacy of the safety database.
L409: Safety Pool S1 consists of all rozanolixizumab-treated subjects who have undergone at least 1 treatment cycle, defined as receiving at least 1 infusion of rozanolixizumab in any 6-week treatment period:
L410: - MG0003 (rozanolixizumab treatment arm only)
- MG0007 (up until the interim cut-off date), and
- MG0004 (the first 6 weeks of data to represent 1 treatment cycle)
L411: ## Assessment
L412: ## *Exposure to rozanolixizumab in the Randomized, Double-Blind, Placebo-Controlled Study MG0003, Safety Population*
L413: In Study MG0003, all subjects received at least 1 rozanolixizumab infusion in 1 treatment cycle. A total of 52 (81.3%) and 48 (69.6%) received all 6 infusions in the treatment cycle in the rozanolixizumab \approx7 mg/kg and \approx10 mg/kg groups, respectively. The mean number of infusions and mean treatment duration were similar across the treatment groups (Table 29). Mock infusions using placebo were given to two subjects in each of the rozanolixizumab treatment arms on day 29 and day 36 when IgG levels dropped below 1 g/L to reduce unblinding potential. Note, the data in Table 29 excludes mock transfusions.

L439: ## The Duration of a Treatment Cycle, Safety Pool S1, Studies MG0003, MG0004, and MG0007
L440: As per the 120-day safety update dated March 3, 2023, (eCTD sequence number 0012), there were 88 subjects with at least 5 treatment cycles (the maximum number of cycles possible per year in the study) and a median treatment cycle duration of the fifth cycle was 69.5 days or 9.9 weeks (**Table 33**). The average cycle duration was 90.6 days or 12.9 weeks for the cohort of subjects with exactly 5 treatment cycles.

L467: The minimum time for a treatment cycle within the first year in the Safety Pool S1 was 63 days or 9 weeks which was protocol-defined based on the combination of the 6-week treatment period and at least 4-week follow-up period. The minimum time for a treatment cycle in Study MG0007 within the first year was 64 days or 9 weeks which is similar to that observed in the combined Safety pool S1 data.
L468: The median treatment-free interval defined as the time from the last dose in the previous treatment cycle to first dose in the subsequent cycle was consistent between the rozanolixizumab dose groups in the Safety Population S1. The median interval was ≈9 weeks between cycles 1 and 2, ≈7 to 8 weeks between cycles 2 and 3, and ≈5 weeks between cycles 3 and 4 and cycles 4 and 5 (MG0007 CSR Section 9.1.9 dated August 17, 2022). Note that the minimum time to initiate a repeat treatment cycle from the last dose in the previous cycle was 4 weeks as defined by the protocol in Study MG0007.
L469: ## **Conclusion**
L470: Treatment periods should be 6 weekly SC infusions, as was conducted in the pivotal efficacy Study MG0003. Based on the data from the cyclical OLE treatment Study MG0007, the median time to retreatment for the maximum feasible 5 treatment cycles per year should be 5 weeks from the last dose in the previous treatment cycle to first dose in the subsequent cycle per year.
L471: Safety data are limited on treatment cycles shorter than 9 weeks (6 weekly SC infusions followed by 4 weeks observation period), based on the minimum protocol-defined intertreatment cycle
L472: periods in Study MG0007. However, this should allow most patients' gMG to be treated when it is anticipated to be clinically indicated (i.e., approximately 5 weeks after completion of the previous treatment cycle). The reviewer recommends informing prescribers that the safety of initiating subsequent cycles sooner than 63 days or 9 weeks from the start of the previous treatment cycle has not been established for rozanolixizumab.
L473: ## 6.3.3. Is the Proposed Dosing Regimen Appropriate for the General Patient Population for Which the Indication Is Being Sought?
L474: ## Issue
L475: Determine if the Applicant's proposed 2-fixed dosing regimen of rozanolixizumab for treatment of gMG patients is adequate.
L476: ## Background
L477: The Applicant has made efforts to simplify the rozanolixizumab dosing regimen throughout the clinical development program. Initially, weight-based dosing was employed in early phase 1 and phase 2 studies. This was followed by the implementation of weight-tiered dosing in the gMG phase 3 studies (Table 36). The Applicant proposed a 2-fixed dosing regimen for labeling:
L478: (b) (4) and 420 mg for patients weighing (b) (4) &lt;50 kg.
L479: **Table 36. Generalized Myasthenia Gravis Phase 3 Dose Levels and Weight Tiers and Proposed Fixed Doses**
L480: Body weight	Rozanolixizumab doses in Phase 3 program	Proposed fixed doses	Primary evidence to support dose
≈7mg/kg	≈10mg/kg
(b) (4) <50kg	280mg	420mg	420mg	MG0003 and MG0007 clinical E&S
≥50 to <70kg	(b) (4)	MG0003 and MG0007 clinical E&S
≥70 to <100kg	MG0003 and MG0007 clinical E&S
≥100kg	Modeling and simulation
L481: ≈=approximate dose; E&amp;S=efficacy and safety
L482: Source: The Applicant's Clinical Overview Page 25, Table 3-1
L483: **Table 37. Applicant's Proposed Dose Based on Body Weight**
L484: Body weight of patient	Dose
(b) (4) <50 kg	420 mg
(b) (4)	(b) (4) mg
L485: Source: The Applicant's draft label, Page 2, Table 1
L486: ## Assessment
L487: The Applicant's 2-fixed dose regimen in patients weighing less than 100 kg is consistent (either \approx 7 mg/kg or \approx 10 mg/kg) with the weight-tiered regimen in subjects less than 100 kg evaluated in MG0003 and is acceptable from a clinical pharmacology perspective.
L488: However, in subjects weighing 100 kg or more, the proposed dose of (b) (4) mg is (b) (4) the weight-tiered regimen (\approx 7 mg/kg or \approx 10 mg/kg) evaluated in Study MG0003. Additional exploratory analyses suggest that in subjects weighing 100 kg or more, drug exposures may be lower than those following either of the doses (\approx 7 mg/kg or \approx 10 mg/kg) evaluated in MG0003 (Figure 6, below. Refer to Section 14.5. for more details). It is unclear if these dose/exposure differences translate into clinically relevant differences in total IgG, anti-AChR or anti-MuSK IgG and/or clinical endpoint (MG-ADL).
L489: Based on these considerations, the clinical pharmacology review team recommends 840 mg in patients weighing 100 kg or more, as evaluated in MG0003. The Applicant was informed of the review team's recommendation, and concurred.
L490: **Figure 6. PK Simulation of Six Weekly SC Infusion of Rozanolixizumab for Different Dose Regimens Across Body Weight Groups**
L491: Source: Reviewer's Analysis.
L492: Note: Doses for the 2-fixed dose regimen are 420 mg in patients weighing &lt;50 kg and (b) (4) Q1W). For the weight-tiered regimen, fixed doses were defined across 4 body weight tiers (for \approx 7 mg/kg: 35 to &lt;50 kg: 280 mg, 50 to &lt;70 kg: 420 mg, 70 to &lt;100 kg: 560 mg, \ge 100 kg: 840 mg; for \approx 10 mg/kg: 35 to &lt;50 kg: 420 mg, 50 to &lt;70 kg: 560 mg, 70 to &lt;100 kg: 840 mg, \ge 100 kg: 1120 mg).
L493: Note: Six weekly doses were simulated for each dosing regimen.
L494: Abbreviations: \approx/, approximate dose; Q1W, once a week; SC, subcutaneous
L495: ## **Conclusion**
L496: In summary, considering that subjects weighing 100 kg or more comprise 23% of the phase 3 study population, there is a concern regarding underdosing in patients weighing 100 kg or more. For subjects weighing 100 kg or more, the efficacy of rozanolixizumab at proposed fixed dose regimen of (b)(4) mg cannot be reliably predicted. Therefore, we recommend 840 mg for subjects weighing 100 kg or more, as evaluated in Study MG0003. This recommendation leads to a modification in the dose table, as illustrated below.
L497: **Table 38. Recommended Dose by Body Weight (Modified by FDA Reviewer)**
L498: Body Weight of Patient	Dose
<50 kg	420 mg
≥50 kg to <100 kg	560 mg
≥100 kg	840 mg
L499: Source: FDA clinical pharmacology reviewer
L500: ## 7. Safety (Risk and Risk Management)
L501: ## 7.1. Potential Risks or Safety Concerns Based on Nonclinical Data
L502: No adverse effects were seen in male and female cynomolgus monkeys administered subcutaneous (SC) doses of up to 150 mg/kg UCB7665 every 3 days for 4, 13, and 26 weeks.
L503: In an enhanced pre- and postnatal development (ePPND) study, 3 groups of 19 pregnant female cynomolgus monkeys were administered SC injections of 0, 50, and 150 mg/kg UCB7665 SC every 3 days over the period of organogenesis (i.e., gestational day 20 until birth). Adverse effects consisted of early embryonic loss, reduced infant body weight, and impaired immune function in infants. There was no NOAEL for adverse effects in the ePPND study.
L504: ## 7.2. Potential Risks or Safety Concerns Based on Drug Class or Other Drug-Specific Factors
L505: Rozanolixizumab is a humanized anti-FcRn immunoglobulin G subclass 4 monoclonal antibody (IgG4p) monoclonal antibody that is proposed to block FcRn leading to enhanced IgG catabolism.
L506: Hypoalbuminemia was of concern because the Fc receptor is involved in the maintenance of serum albumin levels. Hypoalbuminemia is discussed in Section 7.7.5.
L507: Infections were of special interest because Fc receptor inhibition results in a reduction of circulating IgG, which potentially increases the risk of infection. Infections are discussed in Section 7.7.1.
L508: Cholesterol levels were of concern because elevated low-density lipoprotein (LDL) cholesterol levels led to premature termination of a phase 2 study (NCT03938545) after 12 weeks of treatment with RVT-1401, a human monoclonal antibody targeting FcRn developed for the treatment of subjects with Graves' ophthalmopathy (Men et al. 2021). However, a safety signal for elevated lipids was not identified in subjects treated with efgartigimod. Cholesterol levels are discussed in Section 7.6.2.6.
L509: ## 7.3. Potential Risks or Safety Concerns
## Identified Through Postmarket Experience
L510: Not applicable. Rozanolixizumab is a new molecular entity. It is not approved in other regions of the world.
L511: ## 7.3.1. Adverse Events Identified in Postmarket Experiences
L512: Not applicable. Rozanolixizumab is a new molecular entity. It is not approved in other regions of the world.
L513: ## 7.3.2. Expectations on Safety
L514: There was an increased frequency of infections, including serious infections and serious adverse events of aseptic meningitis in subjects treated with rozanolixizumab during clinical trials. Uncertainty exists in whether safety outcomes of infections would be potentially more serious in an unmonitored postmarket setting particularly in individuals with a greater risk of immunosuppression, such as the intended patient population with gMG.
L515: Hypersensitivity reactions including angioedema and rash have occurred in subjects treated with rozanolixizumab in clinical trials. Uncertainty exists in whether these safety outcomes would be potentially more serious in a more unmonitored outpatient setting.
L516: The safety and effectiveness of rozanolixizumab have not been established in patients with purely ocular MG, severe gMG, those with impending MG crisis or with MG crisis, and those who do not have detectable anti-AChR or anti-MuSK antibodies (seronegative MG) in clinical trials.
L517: The safety and effectiveness of rozanolixizumab in women who are pregnant, or breast-feeding have not been characterized in clinical trials. Because rozanolixizumab will be used in women of childbearing potential, a pregnancy outcome study is recommended as a postmarketing requirement (see Section 23).
L518: The risk of reactivation of tuberculosis and hepatitis has not been characterized. The risk of malignancy is not known.
L519: Immunization with vaccines during rozanolixizumab treatment has not been studied. The safety of immunization with live or live-attenuated vaccines and the response to immunization with any vaccine are unknown.
L520: Concomitant use of other medications that bind to the human FcRn may reduce the effectiveness of these compounds and that of rozanolixizumab.
L521: ## 7.3.3. Additional Safety Issues From Other Disciplines
L522: Vyvgart (efgartigimod alfa), also an FcRn blocking monoclonal antibody, was approved for the treatment of myasthenia gravis on December 17, 2021. Although IgG levels were reduced, a signal for infections were not seen in nonclinical studies. Data from clinical studies led to listing infection under Warnings and Precautions in the Vyvgart® label. The section stipulates monitoring for infections and, if serious infections occur, to consider withholding administration until the infection is resolved.
L523: ## 7.4. FDA Approach to the Safety Review
L524: Clinical study data were provided by the clinical data scientist support team and independently analyzed using JMP software. No major data quality or integrity issues were identified that would preclude a safety review of this BLA. There were no major issues identified with respect to recording, coding, and categorizing adverse events (AEs). The Applicant's translations of verbatim terms to the Medical Dictionary for Regulatory Activities (MedDRA) preferred terms for the events reported in Studies MG0003, MG0004, and MG0007 were found to be acceptable. The National Cancer Institute Common Terminology Criteria for Adverse Events (NCI-CTCAE) v.5.0 was used as a supportive tool to evaluate AEs; however, the final intensity of AEs was graded as mild, moderate, or severe by the investigator based on clinical judgement.
L525: Data from the pivotal double-blind, placebo-controlled Study MG0003 and the open-label extension Studies MG0004 and MG0007 formed the basis of the clinical safety evaluation (Table 39). Study MG0003 is the primary source of evidence of effectiveness, and the results of this study are presented individually in this review. The MG0003 study data include findings that occurred in subjects who received one (1) double-blind, placebo-controlled, six-week treatment cycle, which is the maximum number a subject could receive by protocol. Safety data from Study MG0003 will provide the primary description of safety, with Studies MG0004 and MG0007 providing supportive data.

L533: In Study MG0003, the treatment-emergent adverse events (TEAEs) were protocol-defined as any adverse event regardless of relatedness to the study drug occurring during the 6-week treatment period followed by an 8-week (56 days) observation period.
L534: Pool S1 consisted of all rozanolixizumab-treated subjects who have undergone at least one (1) treatment cycle, defined as receiving at least 1 infusion of rozanolixizumab in any 6-week treatment period. This pool includes data from MG0003 and MG0007 (until data cut-off July 8, 2022, 120-day safety update data cutoff date October 7, 2022) and the first 6 weeks of data from MG0004. For Safety Pool S1, the treatment-emergent adverse events were protocol-defined as,
L535: - Study MG0003: Adverse event starting on or after the time of first rozanolixizumab administration up to and including 8 weeks (56 days) after the last dose in the treatment cycle.
- Study MG0004 (first 6 weeks of all study subjects): Adverse event that was not present before the first administration of rozanolixizumab or any unresolved event already present before the first administration of rozanolixizumab that worsened in intensity following exposure to treatment, up to and including 8 weeks after the end of the treatment period or 8 weeks after the last rozanolixizumab administration in subjects who discontinued the study or rozanolixizumab
- Study MG0007: Adverse event starting on or after the time of first rozanolixizumab administration up to and including 8 weeks (56 days) after the last dose of each treatment cycle
L536: Any AEs occurring ≥8 weeks after the last infusion and before the next treatment cycle (intermittent period) were defined as non-TEAEs in Studies MG0003 and MG0007.
L537: Section 7.6.2 presents the safety data for the pivotal double-blind, placebo-controlled Study MG0003 alone. Section 17 presents pooled data Safety Pool S1 for Study MG0003, the first 6-week of treatment in Study MG0004, and the ongoing open-label cyclical treatment extension Study MG0007.
L538: Refer to Section 6.2.2 and Section 15 for the detailed description of the study design, eligibility criteria, objectives, and endpoints, and permitted standard of care treatment in Study MG0003 and the open-label extension Studies MG0004 and MG0007, respectively.
L539: ## 7.5. Adequacy of the Clinical Safety Database
L540: INDI32407 Information Request Advice dated February 10, 2022, stated:
L541: *"Typically, we would require a biologics license application (BLA) submission for a rare disease, such as generalized myasthenia gravis, to be consistent with the International Conference for Harmonization (ICH E1) requirements that 100 subjects be exposed to the drug for a minimum of one year, with at least half of the safety database consisting of patients dosed with the highest anticipated dose and frequency. There may be some flexibility with these requirements."*
L542: INDI32407 preBLA Meeting Preliminary Comments dated July 19, 2022, stated:
L543: *"Your proposed safety database appears capable of supporting a BLA submission for the treatment of gMG. The adequacy of the drug exposure in terms of the number of infusions in a treatment cycle, the duration of a treatment cycle, the number of treatment cycles per year, and the time to initiate a repeat treatment cycle from the start of the previous treatment cycle will be a matter of review. Your submission should include information on the duration of treatment cycles in Pool S1."*
L544: As per the preBLA request the Applicant provided the rozanolixizumab exposure data from subjects in Safety Pool S1 including the data from the 120-day safety update (eCTD seq 0012) which is reviewed below:
L545: ## The Number of Infusions in a Treatment Cycle, Safety Pool S1, Studies MG0003, MG0004, and MG0007
L546: In Safety Pool S1, 196 subjects received a total of 4,557 infusions (mean: 23.3; SD: 15.7; median: 19.5, range: 1, 65), representing a mean of 5.4 infusions per treatment cycle (source: Module 2.7.4; 120-Day SU Table 4.2.1 eCTD seq 0012). A total of 578 treatment cycles of 6 infusions were administered in 196 subjects and the median number of completed cycles was 2 with the range of 0 to 10 cycles. The total number of cycles including incomplete initiated cycles (at least 1 infusion but less than 6 infusions) was 846 with the median 4 and range 1 to 11. Of 846 cycles, 86 cycles in 86 subjects were fixed and 760 cycles were symptom-driven in 196 subjects.
L547: ## The Number and Duration of Treatment Cycle During the Entire Study Period, Safety Pool S1, Studies MG0003, MG0004, and MG0007
L548: As per the 120-day safety update, 196 (100%) subjects in the Safety Pool S1 received at least one infusion of rozanolixizumab (Table 40). The number of subjects with more than 12 months' time in the studies was 133, and of these subjects, 100 (75.2%) subjects had at least four treatment cycles with the median fourth treatment cycle duration of 77 days (11 weeks). There were 88 (66.1%) subjects with at least 5 treatment cycles (the maximum number of treatment cycles
L549: possible per year according to the protocol) completed during the entire study duration and the median fifth treatment cycle duration was 69.5 days or 9.9 weeks. The average cycle duration was 90.6 days or 12.9 weeks for the cohort of 21 subjects with exactly five treatment cycles.
L550: The median treatment-free interval defined as the time from last dose in the previous treatment cycle to first dose in the subsequent cycle was consistent between the rozanolixizumab dose groups in the Safety Population Pool S1. The median interval was \approx9 weeks between cycles 1 and 2, \approx7 to 8 weeks between cycles 2 and 3, and \approx5 weeks between cycles 3 and 4 and cycles 4 and 5 (MG0007 CSR Section 9.1.9 dated August 17, 2022).

L570: ## **The Number and Duration of Treatment Cycle During the First Year in the Study, Safety Population, Study MG0007**
L571: For both the 109 and 133 subjects with more than 12 months participation at the original BLA and 120-day safety update data cuts respectively, within the first year the median number of cycles initiated was four per year (range: 1 to 7) (Source: 120-day safety update dated February 3, 2023, eCTD Sequence number 0012).
L572: As per the response to the information request dated March 3, 2023, out of 49 subjects with more than 12 months' time in the open-label repeated cyclical treatment Study MG0007, eight subjects had initiated five treatment cycles and seven subjects had completed five treatment cycles in the first year. For both subcohorts, the median cycle duration in the first year was 77 days or 11 weeks (Table 42). The median treatment cycle duration was 67 days or 9.6 weeks in the same year in 14 subjects who had initiated the maximum possible six treatment cycles.
L573: The minimum time for a treatment cycle in Study MG0007 within the first year was 64 days or 9 weeks which is similar to that observed in the combined Safety pool S1 data.

L582: The reviewer notes that the safety data are limited on treatment cycles shorter than 63 days or 9 weeks, based on the minimum protocol-defined intertreatment cycle periods in the open-label extension Study MG0007. However, this should allow most patients' gMG to be treated when it is anticipated to be clinically indicated (i.e., approximately 5 weeks after completion of the previous treatment cycle). The reviewer recommends informing prescribers that the safety of initiating subsequent cycles sooner than 63 days or 9 weeks from the start of the previous treatment cycle has not been established for rozanolixizumab.

L586: ## 7.6.2. Safety Results, Study MG0003
L587: ## 7.6.2.1. Overview of Treatment-Emergent Adverse Events
## Summary, Study MG0003
L588: Table 43 provides a summary of treatment-emergent adverse events (TEAEs) in Study MG0003. The overall frequency of TEAEs was similar between the rozanolixizumab \approx7 mg/kg and \approx10 mg/kg groups. Relatively more subjects on rozanolixizumab than placebo reported TEAEs. The frequency of severe adverse events and TEAEs leading to drug discontinuation or dose modification was higher in the rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg group than rozanolixizumab \approx7 mg/kg and placebo groups. There were no deaths.

L597: ## 7.6.2.2. Deaths, Study MG0003
L598: There were no deaths in Study MG0003.
L599: ## 7.6.2.3. Serious Treatment-Emergent Adverse Events, Study MG0003
L600: The frequency of serious adverse events (SAEs) was balanced between the rozanolixizumab and placebo treatment groups. All SAEs are listed in Table 44 and Table 45.
L601: ## Worsening of Myasthenia Gravis
L602: Table 46 summarizes seven subjects who had worsening of MG and required the use of rescue therapy or missed treatment in Study MG0003. Two subjects on placebo developed MG crisis, whereas none of the subjects developed MG crisis while receiving rozanolixizumab treatment in Study MG0003. Five subjects had worsening of MG symptoms including one in the placebo group and two each in the rozanolixizumab \approx7 mg/kg and \approx10 mg/kg groups. Two subjects were withdrawn from the placebo group due to MG worsening and crisis. Three subjects in the rozanolixizumab treatment groups required IVIg due to MG worsening during observation period in Study MG0003; none had interruptions in study treatment. One subject in the rozanolixizumab \approx7 mg/kg treatment group had MG worsening due to severe vomiting and aspiration pneumonia and recovered without rescue therapy; this subject missed the second infusion and received a total of five doses in Study MG0003.
L603: ## Infections
L604: No serious infections were reported in the subjects receiving rozanolixizumab in Study MG0003. There was a single event of serious COVID-19 pneumonia in one subject MG0003/ [REDACTED] (b) (6) in the placebo arm and symptoms resolved in 8 days; the subject missed fourth and sixth placebo infusions during randomized treatment period in Study MG0003.
L605: ## Headache
L606: Subject ID MG0003/ [REDACTED] (b) (6) experienced a serious headache 10 days after the first and third day after the most recent rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg infusion. This subject was admitted overnight to a hospital due to logistical reasons. The subject woke up with moderately severe throbbing and pressurized headache. There was exacerbation with light, sound, and movement. The subject had nausea but no vomiting, fever, or neck stiffness. Headache and nausea resolved in 2 days with ibuprofen and metoclopramide. There were no study drug dose modification or interruption. The subject had no previous history of headaches or migraine. The SAE of severe headache was likely related to rozanolixizumab due to the suggestive time to onset and a history of positive rechallenge (not as severe headache as the first episode).
L607: ## Seizure
L608: Subject ID MG0003/ [REDACTED] (b) (6) was a 71-year-old subject randomized to rozanolixizumab \approx7 mg/kg treatment group developed two episodes of seizures during observation period in
L609: Study MG0003. Hospital notes indicated tonic seizures; no other clinical description provided in narrative. The CT head was normal. Etiology of the seizure is unclear. The event was considered resolved and the relation of causality to rozanolixizumab cannot be ruled out.
L610: ## **Acute Respiratory Failure**
L611: Subject ID MG0003/ [REDACTED](b)(6) was randomized to rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg treatment in Study MG0003. The subject was on concomitant medications for standard MG care including prednisone, mycophenolate, and pyridostigmine. The subject developed severe acute respiratory failure in the setting of tracheostomy device dislocation, tracheal stenosis, and lobar pneumonia (severe acute respiratory syndrome coronavirus 2 negative). This subject required tracheostomy for tracheal device dislocation, did not require ventilatory assistance, treated with antibiotics, the SAE occurred 43 days after the first and seventh day after the last infusion and resolved in 28 days from the onset. The subject discontinued the study due to the device dislocation. Note that this subject developed pyrexia 4 days after the first infusion and later again 29 days after the first infusion, which responded to paracetamol and resolved in 24 hours. The SAE of acute respiratory failure was unlikely related to rozanolixizumab because the subject experienced the SAE due to the tracheal device dislocation. The reviewer notes that the subject was diagnosed with pneumonia which responded to antibiotics during hospitalization and considered resolved; the event of pneumonia was not reported as SAE, and the relation of causality to rozanolixizumab for pneumonia cannot be ruled out.
L612: ## **Other SAEs in the Subjects on Rozanolixizumab Treatment**
L613: Other SAEs included gastritis, vomiting, chest pain, joint pain, nephrolithiasis, cervical dysplasia, metastatic squamous cell carcinoma, and tracheal device dislocation.

L640: ## 7.6.2.4. Adverse Events and FDA Medical Queries Leading to Treatment Discontinuation, Study MG0003
L641: In Study MG0003, the frequency of TEAEs leading to treatment discontinuation was higher in the rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg treatment group compared to rozanolixizumab \approx7 mg/kg and placebo groups (Table 47, Table 48). A total of five subjects (7.2%) in the rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg treatment group experienced TEAEs that led to treatment discontinuation including one subject who reported metastatic squamous cell carcinoma, oral herpes, and deep vein thrombosis; one subject experienced vomiting and upper abdominal pain; and one subject each reported diarrhea, tracheal device dislocation, and pruritus. In the rozanolixizumab \approx7 mg/kg dose group, one subject each experienced headache and arthralgia that led to treatment discontinuation. None of the subjects in the rozanolixizumab treatment groups discontinued treatment due to MG worsening/crisis, whereas two subjects on placebo withdrew from the Study MG0003.
L642: Brief case summaries of TEAEs resulting in rozanolixizumab treatment discontinuation are provided below (source: Module 5.3.5.1. mg0003-narratives):
L643: - Subject ID MG0003/ [REDACTED](b)(6) experienced nonserious AEs of upper abdominal pain and vomiting 4 days after the first rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg SC infusion that led to treatment discontinuation. The event resolved 19 days after symptom onset. The AEs were considered moderate intensity. The subject did not have medical comorbidities or other etiologies which could have caused the AEs. Additionally, absence of the prior history of similar symptoms and the suggestive time to onset indicate that these AEs were likely related to rozanolixizumab.
- Subject ID MG0003/ [REDACTED](b)(6) experienced severe headache (ongoing neck sprain) and vomiting 3 days after the first rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg SC infusion. There was no change in treatment. Headache resolved in 3 days. The subject reported mild pruritus 29 days after the first infusion and 8 days after fourth infusion that led to treatment discontinuation. The event resolved 15 days after onset. Given the suggestive time to onset and absence of other potential etiologies, the AEs of headache and pruritus were likely related to rozanolixizumab.
- Subject ID MG0003/ [REDACTED](b)(6) experienced grade 3 diarrhea 2 days after the first dose of rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg SC infusion that led to treatment discontinuation. Other symptoms included fevers (38.3°C) and cramping abdominal pain. The etiology for diarrhea was not determined. The subject was admitted to a hospital and treated with natrium and a combination of fenpiverinium with metamizole and pitofenone. The event was considered resolved. The suggestive time to onset, the absence of alternative etiologies, and no prior history of similar symptoms indicate that the adverse event (AE) of diarrhea was likely related to the rozanolixizumab.
- Subject ID MG0003/ [REDACTED](b)(6) experienced oral herpes, metastatic squamous cell carcinoma in the inguinal region and femoral vein thrombosis 26 days after the first and fourth day after the fourth rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg SC infusion that led to treatment discontinuation. Because the subject had episodes of squamous cell carcinoma before enrolling into Study MG0003, the AE was unlikely related to rozanolixizumab.
L644: - Subject ID MG0003/ (b)(6) experienced acute respiratory failure due to tracheal device dislocation in the setting of tracheostomy device dislocation, tracheal stenosis, and lobar pneumonia (SARS-COV-2 negative) 43 days after the first and seventh days after the last rozanolixizumab v10 mg/kg SC infusion that led to treatment discontinuation. Note that the subject missed fifth dose due to pyrexia and received a total of five infusions in Study MG0003. The subject had a tracheostomy for tracheal device dislocation, treated with antibiotics and did not require ventilatory assistance. The SAE resolved in 28 days from the onset. The device dislocation was unlikely related to rozanolixizumab.
- Subject ID MG0003/ (b)(6) experienced moderate joint pain 4 days after the first rozanolixizumab \approx7 mg/kg SC infusion. The subject was admitted to a hospital. The subject felt better on oral nonsteroidal anti-inflammatory medication and the joint pain resolved in 5 days. The subject had no previous history of joint pains. The subject experienced worsening joint pain and difficulty in walking 10 days after the first infusion and 2 days after the second infusion that led to treatment discontinuation. The subject was again admitted to a hospital and became a wheelchair bound. The event resolved 11 days after onset. The suggestive time to onset, the absence of alternative etiologies, and no prior history of similar symptoms indicate that the AE of joint pain was likely related to the rozanolixizumab.
- Subject ID MG0003/ (b)(6) experienced nonserious severe headache 4 days after the first rozanolixizumab \approx7 mg/kg SC infusion that led to treatment discontinuation. Other symptoms included pyrexia, photophobia, phonophobia, nausea, and vertigo. The event resolved 14 days after the onset. The subject had a past medical history of headaches and nausea before enrolling into Study MG0003. Given the suggestive time to onset the AE of severe headache was likely related to rozanolixizumab.

L660: ## 7.6.2.5. Treatment-Emergent Adverse Events, Study MG0003
L661: Table 49 lists the TEAEs in Study MG0003 with a cutoff frequency of at least 5% in the either rozanolixizumab arm and higher frequency than placebo consistent with the cutoffs used in labeling for efgartigimod, eculizumab, and ravulizumab, the monoclonal antibodies approved for the indication of anti-AChR antibody seropositive MG.
L662: The most frequently reported TEAEs in Study MG0003 in rozanolixizumab treated subjects were headache, diarrhea, pyrexia, and infections including upper respiratory tract infections and herpes simplex. Injection site reactions were seen in 9% of the subjects in rozanolixizumab treated group compared to 3% in the placebo group. Infections and headaches were a topic of special interest for this review and are discussed further in Section 7.7.1 and Section 7.7.2 respectively. Other frequent TEAEs for potential labeling included nausea, vomiting, musculoskeletal pain, and skin rash. Hypersensitivity reactions and administration site reactions, and gastrointestinal disorders are discussed in Section 7.7.3 and Section 7.7.4 respectively.
L663: The TEAE of hypertension was reported in six (4.5%) subjects treated with rozanolixizumab; however, four subjects had a pre-existing hypertension. The reviewer notes that hypertension was the most common medical comorbidity in the subjects upon entry to Study MG0003 (31.5%; Table 17). Hypertension was not likely related to the use of rozanolixizumab.

L681: ## 7.6.2.6. Laboratory Findings, Study MG0003
L682: ## Hematology
L683: The reviewer did not identify a safety signal for abnormal hematology laboratory findings in subjects treated with rozanolixizumab in Study MG0003. In Study MG0003, the most frequent postbaseline abnormalities were decreased leukocytes, decreased neutrophils, and decreased platelet counts in the subjects receiving rozanolixizumab (Table 50). The laboratory abnormalities of decreased platelets and decreased leukocytes counts had similar maximum lowest values between rozanolixizumab and placebo arms. The maximum lowest platelet count was 114 \times 10^9/L on day 8 and day 36 in the rozanolixizumab \approx 10 mg/kg group compared to placebo 121 \times 10^9/L on day 8 and day 43 during the treatment period (reference range 150 to 400 \times 10^9/L). The maximum lowest leukocytes count was 2.13 \times 10^9/L on day 36 in the rozanolixizumab \approx 10 mg/kg group compared to placebo 2.19 \times 10^9/L on day 43 during the treatment period (reference range 3.8 to 10.7 \times 10^9/L). One subject in the rozanolixizumab \approx 7 mg/kg group had the lowest leukocyte count 1.8 \times 10^9/L on day 99 the end of the observation period in Study MG0003.
L684: One subject experienced neutropenia (less than 1.0 \times 10^9/L) in the rozanolixizumab \approx 10 mg/kg group during the treatment period. The maximum lowest neutrophils count was 0.68 \times 10^9/L on day 36 in the rozanolixizumab \approx 10 mg/kg group compared to placebo 1.05 \times 10^9/L on day 43 during the treatment period (reference range 2.0 to 7.2 to 10.7 \times 10^9/L).
L685: The proportion of subjects with outlier values of decreased leukocytes, decreased neutrophils, and decreased platelets counts was balanced between rozanolixizumab and placebo groups (Table 51). More subjects in the rozanolixizumab \approx 10 mg/kg group had elevated leukocytes counts than rozanolixizumab \approx 7 mg/kg and placebo groups.
L686: ## Change From Baseline in Hematology Laboratory Parameters
L687: The subjects receiving rozanolixizumab had a decrease from baseline in the mean leukocytes, neutrophils and platelets counts compared to placebo during the treatment period. The mean decrease in leukocytes, decrease in neutrophils, and decrease in platelets were observed from day 8 (i.e., the first postbaseline assessment) and the levels returned to either baseline or comparable to placebo by day 99 the end of the observation period in Study MG0003. The incidence of normal to low shifts from baseline in leukocytes, neutrophils and platelets counts was similar between rozanolixizumab and placebo groups (Table 52).
L688: The mean changes from baseline in lymphocytes, eosinophils and hemoglobin levels, and prothrombin time were comparable to placebo and not clinically significant. The activated partial thromboplastin time levels were not assessed during the treatment period and the sample sizes were smaller during later visits in the observation period.

L705: ## Chemistry
L706: ## Decrease in Serum Albumin Levels
L707: The most frequent abnormal postbaseline chemistry laboratory findings were decreased serum albumin values in the subjects receiving rozanolixizumab. The maximum lowest albumin value (2.9 g/dL) was observed in one subject receiving rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg at day 99 which was the last day in the observation period. Please see Section 7.7.5 for the discussion of low albumin levels as a key safety review issue of special interest.
L708: ## Change From Baseline in Serum Albumin Levels
L709: The mean decrease from baseline at day 43 in serum albumin levels was -2.2 g/L in the rozanolixizumab \approx7 mg/kg group and -2.3 g/L in the rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg group compared with -0.45 g/L in the placebo group during the treatment period in Study MG0003. The decreases in albumin values in the rozanolixizumab treated subjects were observed as early as day 8 which was the first postbaseline visit in Study MG0003. The mean albumin values in the rozanolixizumab treated subjects remained persistently lower than placebo throughout the treatment period and resolved to the placebo levels on day 71 during the observation period in Study MG0003.
L710: ## Increase in Fasting Blood Glucose Level
L711: More subjects in the rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg group had increased postbaseline fasting glucose values (\ge100 to 125) compared to the rozanolixizumab \approx7 mg/kg and placebo groups (32.7% vs. 22.9% and 23.5%, respectively). One subject with increased fasting glucose levels 5.97 mmol/L at baseline in the rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg group reported two TEAEs related to abnormal fasting glucose values during the treatment period with the maximum highest glucose value 6.13 mmol/L on day 36 and 6.16 mmol/L on day 43 (normal reference 4.1 to 5.5 mmol/L; source: clinical study report (CSR) MG0003 page 216; by subject listing 8.1.2). One subject with increased fasting glucose levels 5.97 mmol/L at baseline in the rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg group reported two TEAEs related to abnormal fasting glucose values during the treatment period with the maximum highest glucose value 6.13 mmol/L on day 36 and 6.16 mmol/L on day 43 (normal reference 4.1 to 5.5 mmol/L; source: clinical study report (CSR) MG0003 page 216; by subject listing 8.1.2).
L712: ## Change From Baseline in Fasting Blood Glucose Level
L713: There were minimal and clinically nonsignificant changes from baseline to day 43 in the mean glucose levels -0.21 mmol/L in the rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg group during the treatment period compared to 0.20 mmol/L in the rozanolixizumab \approx7 mg/kg group and placebo groups.
L714: ## Serum Electrolytes
L715: There were no reported TEAEs related to serum electrolyte laboratory values. One subject in the rozanolixizumab 10 mg/kg group experienced the maximum lowest sodium levels 117 mEq/L on day 99 the last day in the observation period. None of the subjects had abnormally high sodium levels (&gt;150 mEq/L). The maximum lowest and highest potassium values were 3.3 mEq/L and 5.6 mEq/L on day 43 in the rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg group. The proportions of subjects with
L716: abnormal low potassium levels (&lt;3.6 \text{ mEq/L}) were similar between rozanolixizumab and placebo groups. Relatively more subjects in the rozanolixizumab \approx 7 \text{ mg/kg} group had abnormal low corrected calcium levels (&lt;8.4 and &gt;8) compared with the rozanolixizumab \approx 10 \text{ mg/kg} and placebo groups (28.1% vs. 20.3% and 9%, respectively). The maximum lowest calcium levels were 1.95 \text{ mmol/L} (7.8 \text{ mg/dL}; normal reference 8.5 to 10.2 \text{ mg/dL}) on day 22 during the treatment period in the rozanolixizumab \approx 7 \text{ mg/kg} group.
L717: ## Change From Baseline in Serum Electrolytes
L718: The mean changes from baseline to day 43 in serum sodium, potassium, and corrected calcium levels were minimal and clinically nonsignificant in the subjects receiving rozanolixizumab in Study MG0003. The mean changes from baseline to day 43 in serum sodium, potassium, and corrected calcium levels were minimal and clinically nonsignificant in the subjects receiving rozanolixizumab in Study MG0003.
L719: ## Blood Urea Nitrogen
L720: The proportion of subjects with elevated blood urea nitrogen (BUN) values was higher in the rozanolixizumab treated groups compared with placebo in Study MG0003 (21.1% vs. 17.9%). The maximum highest BUN values were 14.8 \text{ mmol/L} on day 8 and 14.2 \text{ mmol/L} on day 99 which was the last observation day in the rozanolixizumab \approx 7 \text{ mg/kg} and \approx 10 \text{ mg/kg} groups, respectively compared with placebo 10.5 \text{ mmol/L} on day 71 during observation period in Study MG0003 (normal reference generally 2.1 to 8.5 \text{ mmol/L}). Note that the interpretation of BUN values is dependent on the age and sex. There were no reported TEAEs related to BUN levels. None of the rozanolixizumab treated subjects in Study MG0003 had abnormal high creatinine values (see kidney function Table 54).
L721: ## Change From Baseline in BUN Levels
L722: The mean changes from baseline to day 43 in BUN values were minimal and clinically nonsignificant during the treatment period in rozanolixizumab subjects compared with placebo in Study MG0003.
L723: ## Lipid Panel
L724: The reviewer did not identify a safety signal for abnormal LDL or other lipids in subjects treated with rozanolixizumab in Study MG0003. This finding agrees with the absence of a safety signal for elevated LDL or other lipids in subjects treated with efgartigimod, a previously approved FcRn blocker drug for the treatment of anti-AChR-gMG. Note that cholesterol levels were reviewed as an adverse event special interest for efgartigimod because elevated LDL cholesterol levels led to premature termination of a phase 2 study (NCT03938545) after 12 weeks of treatment with RVT-1401, a human anti-FcRn monoclonal antibody for the treatment of subjects with Graves' ophthalmopathy (Men et al. 2021).
L725: The nonfasted monitoring of lipids was introduced in protocol amendment 4 of Study MG0003 (Feb 23, 2021) and in protocol amendment 1 of MG0007 (Mar 03, 2021). Lipids analyte data were available in 53% of the subjects in the rozanolixizumab \approx 7 \text{ mg/kg} and rozanolixizumab \approx 10 \text{ mg/kg} groups, and 58% of the subjects in the placebo group. The Applicant states that the blood samples were collected during nonfasting conditions.
L726: The proportion of subjects with outlier LDL cholesterol and triglycerides values was similar among treatment groups (Table 53). More female subjects receiving rozanolixizumab had lower high-density lipoprotein (HDL) cholesterol levels compared to placebo, but the sample size was small. This difference was not apparent in smaller sample size of male subjects.
L727: The maximum highest LDL cholesterol levels on day 43 were similar among treatment groups: 4.47 mmol/L in the rozanolixizumab \approx7 mg/kg group, 5.96 mmol/L in the rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg group, and 5.83 mmol/L in the placebo group (reference &lt;2.6 mmol/L). The maximum lowest HDL cholesterol levels on day 43 were similar among treatment groups: 0.90 mmol/L in the rozanolixizumab \approx7 mg/kg group, 0.79 mmol/L in the rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg group, and 0.86 mmol/L in the placebo group (reference &gt;1.55 mmol/L).
L728: ## Change From Baseline
L729: The changes from baseline in LDL cholesterol, HDL cholesterol, and triglyceride levels in rozanolixizumab treated subjects were minimal and not clinically significant compared to placebo (Figure 7). The mean change from baseline in LDL cholesterol at day 43 was 0.04 mmol/L in the rozanolixizumab \approx7 mg/kg group, -0.19 mmol/L in the rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg group and -0.1 mmol/L in the placebo group (Figure 7). The mean change from baseline in triglycerides at day 43 was 0.08 mmol/L in the rozanolixizumab \approx7 mg/kg group, 0.16 mmol/L in the rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg group and -0.03 mmol/L in the placebo group. The mean change from baseline in HDL cholesterol levels on day 43 was -0.05 mmol/L, -0.04 mmol/L and -0.01 mmol/L in the rozanolixizumab \approx7 mg/kg, rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg, and placebo groups respectively (Figure 7).

L741: ## **Kidney Function**
L742: Subjects with renal impairment defined as glomerular filtration rate (GFR) less than 45 mL/min/1.73m2 were not enrolled in Study MG0003. None of the subjects receiving rozanolixizumab had abnormal high creatinine levels during Study MG0003 (Table 54). One subject each in the rozanolixizumab 7 mg/kg and 10 mg/kg groups had decreased GFR (≥25%) in postbaseline values.
L743: No subjects had severe or serious TEAEs related to renal impairment. Three subjects (4.3%) with already low eGFR and elevated serum creatinine at baseline in the rozanolixizumab ≈10 mg/kg group had TEAEs of renal impairment (source: CSR Section 11.8.9 and case narratives in response to IR, eCTD Seq number 0013):
L744: - Subject ID MG0003/(b) (6): 76-year-old male subject randomized to rozanolixizumab ≈10 mg/kg dose with a medical history of hypertension, diabetes mellitus type 2, and overweight experienced a decrease in estimated glomerular filtration rate (eGFR) from 53.2 mL/min/1.73m2 at baseline to 41.7 mL/min/1.73m2 on day 71 (36 days after the final sixth infusion) during observation period. Serum creatinine 114.6 mmol/L was elevated at baseline and most of the time throughout the study (normal range, 64 to 104 mmol/L). Risk factors for kidney disease in the medical history of the subject included diabetes mellitus type 2 and hypertension, and concomitant medications such as
L745: candesartan/hydrochlorothiazide and metformin. The event was considered nonserious moderate intensity TEAE which was not resolved during the study. Because the subject had impaired renal function upon study entry and the presence of alternative potential etiologies such as other medical comorbidities and medications, the renal impairment was unlikely related to rozanolixizumab.
L746: - **Subject ID MG0003/**(b)(6) 81-year-old male subject experienced a decrease in estimated glomerular filtration rate (eGFR) from 60.0 mL/min/1.73m2 at baseline to 40.2 mL/min/1.73m2 day 43 a week after the sixth and final dose of rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg SC infusion. Serum creatinine 100.7 mmol/L and BUN 9.8 mmol/L were mildly elevated at baseline and most of the time throughout the study (normal range, creatinine 64 to 104 mmol/L and BUN 3 to 9.2 mmol/L). Risk factors for kidney disease in the medical history of the subject included hypertension and concomitant medications such as ramipril and furosemide. The subject also had an extensive medical and procedural history related to cardiovascular disease. He completed the observation period and rolled over to OLE Study MG0007. The event was considered nonserious mild intensity TEAE unrelated to rozanolixizumab which was not resolved during the study. Because the subject had impaired renal function upon study entry and the presence of alternative potential etiologies such as other medical comorbidities and medications, the renal impairment was unlikely related to rozanolixizumab.

- **Subject ID MG0003/**(b)(6) 58-year-old female subject randomized to rozanolixizumab 10 mg/kg dose experienced a decrease in estimated glomerular filtration rate (eGFR) from 54.5 mL/min/1.73m2 at baseline to 41.4 mL/min/1.73m2 on day 8 one day after the second rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg SC infusion during treatment period. The eGFR was 81.6 mL/min/1.73m2 on day 22. Serum creatinine 98.4 mmol/L and BUN 11.7 mmol/L (normal range, creatinine 49 to 90 mmol/L and BUN 3.5 to 7.2 mmol/L) were elevated at baseline and most of the time throughout the study but returned to normal by day 43. Risk factors for kidney disease in the medical history of the subject included hypertension and concomitant medications such as tacrolimus and rosuvastatin. The subject continued to receive rozanolixizumab 10 mg/kg SC infusion weekly and received a total of five infusions in Study MG0003. She completed the observation period and enrolled into OLE Study MG0007. The event was considered nonserious moderate intensity TEAE which was not resolved during the Study. Because the subject had impaired renal function upon study entry and the presence of alternative potential etiologies such as other medical comorbidities and medications, the renal impairment was unlikely related to rozanolixizumab.

L762: ## 7.6.2.7. Assessment of Drug-Induced Liver Injury, Study MG0003
L763: There were no serious drug-related hepatic disorders, liver function test (LFT) elevations meeting protocol-defined withdrawal criteria, or potential Hy's law cases (Figure 10) in Study MG0003.
L764: Table 55 shows the frequency of maximum postbaseline values meeting cut points above the upper limit of normal (ULN) for liver enzymes and bilirubin in the subjects treated with rozanolixizumab compared with placebo.
L765: Three (2.2%) subjects receiving rozanolixizumab had abnormal LFT elevations during the treatment period in Study MG0003. All of them had their respective LFT values above ULN at the baseline visit. In the rozanolixizumab \approx7 mg/kg group, one subject with a medical history of increased liver enzymes experienced an elevated aspartate aminotransferase (AST) and alkaline phosphatase (ALP) and a nonserious TEAEs of mild hepatic fibrosis and nonalcoholic fatty liver disease. Two subjects with increased bilirubin levels at baseline in the rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg group experienced elevated bilirubin levels and resolved. Because these subjects had impaired liver function upon study entry, the AEs were not likely related to rozanolixizumab.
L766: Two additional subjects had LFT elevations during the last week of the observation period (8 weeks after the last dose) in Study MG0003 including one subject in the rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg group who had elevated AST &gt;10xULN and alanine aminotransferase &gt;10xULN, and one subject in the rozanolixizumab \approx7 mg/kg group who had an elevated total bilirubin with a
L767: medical history of Gilbert's syndrome. Given that these events occurred outside of the anticipated five drug half-lives period, the AEs were unlikely related to rozanolixizumab.
L768: The postbaseline assessments of prothrombin time (PT) in the subjects receiving rozanolixizumab were limited by small sample size during the treatment period (n=6 and n=1 in the rozanolixizumab \approx7 mg/kg and \approx10 mg/kg groups, respectively versus n=7 in the placebo group). All abnormal PT values (&gt;1.1 \times \text{ULN}) were observed during the observation period; all but one subject had abnormally elevated PT values at baseline and therefore unlikely related to rozanolixizumab.

L780: ## 7.6.2.8. Vital-Sign Analyses, Study MG0003
L781: The reviewer evaluated mean change from baseline, outlier shift analyses from normal-to-high and normal-to-low, and TEAEs belonging to the SOC Investigation related to vital signs.
L782: **Table 56** shows shifts in vital signs data from baseline visit during the double-blind placebo-controlled treatment period in Study MG0003. Normal to high shifts from baseline with frequency of at least 5% higher in the rozanolixizumab arm compared to placebo occurred in respiratory rate (11.3% versus 4.8%) and in diastolic blood pressure (rozanolixizumab ≈10 mg/kg dose arm only; 26.1% versus 20.9%). There were no shifts greater than 5% in systolic blood pressure, pulse rate, temperature, and bodyweight in the rozanolixizumab arm compared to placebo.
L783: **Table 57** shows proportion of subjects with vital signs data meeting specific criteria specified in Appendix B of the preBLA meeting minutes dated July 19, 2022. The proportion of subjects with respiratory rate &gt;20 breaths per minute and those with diastolic blood pressure &gt;90 mm Hg was higher in the rozanolixizumab group compared to placebo (18% versus 11.9% and 30.8% versus 25.8%, respectively). No subjects receiving rozanolixizumab experienced increase in Fridericia corrected QT interval duration.

L805: ## 7.6.2.10. Exposure-Adjusted Analyses, Study MG0003

L807: ## 7.7. Key Safety Review Issues
L808: ## 7.7.1. Infections
L809: ## Issue
L810: TEAEs related to infections were among the most frequently reported adverse events in Study MG0003.
L811: ## Background
L812: The topic of infections was of special interest. The Applicant's proposed label notes that infections were among the most frequently reported AEs in clinical studies and includes infections in Warnings and Precautions.
L813: Given the mechanism of drug action, treatment with rozanolixizumab results in a reduction of circulating immunoglobulin G (IgG) and reduced IgG levels are associated with an increased risk of infections (Furst 2009). Bacterial infections of the respiratory tract are the most common types of infection seen in individuals with reduced IgG levels. Based on the available literature review, it is unclear at what IgG level the risk for infections becomes apparent. The overall risk may also depend on the age, concomitant medication, and underlying disease in individual patients. Specifically, MG patients have a high susceptibility for respiratory tract infections and sepsis due to bulbar and respiratory muscle weakness, and concomitant immunomodulating therapies administered as standard MG care (Prior et al. 2018; Sipila et al. 2019; Kassardjian et al. 2020).
L814: ## Assessment
L815: Subjects with serum total IgG levels \le 5.5 g/L were not enrolled in Study MG0003. Treatment with rozanolixizumab resulted in the reduction in total serum IgG levels compared to placebo. As early as the first follow-up visit on day 8, one week after the first infusion, median total IgG levels were decreased by 44.8% and 52.8% in rozanolixizumab \approx 7 mg/kg and \approx 10 mg/kg arms. The median maximum decrease from baseline was -73.2% (range, -95.5% to 5.7%) in rozanolixizumab \approx 7 mg/kg treatment group and -78.9% (range, -92.2% to -46.5%) in rozanolixizumab \approx 10 mg/kg treatment group compared to -9.0% (range, -49% to 4.8%) in the placebo group (source: CSR MG0003, Table 9-2). The lowest serum IgG levels were 0.3 g/L in rozanolixizumab \approx 7 mg/kg treatment group and 0.46 g/L in rozanolixizumab \approx 10 mg/kg treatment group observed at day 29 compared to placebo 6.17 g/L on the same day. By day 99, the median IgG levels had increased to 13% and 9% of the baseline value in rozanolixizumab \approx 7 mg/kg and \approx 10 mg/kg treatment groups, respectively.
L816: Table 59 shows in Study MG0003, the TEAEs of infections occurred at higher frequency in rozanolixizumab \approx 10 mg/kg arm (n=21; 30.4%) compared to the rozanolixizumab \approx 7 mg/kg arm (n=10; 15.6%) and placebo arm (n=13; 19.4%). The most common infections were upper
L817: respiratory tract infections and herpes simplex. One subject in rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg arm developed an opportunistic infection caused by herpes zoster virus. None of the subjects treated with rozanolixizumab had serious infections. There were no deaths in Study MG0003. In one subject rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg treatment was discontinued due to TEAEs of metastatic squamous cell carcinoma and deep venous thrombosis and the subject had oral herpes. About two thirds of the events were considered mild intensity and no subject had severe infection while on rozanolixizumab treatment.
