L821: Treatment-emergent adverse events defined as any AE starting on or after the time of first rozanolixizumab administration up to and including 8 weeks (56 days) after the final dose. Adverse events starting before the date of the first administration of rozanolixizumab or after 8 weeks following the final dose of rozanolixizumab were not considered TEAEs.
L822: Duration is 6 weeks of treatment followed by 8 weeks observation period to total 14 weeks.
L823: Abbreviations: N, number of subjects in treatment arm; n, number of subjects with adverse event; SOC, system organ class; TEAE, treatment-emergent adverse event
L824: In Safety Pool S1 pooled from Studies MG0003, MG0004, and MG0007, out of 196 subjects treated with rozanolixizumab, 94 (48%) subjects reported TEAEs of infections (see Section 17.6 Table 102 and Table 103). The frequency of infections was relatively higher in rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg dose arm (55.4%) compared to \approx7 mg/kg dose arm (40%), which is similar to that observed in the double-blind treatment cycle in Study MG0003. The most common infections were upper respiratory tract infections (17.3%), COVID-19 (14.3%), urinary tract infections (8.7%), and herpes simplex (5.6%). There were no opportunistic infections. Serious infections occurred in 8 (4.1%) subjects including COVID-19 (n=4), multilobar pneumonia due to unidentified pathogen (n=1), E. coli urosepsis (n=1), appendicitis complicated with liver abscess, peritonitis, and cholecystitis (n=1), and aseptic meningitis (n=1). The SAE of aseptic meningitis led to treatment discontinuation in one subject (see case narrative below). TEAEs of infections that led to treatment discontinuation in 6 (3.1%) subjects included oral herpes, complicated
L825: ## BLA 761286
## Rystiggo (rozanolixizumab)
L826: appendicitis, COVID-19, COVID-19 pneumonia, pneumonia, and urosepsis in one subject each (see Section 17.5 Table 101). In the open-label extension Study MG0007, one subject died in the setting of multilobar pneumonia in which no pathogen was identified and a role of rozanolixizumab cannot be ruled out. Two deaths were attributed to COVID-19 pneumonia in unvaccinated individuals. The TEAE of positive Interferon gamma release (IGRA) test led to treatment discontinuation in two subjects in Study MG0007. Case narratives for these subjects do not provide a clinical follow up regarding whether they were diagnosed with latent or active tuberculosis disease. Both subjects were IGRA positive upon entry to Study MG0007 (see Section 17.5). The Applicant has reported that none of the subjects were diagnosed with active tuberculosis or hepatitis in the phase 3 clinical studies of rozanolixizumab.
L827: In 120-day safety update (cutoff date October 7, 2022), two subjects discontinued rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg dose treatment due to TEAEs of appendicitis (SAE, n=1) and endocarditis (n=1). The description of endocarditis leading to death was included in the original BLA submission. No new cases of aseptic meningitis were identified. Compared to the original BLA submission, there were no clinically significant changes in the relative incidence of common TEAEs (\ge5% frequency) in the safety update. There were two new TEAEs of decreased blood immunoglobulin which is expected given the mechanism of drug action; one event led to temporary drug discontinuation.
L828: ## Aseptic Meningitis
L829: At the time of this review, there have been three SAEs of aseptic meningitis reported as drug-induced aseptic meningitis across the rozanolixizumab development program including, one event in one subject receiving rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg dose treatment during the ongoing open-label MG0007 study and one event each in two subjects randomized to blinded rozanolixizumab or placebo treatment in the study evaluating rozanolixizumab for another indication. In the BLA submission, the Applicant provided case narratives of all three subjects (see Module 2.7.4. Section 2.1.7.2.2. Page 97 dated September 29, 2022, III.17).
L830: For the rozanolixizumab gMG program, the SAE of aseptic meningitis occurred in one subject out of total 196 subjects (0.005%) in the Safety Pool S1.
L831: In response to an IR dated [REDACTED] (b) (4), the Applicant indicated that these three cases are the only known cases of aseptic meningitis in their rozanolixizumab development program (n=555; 0.005%). All three subjects experienced headache, nuchal rigidity, and fever within 2 days after the first infusion of rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg dose (n=1) in the MG0007 study or blinded treatment (n=2) in the study for other indication. Cerebrospinal fluid (CSF) was notable for pleocytosis and normal protein in the gMG subject and pleocytosis with elevated protein in the subjects with the other indication. Symptoms improved after two days. All subjects recovered without sequelae as outlined in the brief case summaries below as provided by the Applicant:
L832: ## BLA761286:
L833: - Subject MG0007-[REDACTED] (b) (6): 51-year-old female with gMG who experienced severe headache, nuchal rigidity, fever, diarrhea, nausea, and vomiting 1.5 days after the first infusion of rozanolixizumab 10 mg/kg infusion in Study MG0007. Concomitant medications included prednisolone, pyridostigmine, and azathioprine. The subject was hospitalized, and
L834: CSF was notable for a monocytic pleocytosis of 509 cells/\muL and normal protein. CSF infectious studies were negative, including viral polymerase chain reactions (PCRs), fungal cultures, and bacterial cultures (see 17.4). Magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) demonstrated frontal periventricular meningeal enhancement. The subject was treated with ceftriaxone, acyclovir, and dexamethasone, which were stopped after her headache improved (after two days). The subject recovered in 5 days and was discharged. The subject underwent repeat lumbar puncture at 27 days post-event onset, and the pleocytosis resolved (see also 17.4). The study drug was withdrawn due to aseptic meningitis and the subject discontinued from the study. The SAE was considered severe and related to rozanolixizumab by the reviewer.
L835: IND (b)(4), rozanolixizumab for the treatment of myelin oligodendrocyte glycoprotein antibody disease (MOG-AD)
L836: - Subject MOG0001- (b)(6): 27-year-old male who experienced moderate headache, fever, and neck stiffness 2 days after the first infusion of study treatment rozanolixizumab 560 mg or placebo (blinded) in the study. Concomitant medications included prednisolone and trimethoprim/sulfamethoxazole (for 9 months). CSF was notable for lymphocytic pleocytosis (170/\muL), elevated protein (75 mg/dL), and normal glucose. CSF cultures were negative, and viral PCRs were not performed. MRI showed findings consistent with the underlying disease and an incidental arachnoid cyst. The headache improved 2 days after onset, and he was treated with loxoprofen. The subject was considered recovered after 54 days. The SAE was considered related to rozanolixizumab by the reviewer. Hospitalization was recommended however, subject requested not to be hospitalized and was withdrawn from the study.
- Subject MOG0001 (b)(6): 33-year-old male who experienced mild headache and fever 2 days after the first infusion of study treatment rozanolixizumab 560 mg or placebo (blinded) in the study. Concomitant medications included tacrolimus (discontinued one month prior to study enrollment), prednisolone, trimethoprim/sulfamethoxazole (since 2018), and fexofenadine (since 2020). CSF was notable for pleocytosis (equally neutrophilic and monocytic), elevated protein (89 mg/dL), slightly elevated glucose (78 mg/dL), and negative bacterial cultures. Viral PCR testing was not performed. MRI showed findings consistent with the underlying disease. The subject was treated with dexamethasone, meropenem, vancomycin, acyclovir, and paracetamol. The headache and fever improved after 2 days, and the event was considered resolved at 6 days. The SAE was considered related to rozanolixizumab by the reviewer. The subject was hospitalized and was withdrawn from the study.
L837: ## BLA 761286
## Rystiggo (rozanolixizumab)
L838: In Study MG0003, out of 133 subjects treated with rozanolixizumab, 58 (43.6%) subjects who experienced headaches had associated symptoms of fevers (29.3%), nausea (20.7%), vomiting (3.4%), and neck pain (3.4%). In the Safety Pool S1 from the Studies MG0003, MG0004, and MG0007 out of 196 subjects treated with rozanolixizumab, 98 (50%) subjects experienced headaches and had associated symptoms of fever (30.6%), nausea (28.6%), vomiting (10.2%) and neck pain (6.1%). None of these subjects underwent a lumbar puncture during the study. (source: adae.xpt; Software: R).
L839: The Applicant indicated in their information request (IR) response dated [REDACTED] (b) (4) that none of the 25 subjects who developed a serious or severe headache across the rozanolixizumab development program underwent lumbar puncture. Of these 25 subjects, three subjects were healthy volunteers and received a single dose. Of the other 22 subjects who developed a serious or severe headache in the setting of repeated treatment, 18 continued the treatment and rechallenge was positive in two subjects, one of whom discontinued the treatment. These subjects had associated symptoms of fever (40%), neck pain (32%), nausea and vomiting (52%). In the updated investigator's brochure dated September 1, 2022, the Applicant concluded that the available information supported a potential causal relationship between rozanolixizumab and the TEAE of aseptic meningitis.
L840: Table 60 shows that the frequency of infections in the subjects with abnormal laboratory values of serum IgG levels and blood cell counts was not higher than that reported in the overall population treated with rozanolixizumab in Study MG0003. However, in pooled phase 3 Studies MG0003, MG0004, and MG0007 (Safety Pool S1), compared to all subjects, a higher frequency of infections was reported in subjects with low lymphocyte counts (57% versus 48%) (Table 61). The reviewer cannot conclude a role of low neutrophil and leukocyte counts in the TEAEs of infections in the subjects treated with rozanolixizumab because the number of subjects is relatively small (fewer than 20) in the phase 3 studies.

L865: ## **Conclusion**
L866: The reviewer notes that rozanolixizumab may increase the risk of infection. In Study MG0003, TEAEs of infections were among the most frequently reported adverse events (&gt;10%) in subjects treated with rozanolixizumab. The most common infections were upper respiratory tract infection and herpes simplex. Similar TEAEs of infections were observed in the open-label extension Studies MG0004 and MG0007. The frequency of infections appeared higher in the rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg group compared to rozanolixizumab \approx7 mg/kg group. In these open-label studies, there were reports of SAEs (4.1%), TEAEs leading to treatment discontinuation (3.6%) and fatal TEAEs (1.5%; n=3) belonging to the SOC of Infections and Infestations.
L867: An SAE of aseptic meningitis has been reported in one subject with gMG and two subjects with MOG-AD which led to hospitalization and discontinuation of rozanolixizumab in clinical studies. Moreover, in clinical studies, subjects treated with rozanolixizumab experienced headaches associated with fever, nausea, vomiting, and neck pain. These findings suggest that rozanolixizumab has the potential to cause aseptic meningitis.
L868: Given the mechanism of action, treatment with rozanolixizumab led to reduction in the serum IgG levels. The sample size is not adequately powered to conclusively determine whether these reduced IgG levels contributed to infections in patients treated with rozanolixizumab. In pooled phase 3 studies, the frequency of infections was higher in the subjects with low lymphocyte counts. The reviewer could not determine a causative link between abnormal neutrophil and leukocyte counts and the risk of infection in subjects treated with rozanolixizumab due to small sample size (less than 20 subjects).
L869: It is unknown based on phase 3 clinical studies whether treatment with rozanolixizumab increases the risk of reactivation of hepatitis or tuberculosis in gMG patients. The Applicant reported that there were no cases of active tuberculosis in their phase 3 studies of rozanolixizumab in patients with gMG. The studies did not enroll hepatitis carriers. There were two cases of positive IGRA test on entry to an open-label study. This test does not differentiate latent tuberculosis from active tuberculosis disease. The reviewer recommends increased pharmacovigilance for reactivation of hepatitis or TB in subjects treated with rozanolixizumab.
L870: The reviewer recommends a Warning and Precaution Statement for infections in labeling for rozanolixizumab based on the class of drug, pharmacology, known mechanism of action by inhibition of FcRn receptor, and the observed frequency and seriousness of TEAEs belonging to system organ class (SOC) Infections and Infestations in the phase 3 clinical studies. The reviewer notes that infections are included in the Warning and Precautions section of the label for efgartigimod, a previously approved FcRn inhibitor drug for the treatment of patients with gMG who are anti-AChR antibody positive.
L871: Because there were three cases of SAEs of aseptic meningitis which led to hospitalization and discontinuation of rozanolixizumab in clinical studies, the reviewer recommends a Warning and Precaution Statement for aseptic meningitis in labeling for rozanolixizumab.
L872: There is also a concern regarding potential for more serious outcomes in the postmarketing period where subjects are monitored less frequently than in a clinical study setting, therefore a Warnings and Precautions statement may mitigate potentially serious outcomes of infections by alerting providers to the risk.
L873: ## 7.7.2. Headaches
L874: ## Issue
L875: TEAEs related to headaches were the most frequently reported adverse events in Study MG0003.
L876: ## Background
L877: The topic of headaches was of special interest as the Applicant's proposed label notes that headaches were the most frequently reported aEs in clinical studies. The underlying mechanism for the most common TEAE of headache is unknown.
L878: ## Assessment
L879: In Study MG0003, a total of 58 (43.6%) subjects out of 133 subjects treated with rozanolixizumab experienced headaches compared to 13 (19.4%) subjects out of 67 subjects in the placebo arm (Table 62). Most headaches were reported after the first infusion and did not increase with repeat treatments. In a majority of the subjects, the headaches were mild to moderate intensity and resolved without sequelae. A total of 7 (5.3%) subjects treated with rozanolixizumab experienced severe headache (see case narratives below). Of these seven subjects, six subjects were treated with the rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg dose and one subject was treated with the rozanolixizumab \approx7 mg/kg dose. One subject (MG0003/ [REDACTED](b) (6)) in rozanolixizumab \approx7 mg/kg arm discontinued treatment due to severe headache associated with fevers, photophobia, phonophobia, nausea, and vertigo (see Section 7.6.2.4). One subject (MG0003/ [REDACTED](b) (6)) in rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg arm was hospitalized due to severe headache (see Section 7.6.2.3). Severe or serious TEAEs of headaches were not observed in the placebo arm.

L888: ## **Case Narratives for TEAEs of Severe Headache, Study MG0003 (Source: Module 5.3.5.1. MG0003 Narratives)**
L889: - **Subject MG0003/**(b) (6): 53-year-old female experienced a nonserious TEAE of pulsatile holocephalic severe headache 4 days after the first SC infusion of rozanolixizumab \approx7 mg/kg dose. The headache progressed from moderate to severe intensity over 2 days. The subjects reported fevers, nausea, photophobia, phonophobia, and vertigo. The subject was treated with ibuprofen and paracetamol. Rozanolixizumab treatment was withdrawn due to headache. The event was resolved 4 days after onset. The subject had a history of severe headache with nausea in the preceding five years before enrolling into the study. Given the timing of onset, it is likely drug-related. An infectious etiology cannot be excluded.
- **Subject MG0003/**(b) (6): 26-year-old female experienced a serious TEAE of severe headache, 10 days after the first and third day after the second SC infusion of rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg dose. The subject woke up with a moderately severe throbbing and pressurized headache which gradually worsened during the day making her bedridden. The subject had exacerbation of headache with light, sound, and movement. The subject reported nausea but no vomiting, neck stiffness, or fever. Treatment with paracetamol had no effect on headache. The subject was admitted to an emergency room for less than 12 hours. The subject had a good response with ibuprofen and metaclopramide. The event was considered resolved 2 days after onset. The dosing regimen for rozanolixizumab was unchanged and the subject completed all six planned doses in the double-blind treatment cycle and later enrolled into the OLE Study MG0007. The subject again experienced a milder headache after second dose but that was attributed by the subject to the use of contact lenses. This AE is likely related to rozanolixizumab given the timing of the onset and a positive rechallenge.
- **Subject MG0003/**(b) (6): 42-year-old female experienced a nonserious TEAE of non-throbbing bilateral severe headache 3 days after the first SC infusion of rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg dose. The headache reached maximum intensity 10 on visual analog scale with vomiting and responded minimally to paracetamol during the first 24 hours from symptom onset. The event was considered resolved 3 days after onset. The dosing regimen for rozanolixizumab was unchanged due to headache. Later, she discontinued rozanolixizumab due to TEAE of pruritis 29 days after the first and eighth day after the fourth infusion. There were no reports of positive rechallenge with the subsequent three doses. The TEAE of headache was considered severe and related to rozanolixizumab given the suggestive time of onset.
- **Subject MG0003/**(b) (6): 30-year-old male experienced nonserious TEAEs of severe headache and fevers (38.5^{\circ}\text{C}) 3 days after the first SC infusion of rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg dose. The following day the intensity of headache decreased, and fevers resolved. The event was considered resolved 1 day after onset. The dosing regimen for rozanolixizumab was unchanged and the subject completed all 6 planned doses in the double-blind treatment cycle and later enrolled into the OLE Study MG0007. There were no reports of positive rechallenge with the subsequent doses. A lumbar puncture was not done. SARS-CoV-2 test was negative normal. Other viral origins for headache and fever cannot be
L890: excluded. Given the time of onset, the aEs of headaches and fevers were likely related to rozanolixizumab.
L891: - **Subject MG0003/**(b) (6): 58-year-old female experienced a nonserious TEAE of severe migraine-like headache, 18 days after the first and third day after the third SC infusion of rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg dose. Past medical history was notable for migraine headaches. The headache was unilateral, throbbing/pulsating in nature, and worsened with activity. During the course of the headache, the subject also experienced nausea, infrequent vomiting, increased neck pain or stiffness, increased sensitivity to light and noise, increased sensitivity to smell, weakness on one side; and seeing shimmering lights, lines, dark spots, other shapes or colors before the eyes. Before the headache became severe, one-sided numbness of the lips, tongue, fingers, and legs that migrated was noted, which lasted less than an hour. No dizziness was noted. The subject received treatment with acetylsalicylic acid with caffeine and paracetamol. The event was considered resolved 2 days after onset. The dosing regimen for rozanolixizumab was unchanged due to the TEAE of headache. Later, the subject discontinued rozanolixizumab after the fourth infusion due to a serious TEAE of metastatic squamous cell carcinoma. There was no positive rechallenge with the subsequent doses. The AE of headache was likely related to rozanolixizumab given the time of onset.
- **Subject MG0003/**(b) (6): 39-year-old male experienced a nonserious TEAE of severe headache 3 days after the first SC infusion of rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg, which resolved 3 days after onset. The subject experienced a second nonserious TEAE of moderate headache 9 days after the first dose and 1 day after the second rozanolixizumab infusion. This second event resolved 3 days after onset. The subject completed a total of five infusions in the study. There was no positive rechallenge with the subsequent doses. The AE of headache was likely related to rozanolixizumab given the time of onset and a positive rechallenge.
- **Subject MG0003/**(b) (6): 35-year-old female experienced a nonserious TEAE of severe headache 3 days after the first SC infusion of rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg, which resolved 3 days after onset. The subject experienced a second nonserious TEAE of mild headache 9 days after the first dose and 1 day after the second rozanolixizumab infusion, which resolved on the same day. The subject experienced a third nonserious TEAE of mild headache 3 days after the fifth and final dose, which also resolved on the same day. The AE of headache was likely related to rozanolixizumab given the time of onset and a positive rechallenge.
L892: In Safety Pool S1 pooled from Studies MG0003, MG0004, and MG0007, out of 196 subjects treated with rozanolixizumab, 98 (50%) subjects experienced headache including 52 (54.7%) subjects in the rozanolixizumab \approx7 mg/kg arm and 46 (45.5%) subjects in the rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg arm. The incidence of headache did not increase with repeated treatment cycles. There were no serious TEAEs of headache in the open-label MG0004 and MG0007 studies. A total of 12 subjects experienced severe headache in Pool S1 including seven subjects in the double-blind Study MG0003 discussed above. In 120-day safety update, there were no additional cases of severe or serious headaches.
L893: ## **Case narrative for TEAEs of Severe Headache, Study MG0007 (Source: Module 5.3.5.2. MG0007 Narratives-120-day Safety Update Cutoff October 7, 2022):**
L894: - **Subject MG0007-**(b) (6): 56-year-old female experienced a nonserious TEAE of a mild headache 1 day after the first SC infusion of rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg dose in Study MG0003, which resolved on the same day. The subject experienced another TEAE of a mild headache 1 day after fourth and then a moderate headache after fifth infusion, both of which resolved within 1 day after onset. The subject completed six planned doses in Study MG0003. Subsequently, the subject received about 6 months of weekly SC infusions of rozanolixizumab \approx7 mg/kg dose in the OLE Study MG0004 without any TEAEs. The subject discontinued MG0004 and enrolled into MG0007 study. The subject experienced a TEAE of mild headache during the seventh treatment cycle of rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg. Later, the subject experienced a nonserious TEAE of severe headache 4 days after 26th infusion in Study MG0007, which resolved 3 days after onset. The subject continued the study treatment without any changes made to their rozanolixizumab dosing regimen. The AE of headache was likely related to rozanolixizumab given the time of onset and positive rechallenges.
L895: ## **Case Narratives for TEAEs of Severe Headache, Study MG0004 (Source: Module 5.3.5.2. MG0004 Narratives):**
L896: - **Subject MG0004/**(b) (6): 66-year-old female experienced a nonserious TEAE of moderate headache 2 days after the first SC infusion of rozanolixizumab \approx7 mg/kg in Study MG0003, which resolved within 1 day. The subject completed all six doses planned in the double-blind treatment cycle and enrolled into the OLE Study MG0004. The subject experienced a nonserious TEAE of bilateral pulsatile severe headache 2 days after receiving the first open-label dose of rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg. The subject also experienced fevers (38.3°C), pelvic pain, dysuria with burning, and mild abdominal pain. The headache and fever improved the next day and the event was resolved in 2 days. A bacterial test confirmed urinary tract infection, which was treated with fosfomycin. The subject experienced another TEAE of mild headache after 24-week infusion and a nonserious TEAE of moderate headache after 51-week infusion of rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg dose in Study MG0004. The subject completed MG0004 study without any further events and enrolled into MG0007 study. The AE of headache was likely related to rozanolixizumab given the time of onset and positive rechallenges.
- **Subject MG0004/**(b) (6): 35-year-old female experienced a nonserious TEAE of severe headache 2 days after the first infusion of rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg dose in Study MG0004. The subject also experienced nausea, dizziness, neck pain or stiffness, increased sensitivity to light, and vomiting. The headache got worse with physical activity. The subject received metamizole, ibuprofen, and paracetamol as treatment for the headache. The event resolved 2 days after onset. The dosing regimen for rozanolixizumab was unchanged. The subject experienced mild headaches treated with paracetamol within 1 to 2 days of repeated weekly infusions of rozanolixizumab during the Study MG0004. The subject withdrew from the study after Week 14 dose. The subject had experienced two episodes of milder headaches after the first and sixth (final) infusion of placebo in Study MG0003. The subject had not experienced headache in the 5 years preceding the
L897: study. The AE of headache was likely related to rozanolixizumab in Study MG0004 given the time of onset and positive rechallenges.
L898: - **Subject MG0004/** (b) (6): 60-year-old female experienced a nonserious TEAE of severe headache 3 days after the first infusion of rozanolixizumab \approx7 mg/kg dose. The subject described the headache as intermittent, pulsating feeling from the back of the head to the neck bilaterally associated with nausea, neck stiffness, phonophobia, photophobia, and tingling and numbness in the lower legs, weakness on “one side”, and pain on one side of head. The subject received diphenhydramine, paracetamol, and paracetamol-codeine combination for headache. The event resolved 7 days after onset. The subject transitioned into Study MG0007. The subject received placebo infusions in Study MG0003. The subject had no history of migraine or headache in the past. The subject was taking ramipril, amlodipine, and hydrochlorothiazide for hypertension. The TEAE of headache was likely related to rozanolixizumab given the time of onset.
- **Subject MG0004/** (b) (6): 27-year-old female experienced a nonserious TEAE of severe headache 3 days after the first infusion of rozanolixizumab \approx7 mg/kg dose. The subject did not experience nausea, vomiting, or other neurological symptoms. The subject received ibuprofen, paracetamol, codeine, and paracetamol with tramadol for headache. The event resolved 3 days after onset. The subject received a total of five infusions in Study MG0004 and the drug was withdrawn due to pregnancy. The subject experienced MG crisis 39 days after the last infusion, which resolved with IVIg treatment. The subject had experienced two episodes of mild headache while receiving placebo infusions in Study MG0003. The AE of headache was likely related to rozanolixizumab given the time of onset.
- **Subject MG0004/** (b) (6) (see MG0003/ (b) (6) for MG0003): 39-year-old male experienced a nonserious TEAE of severe headache 3 days after the first infusion of rozanolixizumab \approx7 mg/kg dose. The subject was treated with ibuprofen for the headache. The subject also experienced pyrexia which resolved on the same day. The subject experienced a repeat mild headache 3 days after week 6 infusion which resolved 3 days after onset. The subject received 23 infusions in Study MG0004 with no repeat episodes of headaches. The subject rolled into Study MG0007. The subject had experienced nonserious TEAEs of two episodes of moderate to severe headaches while receiving rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg in Study MG0003. L899: ## Conclusion
L900: Headache was the most commonly reported adverse events related to rozanolixizumab in the phase 3 studies reported by 50% of subjects (98 out of 196 subjects). In the placebo-controlled Study MG0003, headaches occurred at higher frequency in subjects treated with rozanolixizumab than placebo (43.6% versus 19.4%). Headaches were classified as migraine in 4 (3%) of subjects. There were no cases of tension headaches. The reviewer notes that the medical history of headache was balanced across the treatment groups at baseline. In phase 3 studies of subjects with gMG, there were 12 (6%) cases of severe headaches, one case of serious headache requiring hospitalization, and one case of headache that led to discontinuation of rozanolixizumab.
L901: Headaches generally occurred within 1 to 4 days after the infusion and more commonly after the first infusion. Subjects with headaches also reported associated symptoms of nausea, dizziness, neck pain or stiffness, increased sensitivity to light, and vomiting. These subjects did not undergo evaluation for meningitis. The Applicant reported three subjects with aseptic meningitis in their rozanolixizumab studies, including one subject with gMG in Study MG0007 and two cases with myelin oligodendrocyte glycoprotein antibody disease (MOG-AD (see Section II.7.7.1)). The pathogenesis of headache in the subjects treated with rozanolixizumab is unknown. Given the timeline of event in relation to rozanolixizumab infusion, it is possible that headaches represent drug hypersensitivity. In some cases of severe headaches with associated symptoms, headaches potentially represent drug-induced aseptic meningitis. The reviewer recommends inclusion of headache as the most frequent adverse event and a Warning and Precaution statement for aseptic meningitis in labeling for rozanolixizumab to alert treating physicians for adequate risk mitigation.
L902: ## 7.7.3. Hypersensitivity Reactions
L903: ## Issue
L904: TEAEs related to hypersensitivity reactions and local reactions at the site of administration were frequently observed in the subjects treated with rozanolixizumab in the Studies MG0003, MG0004, and MG0007.
L905: ## Background
L906: The topic of hypersensitivity reactions was of special interest as the Applicant's proposed label notes (b) (4). Rozanolixizumab is a humanized anti-FcRn IgG4 monoclonal antibody that will be administered as a subcutaneous infusion. Like other monoclonal antibodies (Isabwe et al. 2018; Pintea et al. 2021), rozanolixizumab treatment may potentially lead to an increase in hypersensitivity reactions and local reactions at the site of administration.
L907: ## Assessment
L908: In Study MG0003, subjects were observed at the study site for at least 4 hours following the first two infusions, and then 2 hours for subsequent infusions. In the open-label chronic weekly administration Study MG0004, subjects were observed at the study site for at least four hours following the first two infusions, and then two hours for subsequent infusions until Week 7, and afterwards at least 1 hour at the investigator's discretion. In the open-label cyclical treatment Study MG0007, subjects were observed for at least 1 hour following the first two infusions, and then a minimum of 15 minutes for subsequent infusions.
L909: ## Anaphylactic Reactions
L910: In Study MG0003, no TEAEs met the criteria for potential anaphylactic reactions during the double-blind treatment cycle. Similarly, in Safety Pool S1 pooling the data from Studies MG0003, MG0004, and MG0007, no TEAEs met the criteria for anaphylactic reactions. In 120-day safety update dated February 3, 2023, eCTD Sequence number 0012), a single nonserious
L911: mild intensity TEAE of anaphylactic reaction to the bee sting and ant bite in the right upper extremity was reported that occurred 7 days after the last infusion of rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg dose.
L912: ## Hypersensitivity Reactions
L913: Table 63 shows the frequency of TEAEs of hypersensitivity reactions in the subjects treated with rozanolixizumab in the Study MG0003. Overall, seven (10.9%) subjects in the rozanolixizumab \approx7 mg/kg arm and eight (11.6%) subjects in the rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg arm experienced symptoms and signs of hypersensitivity reactions compared with three (4.5%) subjects in the placebo arm. A majority of subjects experienced skin rash, erythema, and pruritis. One subject (MG0003/ [REDACTED](b) (6)) experienced swollen tongue the same day as the first infusion of rozanolixizumab \approx7 mg/kg dose and received cetirizine and paracetamol. The subject received prophylactic cetirizine for the remaining doses. The subject experienced a second episode of tongue swelling 4 days after the fourth infusion. Both events resolved within a day and the study treatment was unchanged. All hypersensitivity reactions occurred within 1 to 13 days after the recent rozanolixizumab infusion and were observed after a total of 1 through 6 infusions in a 6-weekly infusion treatment cycle. In Safety Pool S1, out of 196 subjects, 26 (13.3%) subjects experienced skin rash, 17 (8.7%) experienced erythema, and 9 (4.6%) and 3 (1.5%) had TEAEs of pruritis and urticaria, respectively. In 120-day safety update, 27 (14.4%) subjects had TEAEs mapping to the SMQ hypersensitivity including 12 (6.4%) subjects who experienced rash and 3 (1.6%) subjects who experienced urticaria. The TEAEs of hypersensitivity reactions were mild or moderate intensity. There were no severe or serious TEAEs. One subject (MG0003- [REDACTED](b) (6)) discontinued rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg dose treatment due to pruritis in Study MG0003 (see Section 7.6.2.4). The time of onset of pruritis was 29 days after the first infusion and 8 days after most recent fourth infusion. The Applicant determined the pruritis was likely related to rozanolixizumab because there was no alternative explanation and the reported de-challenge.
L914: In the 120-day safety update (dated February 3, 2023, eCTD Sequence number 0012), the Applicant stated that across the rozanolixizumab program, one subject with idiopathic thrombocytopenic purpura in study TP0006 reported serious urticaria of severe intensity 46 days after the first and fourth day after the most recent rozanolixizumab infusion.
L915: ## Administration Site Reactions
L916: Table 63 shows the frequency of TEAEs of administration site reactions in the subjects treated with rozanolixizumab in the Study MG0003. Overall, four (6.2%) subjects in the rozanolixizumab \approx7 mg/kg arm and six (8.7%) subjects in the rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg arm experienced symptoms and signs of administration site reactions compared with two (3%) subjects in the placebo arm. The TEAE symptoms included skin rash, redness, itching, pain, discomfort, inflammation, and bruising, with each category occurring in mostly one subject. These administration site reactions occurred within 1 to 3 days after administration and were observed after a total of 1 through 6 infusions in a 6-weekly infusion treatment cycle. In Safety Pool S1, out of 196 subjects, 26 (13.3%) experienced TEAEs of administration site reactions. There was no change in the number of subjects with TEAEs of administration site reactions in
L917: the 120-day safety update. All TEAEs were graded as mild. There were no severe or serious TEAEs.

L925: The reviewer notes that pyrexia was one of the most common TEAEs in the subjects receiving rozanolixizumab. In Study MG0003, 15 (21.7%) subjects in the rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg arm and 8 (12.5%) subjects in the rozanolixizumab \approx7 mg/kg arm experienced fevers compared with 1 (1.5%) in the placebo arm. Most fever events occurred within 4 days (mean 2.7 days) after rozanolixizumab infusion and lasted for the average of 1.2 days (range 0 to 4 days). All events were considered mild or moderate intensity and resolved without sequelae. The reviewer cannot rule out drug hypersensitivity as a potential cause for fevers in the subjects when no other cause of fever was evident after clinical and laboratory investigation.
L926: ## **Conclusion**
L927: The TEAEs of hypersensitivity reactions including skin rash, pruritis, swollen tongue, and flushing, as well as administration site reactions such as rash, redness, inflammation, itching, pain, and discomfort, were frequently observed in rozanolixizumab treated subjects in Studies MG0003, MG0004, and MG0007. There were no severe or serious TEAEs, or anaphylactic reactions reported during these studies. One subject discontinued rozanolixizumab due to a hypersensitivity reaction (pruritis).
L928: The reviewer recommends that hypersensitivity reactions should be included in Warnings and Precautions. The label for a related drug, efgartigimod, has a Warning and Precaution for hypersensitivity reactions. The reviewer notes that hypersensitivity reactions are often unpredictable and can potentially lead to serious outcomes such as hypotension, anaphylaxis, or death. A Warnings and Precautions statement will help alert providers to this risk.
L929: ## **7.7.4. Gastrointestinal Disorders**
L930: ## **Issue**
L931: TEAEs related to gastrointestinal (GI) disorders including diarrhea, nausea, and vomiting were frequently observed in the subjects treated with rozanolixizumab in the Studies MG0003, MG0004, and MG0007. These GI disorders TEAEs were also considered adverse events of special interest during these studies.
L932: ## **Background**
L933: The Applicant's proposed label notes that diarrhea was one of the most common adverse reactions (\ge 10\%) and other GI symptoms such as nausea and [REDACTED] (b) (4) were more frequently reported aEs (\ge 5\%) in the subjects treated with rozanolixizumab compared to placebo in clinical studies.
L934: ## **Assessment**
L935: **Table 64** shows that in Study MG0003, the TEAEs of diarrhea, nausea, vomiting, and abdominal pain were reported in \ge 5\% of subjects treated with rozanolixizumab and occurred more frequently in the rozanolixizumab arm than the placebo arm. The TEAE of diarrhea was more common in the rozanolixizumab \approx 7 mg/kg arm (25%) compared to rozanolixizumab \approx 10 mg/kg arm (15.9%) and placebo arm (13.4%). The majority of these GI disorders TEAEs were graded as nonserious events of mild or moderate maximum intensity. Two (3.1%) subjects in the rozanolixizumab \approx 7 mg/kg arm experienced serious TEAEs of severe vomiting (n=1) and gastritis (n=1) which led to temporary drug discontinuation. Two (2.9%) subjects in the rozanolixizumab \approx 10 mg/kg arm experienced nonserious TEAEs of vomiting and abdominal pain (n=1), and diarrhea (n=1) which led to discontinuation from study. The case narratives are described below.

L944: The case narratives of two subjects who experienced serious TEAEs of vomiting and gastritis leading to temporary treatment discontinuation in the rozanolixizumab ≈7 mg/kg arm are described below (source: Module 5.3.5.1. mg0003-narratives):
L945: - **Subject MG0003/**(b) (6): 60-year-old male experienced serious TEAEs of vomiting and MG exacerbation 3 days after the first infusion of rozanolixizumab ≈7 mg/kg dose in Study MG0003. The subject presented to the emergency room with nausea, vomiting, and diarrhea along with severe MG exacerbation symptoms including worsening speech pronunciation, droopy eyelids, and shortness of breath. The subject threw up his usual dose of pyridostigmine with vomiting. The subject was hospitalized and placed on supplemental oxygen without a need for further ventilation. The subject had a fever (39.1°C) and oxygen saturation of 90s. Complete blood count was notable for total leukocyte count increased from 10.9 to 14.7 in 2 days (unit and range not reported) and comprehensive metabolic panel showed glucose 193 (unit and range not reported). An x-ray of the chest showed increased central interstitial markings and pulmonary edema versus atypical infection. The subject had a negative COVID-19 antigen test result. The subject was diagnosed with aspiration pneumonia of mild intensity. The subject was treated with pyridostigmine for MG, albuterol with ipratropium, ceftriaxone, metronidazole, and magnesium sulfate in dextrose for pneumonia aspiration, and metronidazole and cefdinir for pneumonia/sepsis. The symptoms resolved 3 days after onset. The subject did not require rescue therapy for MG worsening. The subject completed five infusions of rozanolixizumab ≈7 mg/kg dose (missed the second dose) and then rolled into open-label Study MG0007. There was no reported repeated dose rechallenge. The serious TEAE of severe vomiting was likely related to rozanolixizumab.
- **Subject MG0003/**(b) (6): 55-year-old male experienced a serious TEAE of gastritis 24 days after the first and third day after the fourth infusion of rozanolixizumab ≈7 mg/kg dose in Study MG0003. The subject was admitted to the emergency room with mild vomiting and nonspecific chest pain due to gastritis and gastroesophageal reflux disease (GERD). The subject was treated with ketorolac and discharged after 23 hours. The Applicant stated in the case narrative that the laboratory test results, electrocardiogram, and imaging confirmed that chest pain was nonspecific and due to pre-existing condition of GERD. The laboratory tests provided in the case narrative were within reference range for complete blood count, liver
L946: enzymes, procalcitonin, and troponin levels. The study drug was temporarily interrupted due to the TEAE of gastritis. The subject completed five infusions and rolled onto open-label Study MG0004. There was no reported repeated dose rechallenge. The serious TEAE of mild gastritis was likely related to rozanolixizumab.
L947: The case narratives of two subjects who experienced nonserious TEAEs of vomiting, abdominal pain, and diarrhea leading to permanent treatment discontinuation in the rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg arm are described below (source: Module 5.3.5.1. mg0003-narratives):
L948: - **Subject MG0003/** (b) (6): 41-year-old female experienced nonserious TEAEs of upper abdominal pain (epigastric pain) and vomiting 4 days after the first infusion of rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg dose in Study MG0003. The subject received hyoscine and tiquizium for the upper abdominal pain and domperidone and metoclopramide for the vomiting on the same day as the second infusion of rozanolixizumab. The symptoms resolved 19 days after onset and 16 days after the last dose of rozanolixizumab. The Applicant noted that the subject received the second dose of rozanolixizumab despite the investigator considering the TEAEs of abdominal pain and vomiting related to rozanolixizumab. The subject received treatment for the TEAEs after receiving the second dose of rozanolixizumab. The subject discontinued from the study and did not enroll into either of the open-label Studies MG0004 or MG0007.
- **Subject MG0003/** (b) (6): 67-year-old male experienced a nonserious TEAE of severe diarrhea 2 days after the first infusion of rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg dose in Study MG0003. The subject reported Grade 3 diarrhea, cramping abdominal pain, and fever (38.3°C) 20 hours after the rozanolixizumab administration. The subject did not have vomiting or jaundice. The subject self-treated his symptoms with paracetamol at home. The subject had persistent diarrhea described as at least 10 episodes of loose stools without blood or mucus or undigested fats in the stools over 2 days. The subject received unspecified treatment in the emergency room and later hospitalized for diarrhea. The hospital workup excluded any condition which could have caused diarrhea. Laboratory test results (units and normal ranges not reported) revealed leucocyte count 14, C-reactive protein level 9, and bilirubin level 30. On day 4 after symptom onset, laboratory test results (units and normal ranges not reported) revealed sodium level 138, potassium level 3.8, chloride level 101, glucose level 5.1, urea level 7, creatinine level 111, bilirubin level 23, albumin 41, alanine aminotransferase 0.82, aspartate aminotransferase 0.39, gamma-glutamyl transferase 0.75, amylase 0.51, lipase 0.25, differential leucocytes 13.5, and no other significant abnormalities were noted. On the same day, an abdominal ultrasound revealed nonsignificant hepatosteatosis, and no other relevant findings or signs of cholecystopathy were noted. A stool culture was not performed. The immunoglobulin E predose was increased. The subject received natrium and a combination of fenpiverinium with metamizole and pitofenone as treatment medications for the diarrhea. The event of diarrhea resolved 6 days after onset. Rozanolixizumab treatment was withdrawn due to diarrhea. The subject had a mild episode of diarrhea 5 months after the last dose of rozanolixizumab. The TEAE of diarrhea was likely related to rozanolixizumab.
L949: Table 65 shows the frequency of the GI disorders TEAEs including diarrhea, abdominal pain, nausea, and vomiting in the Safety Pool S1 comprising pooled data from Studies MG0003, MG0004, and MG0007. Overall, 89 (45.4%) subjects out of 196 subjects experienced GI disorders TEAEs with the frequency of the aEs balanced between rozanolixizumab \approx7 mg/kg and \approx10 mg/kg dose arms. The TEAE of diarrhea was the most common (31.6%) followed by nausea (16.8%), abdominal pain (15.3%), and vomiting (7.7%). Most TEAEs were of mild or moderate maximum intensity. There were no severe or serious TEAEs in the open-label Study MG0007. There were no treatment interruptions or discontinuation due to the GI TEAEs in Study MG0007. Overall, the incidence of the GI events remained similar with repeated treatment cycles (CSR MG0007 and 120-day safety update eCTD sequence number 0012). The Applicant reported in their 120-day safety update that there were 23 new GI disorders TEAEs and consistent with the BLA, the most common TEAEs were diarrhea, nausea, and abdominal pain.

L958: ## Conclusion
L959: The TEAEs of GI disorders including diarrhea, vomiting, nausea, and abdominal pain were frequently observed in the subjects receiving rozanolixizumab during the Studies MG0003, MG0004, AND MG0007. The event of diarrhea was the most common TEAE in rozanolixizumab treated subjects. A majority of these TEAEs were nonserious and of mild or moderate maximum intensity; however, there were also serious and severe GI events which resulted in the emergency room visit, hospitalization, treatment interruptions and study discontinuation.
L960: ## 7.7.5. Hypoalbuminemia
L961: ## Issue
L962: TEAE of hypoalbuminemia was observed in the rozanolixizumab treated subjects in the Studies MG0003, MG0004, and MG0007.
L963: ## Background
L964: The proposed mechanism of rozanolixizumab action is to inhibit the activity of neonatal Fc receptor (FcRn) and accelerate the catabolism of IgG, including MG-specific autoantibodies. The FcRn has affinity for both IgG and albumin, the two most abundant plasma proteins accounting for 80% to 90% of total protein pool. The FcRn plays an important role in the homeostatic regulation of IgG and albumin. Therefore, the topic of hypoalbuminemia was of special interest in this review.
L965: ## Assessment
L966: In Study MG0003, there was a mean decrease in serum albumin levels from baseline to day 43 of -2.2 g/L in the rozanolixizumab \approx7 mg/kg arm and -2.3 g/L in the rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg arm compared to -0.45 g/L in the placebo arm (see Figure 11; also refer to Section 7.6.2.6). The decrease in mean albumin levels were observed from the first post baseline visit on day 8 and the mean albumin levels remained persistently lower in the rozanolixizumab-treated subjects compared to placebo throughout the treatment period. The albumin levels recovered to the placebo levels on day 71 during the observation period. The maximum lowest albumin value was 2.9 g/L in one subject receiving rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg dose at day 99, the last day in the observation period (see Section 7.6.2.6). None of the subjects had serum albumin levels lower than 2.5 g/L in the Study MG0003.
L967: **Figure 11. Mean Serum Albumin Levels Change From Baseline Over Time, Safety Population, Study MG0003**
L968: Source: ad b.xpt; Software: R
L969: Note: Figures do not include time points with data from fewer than 10% of randomized/enrolled patients in all treatment groups.
L970: Abbreviations: RLZ, rozanolixizumab
L971: In the OLE Study MG0007, the mean reductions in the serum albumin levels ranged between -2.7 g/L (cycle 6, day 43) and -1.1 g/L (cycle 5, day 43) in the rozanolixizumab ~7 mg/kg arm and between -3.3 g/L (cycle 7, day 15) and -0.5 g/L (cycle 5, day 15) in the rozanolixizumab ~10 mg/kg arm (CSR MG0007). None of the subjects had serum albumin levels lower than 3.1 g/L during any of the repeat treatment cycles (Table 66). In 120-day safety update (eCTD sequence number 012), the Applicant stated that there were minimal (around 5%) reductions in albumin levels during treatment period which were not considered clinically meaningful in subjects treated with rozanolixizumab.

L978: ## **Conclusion**
L979: Whereas reduction in the serum albumin levels were reported with rozanolixizumab treatment, these reductions (up to \approx5% of baseline value) were not clinically significant in the subjects with gMG.
L980: ## **7.7.6. Immunization**
L981: ## **Issue**
L982: The draft label has a section on immunization with vaccines.
L983: ## **Background**
L984: Treatment with rozanolixizumab is associated with increased risk of infections and reductions in the serum IgG levels. Refer to Section 7.7.1 for further details.
L985: The Applicant has submitted the following language in the draft label:
L986: (b) (4)
L987: ## **Assessment**
L988: Subjects who had received a live vaccination within 8 weeks before baseline visit or intended to have a live vaccination during the course of the Study or within 8 weeks following the final dose of study medication were excluded from the Studies MG0003, MG0004, MG0007. The reviewer notes that insufficient data are submitted by the Applicant to support safety of immunization with any vaccine during treatment with rozanolixizumab. Because treatment with rozanolixizumab is associated with reductions in serum IgG levels and increased frequency of infections, the reviewer agrees that the safety of immunization with live-attenuated or live vaccines and the response to immunization with any vaccine is unknown during treatment with rozanolixizumab, and that patients should be vaccinated with age-appropriate vaccinations according to immunization guidelines before starting treatment with rozanolixizumab.
L989: ## **Conclusion**
L990: Because treatment with rozanolixizumab is associated with reductions in serum IgG levels and increased frequency of infections, a Warnings and Precautions statement in labeling can help highlight that the safety of immunization with live-attenuated or live vaccines and the response
L991: to immunization with any vaccine is unknown during treatment with rozanolixizumab, and that patients should be vaccinated with age-appropriate vaccinations according to immunization guidelines before starting treatment with rozanolixizumab.
L992: ## 8. Therapeutic Individualization
L993: ## 8.1. Intrinsic Factors
L994: ## Hepatic Impairment
L995: No dedicated hepatic impairment study was performed. The Applicant recommends that no dose adjustment is needed in patients with hepatic impairment. Although patients with hepatic impairment were allowed to enroll in Study MG0003, no data are available to allow identification and classification of hepatically impaired patients based on Child-Pugh criteria.
L996: Rozanolixizumab is expected to be predominantly catabolized by lysosomal degradation to small peptides and amino acids. Further, markers of hepatic function, e.g., total bilirubin, ALT, AST, ALP were evaluated as potential covariates in the population pharmacokinetic (PK)/PD analysis but were not found to influence the final population PK/PD model parameters (Section 14.5.2.3, Figure 34).
L997: As a result, hepatic impairment is not expected to affect rozanolixizumab clearance, and therefore, the review team agrees with the Applicant's recommendation that no dose adjustment is needed in patients with hepatic impairment.
L998: ## Renal Impairment
L999: No dedicated renal impairment study was performed. The Applicant stated that patients with mild to moderate renal impairment were allowed to enroll in the clinical program. Per the Applicant, based on population PK/PD analyses, which included subjects with mild and moderate renal impairment, eGFR (range: 38.2 to 161 mL/min/1.73m2) did not significantly impact rozanolixizumab clearance. There are no data on the impact of severe renal impairment on PK of rozanolixizumab. As rozanolixizumab is a monoclonal antibody (with molecular weight &gt;69 kDa), it is not expected to undergo renal elimination or excretion and thus no dosage adjustment is necessary for rozanolixizumab in patients with renal impairment.
L1000: ## Other Intrinsic Factors
L1001: The population PK analyses indicated that rozanolixizumab PK was not affected by age range: (range 18 to 89 years) and sex (57.5% female). For race, the phase 1 Study (UP0060) enrolled Chinese, Japanese, and Caucasian subjects who received single doses of rozanolixizumab at 4 mg/kg, \approx7 mg/kg or \approx10 mg/kg. In this study, compared to the Caucasians, lower exposures were observed in Chinese and Japanese subjects. This can be attributed to the lower body weight of Chinese and Japanese subjects, thus leading to lower actual dose (mg). However, after accounting for the body weight differences across Chinese, Japanese, and Caucasian populations, exploratory population PK/PD analysis revealed that ethnicity was not a significant covariate to affect PK or PD of rozanolixizumab. In the final population PK/PD analyses, due to small
L1002: number of patients in individual ethnic categories, the subjects were grouped as either Asian or non-Asian. The Applicant noted that no significant differences in rozanolixizumab clearance (CL)/fraction absorbed) F or V/F or in the total IgG or anti-AChR parameters of the models were found between Asian and Non-Asian subjects. Thus, the Applicant recommends that no dose adjustments are warranted based on these factors (Section 14.5), and the review team agrees with that recommendation.
L1003: Body weight was found to be significant covariate to affect PK of rozanolixizumab. Refer to the Pharmacometrics review for additional discussion on effect of body weight on PK and PD of rozanolixizumab.
L1004: ## 8.2. Extrinsic Factors
L1005: ## 8.2.1. Drug Interactions
L1006: ## 8.2.1.1. Effect of Other Drugs on Rozanolixizumab
L1007: Rozanolixizumab is a human IgG1 Fc-fragment and is expected to be catabolized by degradation to small peptides and individual amino acids, which are expected to be excreted or recycled via pathways in the same manner as endogenous IgG in humans. Rozanolixizumab is not subject to disposition via CYP450 metabolism and traditional drug-transporter-mediated pathways, and therefore, drug-interaction liabilities with CYP enzyme or transporter modulators are not expected.
L1008: In vitro pharmacology studies revealed that at pH of 7.4, rozanolixizumab K_D (dissociation constant) was 55pM for human FcRn, likely indicating efficient FcRn blockage as binding of rozanolixizumab already occurs at the cell surface without the competition of endogenous IgG that is unable to bind FcRn at neutral pH. Further, rozanolixizumab was reported to bind human FcRn expressed at cell surface in Madin-Darby Canine Kidney (K_D=0.43\text{nM} at pH 7.4).
L1009: After binding to FcRn, a portion of rozanolixizumab is hypothesized to be subject to lysosomal degradation alone or in complex with FcRn. The percentage of potential loss of FcRn-complexed rozanolixizumab has not been investigated; hence the rate of its catabolism, though unknown, is likely the predominant pathway in disposition of rozanolixizumab. Therefore, other therapeutic proteins are unlikely to alter the binding of rozanolixizumab to FcRn receptor.
L1010: ## 8.2.1.2. Effect of Rozanolixizumab on Other Drugs
L1011: Rozanolixizumab binds to FcRn and may decrease the concentrations of therapies that binds to human FcRn, i.e., immunoglobulin products, monoclonal antibodies, or antibody derivatives containing the human Fc domain of the IgG subclass, likely to result in reduced effectiveness of these moieties, if administered concomitantly.
L1012: The Applicant noted that approximately two weeks after the last of six weekly subcutaneous infusions of rozanolixizumab \approx 7\text{ mg/kg} or \approx 10\text{ mg/kg}, total IgG levels start returning towards baseline, while rozanolixizumab concentrations were mostly below the limit of quantitation
L1013: observed at the end of the infusion (seven days post-dose). Based on these data, the Applicant states that 2 weeks after the last infusion, IgG catabolism is no longer meaningfully affected by rozanolixizumab and has returned to its normal rate. The Applicant expects that a clinically relevant effect on PK and PD of therapeutic moieties noted above is unlikely to occur when they are given 2 weeks or later after the last rozanolixizumab infusion. If possible, the Applicant recommends postponing the start of treatment with a therapeutic moiety noted above until two weeks after the last rozanolixizumab dose is administered.
L1014: The review team acknowledges that the total IgG levels start to increase after the last of six weekly subcutaneous infusions of rozanolixizumab \approx7 mg/kg or \approx10 mg/kg, but they return to baseline levels approximately nine weeks after the last (sixth) infusion of rozanolixizumab. Therefore, the rationale for Applicant's recommendation to postpone the initiation of treatment with a therapeutic moiety noted above until 2 weeks after the last rozanolixizumab dose is administered, is unclear.
L1015: The review team recommends close monitoring for reduced effectiveness of therapeutic moieties that bind to FcRn such as those noted above when concomitant use is necessary. However, when long-term use of medications is necessary for patient care, the review team recommends stopping rozanolixizumab and using alternative medications to treat gMG. Furthermore, minimal reductions in albumin were observed during rozanolixizumab treatment (phase 3 study) which were not clinically meaningful. Therefore, rozanolixizumab is not expected to have a clinically relevant effect on the disposition of highly protein-bound drugs.
L1016: ## **Immunizations**
L1017: Immunization with vaccines during rozanolixizumab therapy has not been studied. The Applicant notes that the safety of immunization with live or live-attenuated vaccines and the response to immunization with vaccines are unknown and vaccination with live-attenuated or live vaccines is not recommended during treatment with rozanolixizumab.
L1018: ## **8.3. Plans for Pediatric Drug Development**
L1019: Not applicable. Rozanolixizumab was granted orphan drug designation. Pediatric assessments under the Pediatric Research Equity Act are not required.
L1020: ## **8.4. Pregnancy, Lactation, and Females/Males of Reproductive Potential**
L1021: There are limited data for the use of rozanolixizumab in pregnant and lactating women because the subjects of childbearing potential were required to use a highly effective method of birth control and study drug was discontinued if they became pregnant as per the protocol. There was one subject MG0004/ [REDACTED] (b) (6) in the open-label Study MG0004 who gave birth to a full-term healthy child after 4-weeks exposure to rozanolixizumab \approx7 mg/kg dose in the first trimester of pregnancy. Rozanolixizumab was discontinued after a positive pregnancy test. The subject had an SAE of MG crisis 39 days after the last dose of rozanolixizumab, which resolved
L1022: 4 days after onset with IVIg therapy. She had received placebo in the double-blind controlled Study MG0003.
L1023: ## 9. Product Quality
L1024: ## Approval With Postmarketing Commitments
L1025: Rozanolixizumab is a recombinant, humanized antineonatal Fc receptor (FcRn) immunoglobulin G4 monoclonal antibody with amino acid substitution at serine (b) (4) to proline (IgG4P) to prevent Fab arm exchange characteristic of endogenous human IgG4. Rozanolixizumab is produced in Chinese hamster ovary DG44 cell line by recombinant DNA technology. The approximate molecular weight is 148 kDa.
L1026: Rystiggo (rozanolixizumab-noli) injection is supplied as a sterile, preservative-free, colorless to pale brownish yellow, clear to slightly opalescent solution for subcutaneous injection. Each vial contains 2mL of rozanolixizumab at a nominal formulation of 140 mg/mL in [REDACTED] (b) (4) L-histidine hydrochloride monohydrate, (b) (4) mM L-proline, (b) (4) % (w/v) polysorbate 80, at pH 5.6.
L1027: The Office of Pharmaceutical Quality, CDER, recommends approval of BLA 761286 for Rystiggo manufactured by UCB, Inc. for human use under conditions specified in the package insert. The data submitted in this application are adequate to support the conclusion that the manufacture of Rystiggo is well-controlled and leads to a product that is pure and potent.
L1028: The chemistry, manufacturing, and controls postmarketing commitments to be included in the action letter are listed below.
L1029: ## CMC PMC #1
L1030: To develop a two-tiered reference standard system consisting of primary and working reference standards to support the rozanolixizumab product lifecycle. The primary and working reference standard will be created from representative DS batches which have passed all release specifications. The first new reference standards will be qualified against the current reference standard [REDACTED] (b) (4) according to a predefined protocol. The results for qualification of new primary and working/secondary reference standards will be submitted in a Prior Approval Supplement. Alternatively, protocols for qualification and requalification of future primary and working/secondary reference standards may be submitted as a Prior Approval Supplement, which may facilitate a reduced reporting category for implementation of new reference standards. Final reference standard qualification report submission date: 02/29/2024
L1031: ## CMC PMC #2
L1032: To perform a shipping validation study under real time shipping conditions (i.e., temperature, mode of transport, shipping duration, and shipping containers and packing representative of the minimum and maximum load) using rozanolixizumab finished product in the final commercial container closure and packaging systems to verify the impact of shipping on the product quality of rozanolixizumab finished product. The shipping validation data will be submitted in accordance with 21 CFR 601.12.

L1039: ## III. Additional Analyses and Information
L1040: ## 12. Summary of Regulatory History
L1041: Rozanolixizumab was developed by Union Chimique Belge (UCB) under investigational new drug (IND) 132407 for the treatment of myasthenia gravis (MG) in adults. In the Division's December 9, 2016, pre-IND written responses, the Division responded to the Sponsor's interest in developing a patient-reported outcome (PRO) assessment that would be used to evaluate treatment benefit directly from the patients' perspective for use as a secondary endpoint (b)(4). The Division agreed with the core concepts of the PRO but was concerned with the validity of the PRO instrument (b)(4).
L1042: The IND was submitted to the Agency on February 28, 2017. Initial reviews described rozanolixizumab as a recombinant, humanized, neonatal Fc receptor (FcRn) immunoglobulin (Ig) G4P monoclonal antibody. The Applicant stated that a first-in-human study (UP0018) was conducted and completed in the United Kingdom and there was an ongoing study in patients with immune thrombocytopenia (ITP) (TP0001) in the European Union and Australia. Therefore, the initial IND proposed a phase 2a clinical study (MG0002) in subjects with moderate to severe generalized MG. The study was allowed to proceed on March 29, 2017. Rozanolixizumab was granted orphan drug designation on February 1, 2019, for the treatment of gMG.
L1043: At the end-of-phase-2 meeting, discussions focused on the design and adequacy of the proposed phase 3 Study, MG0003 (minutes dated June 14, 2019), including the use of a novel MG symptoms PRO instrument (MGSPRO). The Division was concerned that the MG Symptoms PRO was not a clinically meaningful endpoint. A Type C Guidance meeting discussed patient-focused MG Symptoms PRO (minutes dated November 3, 2020). The Division reiterated their comments and concerns regarding the MG Symptoms PRO from the end-of-phase 2 meeting. The Division noted that (b)(4) would be a matter of review at the time of the biologics license application (BLA) submission.
L1044: A chemistry, manufacturing, and controls (CMC)-only meeting discussed key (b)(4) facility design features and concepts as well as obtained relevant good manufacturing practice input (minutes dated December 10, 2020). A Type C Guidance meeting discussed the proposed statistical analysis plan for the phase 3 Study, MG0003 (minutes dated December 22, 2020). The Division advised the Applicant to describe the statistical methodology used to estimate the treatment effect in detail. At the Model Informed Drug Development meeting, discussions focused on the use of a model-informed approach to support a switch from weight-tiered doses to a fixed dose of (b)(4) mg of rozanolixizumab in patients with generalized MG (gMG). The objective of the switch was to simplify dosing and reduce potential for dosing errors (minutes dated March 26, 2021). A Type C Guidance meeting discussed the integrated statistical analysis plan and development of the integrated summary of efficacy and integrated summary of safety to support the rozanolixizumab BLA (minutes dated November 10, 2021). The Division stated that at least half of the safety database should consist of patients dosed with the highest anticipated dose and
L1045: frequency of dosing planned for marketing.
L1046: (b) (4)
L1047: the Division stated that they would consider these analyses to be exploratory. The Division stated that primary analysis of safety data should use the number of patients as the denominator.
L1048: In the pre-BLA meeting (minutes dated July 19, 2022), the Division recommended the Applicant request a CMC-only Type B Pre-BLA meeting to discuss the specific aspects of the CMC program. Additionally, the Division recommended that the Applicant include a statement of how they intend to meet the substantial evidence of effectiveness standard, provide subgroup analyses of the acetylcholine receptor antibody positive population and the muscle-specific kinase antibody positive population, and conduct the primary and secondary analyses on these two subgroups and include the analyses in their BLA submission.
L1049: Because rozanolixizumab for the treatment of gMG was granted orphan designation, the Applicant was exempted from the Pediatric Research Equity Act requirements. No written requests have been issued under Best Pharmaceuticals for Children Act.
L1050: BLA 761286 was submitted on October 24, 2022, and was filed on December 21, 2022, with no filing issues identified. This original BLA received priority review designation because of the potential benefit to the muscle-specific kinase antibody positive population; and was reviewed under “The Program.” The nonproprietary name suffix request was submitted on September 1, 2022. Rozanolixizumab was conditionally approved on March 27, 2023. The proprietary name review request was submitted on October 28, 2022, and found conditionally acceptable on January 13, 2023.
L1051: ## 13. Pharmacology Toxicology
L1052: ## 13.1. Summary Review of Studies Submitted
## With the Investigational New Drug
## Application
L1053: ## 13.1.1. Pharmacology (Primary and Secondary)
L1054: ## Pharmacology
L1055: UCB7665 is a humanized immunoglobulin G (IgG) monoclonal antibody intended to suppress FcRn-mediated IgG recycling. In vitro studies using plasmon surface resonance and transfected Madin-Darby canine kidney (MDCK) cells indicated picomolar affinity of UCB7665 for both human and monkey FcRn. In vivo studies in transgenic mice that express human FcRn (i.e., hFcRNTg) indicated increased clearance of previously administered human IgG following single intravenous (IV) doses of 0, 10, 30, and 100 mg/kg UCB7665. Additionally, circulating IgG levels were reduced in male and female cynomolgus monkeys in the general toxicity studies in which subcutaneous (SC) doses of up to 150 mg/kg UCB7665 were administered every 3 days for up to 26 weeks.
L1056: ## Secondary Pharmacology
L1057: The potential for UCB7665 to stimulate cytokine release was investigated in in vitro studies using whole blood, human peripheral blood mononuclear cells (PBMC), human umbilical vein endothelial cells (HUVEC), whole blood with HUVECs, and PBMCs with HUVECs.
L1058: Four assay designs were used that assessed soluble UCB7665, UCB7665 immobilized on plates, UCB7665 cross-linked with an anti-human Fc F(ab')2, and UCB7665 immobilized on beads. All assays, conducted using appropriate positive and negative controls, did not indicate the potential for stimulation of UCB7665-induced cytokine release.
L1059: ## Pharmacokinetics
L1060: Pharmacokinetic parameters were assessed following intravenous and SC administration of UCB7665 in the 4- and 13-week toxicology studies in cynomolgus monkeys. In both studies, anti-drug antibody (ADA) development in most animals was observed. The ADA response was generally correlated with reduced pharmacodynamic (PD) effect (i.e., a reduction in IgG levels) and reduced exposure in animals with higher ADA titers.
L1061: ## 13.1.2. Safety Pharmacology
L1062: No stand-alone safety pharmacology studies were conducted. There were no test article-related effects on cardiovascular, neurobehavioral, or respiratory (no plethysmography) safety pharmacology parameters that were incorporated into the 4- and 13-week general toxicity studies.
L1063: ## 13.1.3. Toxicology
L1064: ## 13.1.3.1. General Toxicology
L1065: General toxicity studies of 4-, 13- and 26-weeks' duration were conducted in cynomolgus monkey administered up to 150 mg/kg UCB7665 by SC injection every 3 days. There was no serious toxicity. Drug-related findings consisted of reductions in globulin levels due to reduced IgG levels, which are expected based on the pharmacology of the drug. Accompanying the dose-related reductions in IgG were decreases in total protein and increased A/G ratio. In addition, a trend toward slightly reduced albumin was observed. Full recovery was observed by the end of the 8-week recovery period. Immunophenotyping was conducted and no test article-related changes in immune cell subtypes were observed.
L1066: An assessment of T-cell-dependent antibody response was conducted during the dosing period in the 13-week study. Although there was no effect on immunoglobulin M response, administration of UCB7665 resulted in inhibition of the IgG response to keyhole limpet hemocyanin (KLH); recovery was not assessed. Neutralizing anti-drug antibodies (ADAs) were detected in all dosed groups and ADA was greater in groups that received UCB7665 by SC injection.
L1067: ## 13.1.3.2. Genetic Toxicology
L1068: No genetic toxicology studies were conducted.
L1069: ## 13.1.3.3. Carcinogenicity
L1070: Based on a weight of evidence assessment, conduct of carcinogenicity studies were deemed not necessary for UCB7665.
L1071: ## 13.1.3.4. Special Toxicology
L1072: ## Tissue Cross-Reactivity
L1073: Good Laboratory Practice-compliant tissue cross-reactivity studies were conducted in human and cynomolgus monkey tissues. The staining patterns were generally similar between species; however, the study report did not specify whether specific binding was membranous or cytoplasmic. Therefore, relevance of the binding in specific tissues for assessing safety is difficult to determine. However, the similarity of staining patterns between species supported use of the cynomolgus monkey for nonclinical testing.
L1074: ## 13.2. Individual Reviews of Studies Submitted With the New Drug Application
L1075: ## 13.2.1. Introduction
L1076: An enhanced pre- and postnatal development study in cynomolgus monkey was the only nonclinical study of UCB7665 submitted only to the NDA.
L1077: ## 13.2.2. Key Findings
L1078: Subcutaneous administration of 0, 50, or 150 mg/kg UCB7665 every 3 days to pregnant female cynomolgus monkeys from gestational day (GD) 20 through parturition caused embryonic losses in 3 of 19 females at both doses.
L1079: Throughout the postnatal period, infant body weights in females were reduced at both doses. The immune function in infants was impaired at both doses.
L1080: There were no test article effects on maturation, neurobehavioral endpoints, or postmortem findings in offspring from postnatal day (PND) 7 through PND 180.
L1081: ## 13.2.3. Enhanced Pre and Postnatal Development

Clinical chemistry deviations were mostly due to inadequate sample volume or clotted samples from infants. Necropsy deviations were largely of missing tissues, altered fixation timing.

L1106: ## Toxicokinetics (TK)
L1107: Blood samples for analyses of plasma concentrations or TK parameters were collected on GDs 20, 44, 59, 86, 110, and 143, and on LD 1, 7, and 28. TK parameters were assessed on GDs 44, 86, and 143. Increases in C_{max} and area under the curve (AUC) were greater than dose proportional and increased with repeat dosing.
L1108: Significant variability in plasma exposure was observed by GD 44, in which plasma concentration analysis in 11/16 laser Doppler flowmetry (LDF) indicated no detectable drug at 72 hours post dose. In contrast, a similar effect was only observed in 4/15 hemodiafiltration (HDF). Although the pharmacodynamic (PD) effect of UCB7665 (i.e., FcRn suppression) may have contributed to drug clearance, the higher variability in LDF was likely due to more extensive formation of neutralizing ADA relative to HDF.
L1109: In LDF and HDF, plasma concentrations on postpartum day (PPD) 1 were similar to those at GD 143 but were below the limit of quantification by PPD 7.

L1130: ## Pregnancy Outcomes
L1131: There was a clear test article-related effect on embryonic loss. Three LD and three HD losses were documented by ultrasonography on GD 30 – after the first three doses. The females were placed in the social phase by GD 32 and prior to the first TK sampling.
L1132: Early fetal loss in a fourth HDF was not considered to be a test-article related effect. Ultrasonography showed that the fetus was alive and within group range of fetal size on GD 30. On GD 44, after surgery and medication, an intrauterine death was documented. Aborted material was found in the cage 16 days later; the animal was moribund and euthanized.

L1150: ## Mortality
L1151: Early death at all doses was consistently associated with low birth weights (&lt;300 g) and/or early parturition. A cause of death was determined for 5 of 8 Control and 1 of 5 HD infants.
L1152: ## Controls
L1153: - Found dead PND 3. Death considered related to a patent *foramen ovale* observed on macroscopic examination of the heart.
- Rejected at birth by maternal female and sacrificed as moribund on PND 5 with acute pulmonary inflammation.
- Severe bite wounds from the maternal female: infant was sacrificed moribund on PND 17.
- Sacrificed as moribund on PND 1. Upon macroscopic evaluation of the fixed heart, a patent *foramen ovale* and a description (“2 tips at the bottom”) compatible with a double apex sign due to ventricular hypertrophy.
- Death was found to be the traumatic injury when caught in a fight between two maternal females
L1154: ## **HD**
L1155: - Found dead on PND 11. Very low birth weight (208 g) and failed to thrive. Based on macroscopic evidence of nasal skin erosions and scabs on the eyelids, the cause of death was determined to be related to trauma.
L1156: After macroscopic and microscopic examinations, a cause of death was not determined for the remaining infants.

L1166: ## Clinical Signs
L1167: No test article-related effects were observed.
L1168: ## *Birth Body Weights*
L1169: - Infants with a birth body weight of less than 300 g in the C groups were: 2 premature births (prior to GD 140) and 2 early births (GDs 145 and 148) and 2 born at term.
- Infants with low body weights in the LD were: one premature birth (GD 139) one early birth (GD 145) and one at term (GD 157).
- Infants with low body weights in the HD were: two early births (GDs 133 and 144) that were found dead, and one early birth (GD 145) that remained at a very low weight at PND 7 and died on PND 11.
- Body weights from PND 7: Body weight and weight gains of surviving infant males were similar among groups. Body weight gains of surviving dosed female infants were less than controls.

L1172: **Figure 16. Applicant's Figure (Study NCD3001): Female Infant Mean Body Weights**
