L1174: ## Physical Development
L1175: Size correlated with body weights, but there were no test article effects on body measurements, grip strength, skeletal development, heart rates, or ophthalmologic exams.
L1176: ## Hematology
L1177: - Elevated platelet counts and giant platelets were reported in 3 HD and 2 C infants. Differences in other parameters were sporadic and lacked correlation to dose.
L1178: ## Clinical Chemistry
L1179: - No test article-related effects were reported.
L1180: ## Immunotoxicology
L1181: ## Immunophenotyping
L1182: - No test article-related effects were seen in relative or absolute numbers of CD3+ T cells, CD3+CD8+ cytotoxic T cells, CD3+CD4+ T helper-cells, CD20+ B-cells, or CD16+ natural killer cells.
- No test article-related effects were seen in splenic B cells, naïve B cells, germinal center B cells, plasma blasts, or total CD3+ T-cells, nor in bone marrow plasma cells, plasma blasts, or CD-3+ T cells.
- T Cell-Dependent Antibody Response1 mL keyhole limpet hemocyanin (KLH) was administered SC on PND 119 and again on PND 147. Samples were collected and analyzed from pre-dose PND 119 and 147, and 7-, 14-, and 21-days post-inoculation (on PNDs 126, 133, 140, 154, 161, 168, and 175).
- IgM anti-KLH titers were similar among groups and peaked 7-14 days after the first and second immunizations. All three groups developed IgG responses, and the titers uniformly peaked on day 154 - after the second immunization. Although male group sizes were similar (6 CM, 5 MDM, and 7 HDM infants), female group sizes varied (4 CF, 7 MDF, and 3 HDF infants) and there was notable inter-animal sampling variability in all groups. IgG titers in HDM infants were lower than in CM and MDM infants. IgG titers were reduced in MDF and HDF compared to CF infants.
- Maternal ADA had no apparent effect on the infant immune responses.
L1183: ## Neurological Assessment
L1184: - Results of examinations in male and female infants on PND 1 and 7, to include postural tonus, hip and shoulder abduction, knee, finger, and toe flexion, righting reflex, progression while prone, eye tracking, lip smacking, suckling, "snout reflex," nystagmus, and Moro reflex, did not differ among groups. Grip strength, tested on PND 28, was similar among groups.
- ADA was detected in infants born to females that developed ADA. As seen in maternal females, the frequency and level of ADA was greater in infants in the mid dose group.
L1185: ## Toxicokinetics
L1186: - Drug was not detected in LD infants at any time. On day 1 at the HD, the mean infant concentration was 9.30 /ml; (compared to maternal levels of 584 ug/mL) and no drug was quantifiable by PND 7 and 28.
- Pathology findings in infants surviving through maturation
L1187: ## Necropsy
L1188: - On LD/PND 180 \pm 1. Mothers and infants were necropsied together. Intramuscular ketamine was followed by IV barbiturates and then exsanguination. After body weights were determined, and a full macroscopic examination, organs were weighed. Tissues were preserved, processed, and examined histologically as described for the maternal necropsy procedures (above).
- Organ weights: No test article-related effects on organ weights were reported.
- No macroscopic or microscopic test article-related effects were seen.
L1189: ## 14. Clinical Pharmacology
L1190: ## 14.1. In Vitro Studies
L1191: Rozanolixizumab is a human IgG4 monoclonal antibody to target and bind to FcRn receptor with increased affinity. It is neither subject to CYP450 metabolism nor expected to interfere with cytokine levels. Further, rozanolixizumab is not expected to be affected by transporters. Therefore, no in vitro transporter or CYP450-mediated drug interaction studies were conducted.
L1192: ## 14.2. In Vivo Studies
L1193: ## Study UP0018/UK: First-in-Human, Phase 1, Single-Ascending Dose Study in Healthy Volunteers
L1194: ## Study Design
L1195: UP0018 was a first-in-human, randomized, participant- and investigator-blind, placebo-controlled, single dose-escalating study conducted in healthy study subjects. Each study participant was randomized to 1 of 6 cohorts and received a single dose of placebo (n=2 per cohort) or rozanolixizumab at a dose of \approx 1 mg/kg, \approx 4 mg/kg, or \approx 7 mg/kg (n=6 per cohort) administered intravenously (IV) or SC as a 1-hour infusion. The study objectives were to assess safety, tolerability, PK, PD, and immunogenicity.
L1196: A total of 49 subjects were randomized to 6 dosing cohorts and were included in the full analysis set and the pharmacodynamic per-protocol set. Additionally, all 36 subjects who received rozanolixizumab were included in the pharmacokinetic per-protocol set.
L1197: The plasma concentration-time profiles for rozanolixizumab administered by the IV route showed similar profiles for the \approx 4 mg/kg and \approx 7 mg/kg doses, with nonlinear increases in rozanolixizumab plasma concentrations consistent with target-mediated disposition. Rozanolixizumab administered by the SC route was detectable only at the highest concentration of \approx 7 mg/kg. There was no effect of ADA on the plasma concentrations of rozanolixizumab.
L1198: **Figure 17. Geometric Mean (With 95% CIs) Rozanolixizumab Plasma Concentration-Time Profile: IV and SC Comparison**
L1199: Source: Clinical Study Report for UP0018

L1202: ## 14.3. Bioanalytical Method Validation and Performance
L1203: Quantification of rozanolixizumab in plasma was performed using electrochemiluminescence assay on the Meso Scale Discovery platform. For phase 1 Study UP0018 and phase 2 Study (MG0002), a sandwich format using an anti-idiotypic rozanolixizumab rabbit monoclonal antibody as capture and a goat antihuman Kappa light chain antibody for detection was used (PK-1). This method was redeveloped as a sandwich format using an anti-idiotypic rozanolixizumab rabbit monoclonal antibody as capture and for detection to increase assay sensitivity, dynamic range, adopt newly labeled reagents, and validate according to the latest guidance (PK-2) for phase 1 Study UP0060 and phase 3 Study MG0003.
L1204: The method validation parameters and performance characteristics are summarized as PK method 1 and PK method 2 in tables below.

L1216: ## 14.4. Immunogenicity Assessment—Impact of PK/PD, Efficacy, and Safety
L1217: ## Summary of Clinical Studies
L1218: Immunogenicity of rozanolixizumab was evaluated in two phase 1 Studies (UP0018 and UP0060, healthy subjects), four phase 2 Studies (MG0002, TP0001, CIDP01, and CIDP04), and three phase 3 Studies (MG0003, MG0004, and MG0007). Subjects completed Study MG0003 were eligible to continue receiving rozanolixizumab treatment in the open-label extension (OLE) Study MG0007. Studies MG0003 and MG0007 provided main data for IS analysis to assess the impact of immunogenicity on PK. Study MG0004 is not evaluated since approximately half of the study subjects had fewer than 18 weeks of exposure and rolled over to Study MG0007 upon site initiation.
L1219: The subsequent analysis presented in this section is considered exploratory because there are limitations due to analytical assay sensitivity.
L1220: Table 77 contains (1) the information for key individual studies assessing immunogenicity and (2) a high-level summary of study results for PK (trough concentration). Overall study design in terms of treatment duration, sampling time, and sample size were adequate to assess the ADA impact on PK.

L1233: ## **Methods for Evaluating the Effect of Immunogenicity on PK of Rozanolixizumab**
L1234: To evaluate the impact of immunogenicity on PK, we compared the observed concentrations in two groups (ADA+ and ADA-) in subjects with time-matched PK and ADA data using Immunogenicity Specimen (IS) tool developed in-house. At each timepoint, rozanolixizumab concentrations are summarized by ADA+ and ADA- groups. Summary of rozanolixizumab concentrations by ADA status (ADA+ or ADA-) are tabulated along with the geometric mean (GM) concentration by timepoint as well as the number of subjects. For the statistical comparisons of concentration data between ADA+ and ADA- groups, the geometric mean ratio (GMR) of ADA+/ADA- and the corresponding 90% CI are tabulated.
L1235: Data from MG0003 (completed study) and MG0007 (interim data) were analyzed and summarized by dose groups (≈7 mg/kg and ≈10 mg/kg) below.
L1236: ## **Effect of Immunogenicity on PK of Rozanolixizumab – Results**
L1237: Analysis results for Studies MG0003 and MG0007 indicate that the development of ADA in Study MG0007 is associated with a reduced systemic concentration of rozanolixizumab, and the effect of ADA is visible at several timepoint.
L1238: ## **Study MG0003 – (Subjects Received ≈7 mg/kg or ≈10 mg/kg Rozanolixizumab Dose)**
L1239: For the subjects in dose group received ≈7 mg/kg, the ADA+ group has a lower rozanolixizumab concentration than ADA- group at day 15, indicating that ADA has a negative effect on PK of rozanolixizumab. However, during the 43 days of treatment the GMR values were around 1 for the remaining timepoints and the 90% CI contained 1 at all timepoints. See Table 79, below for summary of rozanolixizumab concentrations (geometric mean values) in Study MG0003 in ADA+ and ADA- subjects during the treatment period (43 days).
L1240: For the subjects in dose group received ≈10 mg/kg, as shown in Table 80, the number of subjects in ADA+ groups are limited to accurately calculate the GMR and 90% CI except for on day 43 where the GMR was &gt;1 and the 90% CI contains 1.

L1262: ## **Effect of Immunogenicity on Efficacy/Safety of Rozanolixizumab**
L1263: As per the Applicant, there was no clinically meaningful impact of ADA (including neutralizing ADA) on efficacy (MG-ADL) (see Table 83 below for reference). Additionally, the Applicant noted that presence of higher ADA or NAb titer did not have a clear effect on individual changes from baseline in MG-ADL score (see Figure 19 and Figure 20 below for reference).
L1264: The reviewer analysis confirmed the Applicant's findings. The impact of ADA positivity on the total IgG profile was evaluated in the phase 3 Studies where occurrence of ADA on changes in MG-ADL score (for efficacy) or changes in pain, diarrhea, injection site reactions, and headache (for safety) were not significantly different.

L1274: ## 14.5. Pharmacometrics Assessment
L1275: ## 14.5.1. Applicant's Analysis
L1276: The population analyses were conducted using plasma concentrations of rozanolixizumab and total serum IgG concentrations in studies UP0106, UP0018, UP0060, TP0001, MG0002, MG0003, and MG0004; serum concentrations of anti-AChR and anti-MuSK from Studies MG0002, MG0003, and MG0004; and MG-ADL score data from MG0003. Interim data from MG0007 was used for external validation of the PK and total IgG models, and MG-ADL data from MG0002 for external validation of the MG-ADL model.
L1277: Data used in the popPK/PD analysis and a brief description of these studies is given in Table 84.
L1278: Model development was based on 898 rozanolixizumab plasma concentrations from 266 subjects (65 healthy volunteers, 154 gMG patients, and 47 ITP patients), 5,566 total IgG levels from 412 subjects (103 healthy volunteers, 243 gMG patients, and 66 ITP patients), 1,202 anti-AChR observations from 203 gMG patients, 116 anti-MuSK observations from 17 gMG patients, and 2,180 MG-ADL scores from 200 gMG patients.

L1297: ## Pharmacokinetic (PK)/Immunoglobulin G (IgG) Modeling
L1298: A nonlinear mixed effects modelling approach was used, using nonlinear mixed-effects modeling (NONMEM) V7.4.4. Parameter estimation was performed using the first-order conditional estimation with interaction method. The structural population pharmacokinetics (popPK) model consisted of a one-compartment model with target-mediated drug disposition (TMDD) using the quasi-equilibrium approximation. This implies two elimination pathways: a linear FcRn independent pathway representing typical proteolysis common to all immunoglobulins, and a nonlinear elimination pathway driven by binding to the FcRn target. The model was described by the set of equations (Equation 1):
L1299: ## Equation 1. Structural Population Pharmacokinetics Model, Rozanolixizumab
L1300: K_a = \frac{1}{MAT}K_e = \frac{CL/F}{V/F}\text{Complex} = \frac{(K_d \cdot V/F + TA + FcRn_{tot}) - \sqrt{(K_d \cdot V/F + TA + FcRn_{tot})^2 - (4 \cdot TA \cdot FcRn_{tot})}}{2}\frac{dA_1}{dt} = -K_a \cdot A_1\frac{dA_2}{dt} = K_a \cdot A_1 - K_e \cdot A_{2,free} - K_{deg} \cdot \text{Complex}
L1301: Source: FDA Reviewer.
L1302: where A1 is the total amount of rozanolixizumab in the depot compartment, K_a is the absorption rate constant following the SC administration of rozanolixizumab, MAT is the mean absorption time, A2 is the total amount of rozanolixizumab in the central compartment, FcRn independent CL/F is the FcRn independent apparent clearance, V/F is the apparent volume of distribution, K_e describes the elimination rate constant of the free drug in the central compartment A2, free which is FcRn independent, K_{deg} is the receptor degradation rate constant, assumed complex is degraded with the same rate based on this approximation, K_d is the dissociation constant, FcRn_{tot} is the estimated total FcRn, TA is total rozanolixizumab with consideration of surface bound FcRn. Interindividual variability (IIV) was evaluated on all parameters and was generally added in an exponential form. The starting residual unexplained variability (RUV) model was a combined error model.
L1303: An indirect-response model, where target binding increased the elimination rate of total IgG, was used to describe the turnover of IgG. Rozanolixizumab plasma concentrations and IgG serum concentrations were modelled simultaneously. The effect on IgG elimination was assumed to be related to the degree of target occupancy (Equation 2).
L1304: ## **Equation 2. Indirect Response Model, Rozanolixizumab**
L1305: \frac{dIgG}{dt} = K_{in} - IgG \cdot K_{out} \cdot (1 + ER_{ij})K_{in} = K_{out} \cdot \text{Baseline IgG}FcRn_{bound} = \frac{\text{Complex}}{FcRn_{tot} \cdot V/F}ER_{ij} = E_{max} \cdot FcRn_{bound}
L1306: Source: FDA Reviewer.
L1307: where K_{in} is the zero-order rate input, K_{out} is the first-order elimination rate constant, ER_{ij} is the rozanolixizumab effect as fractional increase in K_{out}, for subject i, at time point j, E_{max} is the maximum effect (i.e., maximum fractional increase in K_{out}), FcRn_{tot} is the estimated total FcRn, FcRn_{bound} is the estimated bound FcRn as a fraction from 0 to 1. All model parameters were estimated. IIV was evaluated on all parameters and was generally added in an exponential form. The starting RUV model was a proportional error model.
L1308: The starting point for the covariate model building was the base PK-IgG model. The covariate (study population, age, body weight, sex, race, baseline steroid usage, estimated glomerular filtration rate (eGFR), alanine aminotransferase (ALT), aspartate aminotransferase (AST), alkaline phosphatase (ALP), bilirubin, antidrug antibodies, neutralizing antibodies (NAb)-parameter (FcRn independent CL/F, V/F, MAT, baseline IgG, K_{out}, and E_{max}) relationships were evaluated for inclusion in the model. Structural covariates were tested by full fixed effects model (FFEM) where all the prespecified covariate-parameter relationships were included in the model in one step, followed by a backward elimination step of parameters with high relative standard error (relative standard error &gt;70%). Exploratory covariates were tested by the stepwise covariate model building procedure with adaptive scope reduction.
L1309: ## **PK/PD MG-ADL Modeling**
L1310: The modeling approach was guided by the graphical analysis and consisted of a placebo effect model and a drug effect model. The rozanolixizumab effect on MG-ADL score was linked to IgG inhibition.
L1311: The covariate modeling for the PK/PD model for MG-ADL score followed the same principles as for the PK-IgG model. Exploratory covariates (baseline IgG concentration, age, sex, region, stratification factor (MuSK+ or AChR+), baseline steroid usage, duration of disease at baseline, thymectomy at baseline, and Myasthenia Gravis Foundation of America disease class at baseline) were evaluated for inclusion in the model. Exploratory covariates were only considered for parameters associated with IIV terms. The discrimination between models was mainly based on the inspection of graphical diagnostics and changes in the objective function value.
L1312: Covariates that had a statistically significant impact on the PK-IgG model parameters were body weight on FcRn independent CL/F, V/F, and baseline IgG, presence of antidrug antibodies /NAb on FcRn independent CL/F, formulation on MAT, ITP patient on V/F, and baseline steroid use on baseline IgG and E_{max}. The final parameter estimates are shown in Table 87.

L1326: The main findings of the covariate analysis were:
L1327: - Body weight (range 37.7 to 156 kg) had a statistically significant impact on the PK-total IgG model parameters of FcRn independent CL/F, V/F, and baseline total IgG. The presence of NAb had a significant impact on FcRn independent CL/F, (b) (4) and baseline steroid use had a significant impact on Baseline total IgG and maximum effect (E_{max}). (b) (4), and baseline steroid use had a significant impact on baseline total IgG and maximum effect (E_{max}).
- The estimated glomerular filtration rate (range 38.2 to 161mL/min/1.73m2) and hepatic function tests, including alanine aminotransferase (range 3.00 to 158U/L), aspartate aminotransferase (range 9.00 to 127U/L), alkaline phosphatase (range 22.0 to 386U/L), and bilirubin (range 3.50 to 37.2U/L), did not exhibit any statistically significant impact on rozanolixizumab CL/F. There are no statistically significant differences in CL/F or V/F between healthy study subjects and gMG patients. Additionally, no statistically significant impact on FcRn independent CL/F, V/F, baseline total IgG, E_{max}, or total IgG half-life was found with sex (57.5% female), race (Asian versus non-Asian), or age (range 18 to 89 years). Additionally, no statistically significant impact on FcRn independent CL/F, V/F, baseline total IgG, E_{max}, or total IgG half-life was found with sex (57.5% female), race (Asian versus non-Asian), or age (range 18 to 89 years).

L1355: The Applicant used the final PK/PD model to compare the performance of the phase 3 weight-tiered dosing regimen and a simple 2-fixed dosing regimen in terms of exposure, total IgG reduction, disease-specific autoantibody reduction, and MG-ADL response. Additionally, simulations were conducted to assess the treatment strategy in case of a missed or delayed dose by analyzing the PK and total IgG profiles.
L1356: Simulations were performed using PK-total IgG and AChR mg-ADL models to predict outcomes of 2-fixed dosing and compare them with the phase 3 weight-tiered dosing regimen. These simulations were carried out for an MG population by sampling subject characteristics from MG0003 or for specific body weight groups. Two dosing regimens were compared after a 6-week cycle: a 2-fixed dose regimen and the weight-tiered regimen studied in the phase 3 gMG program. Six weekly doses were simulated for each regimen (Table 36 in Section II.6.3.3).
L1357: The 2-fixed dose regimen resulted in predicted rozanolixizumab C_{max,ss} almost equivalent to the weight-tiered dose regimen of \approx 7 mg/kg or \approx 10 mg/kg for the overall MG population. However, for subjects with a body weight of 100 kg or more, the simulated exposure with the 2-fixed dose was significantly lower compared to the lowest studied dose of 7 mg/kg.
L1358: The distribution of C_{max,ss} with the \approx 7 mg/kg, \approx 10 mg/kg and 2-fixed dose regimens was compared by weight tier to investigate the influence of extreme body weights on exposure (Figure 27A and Figure 28A).
L1359: According to the Applicant, the simulation results suggested that the weekly doses of rozanolixizumab approximating 7 mg/kg, 10 mg/kg and 2-fixed dose regimens for 6 consecutive weeks were indicative of potentially achieving mean maximum total IgG reductions of \ge 70\% (Figure 27B and Figure 28B).
L1360: To compare the simulated PD between the 2-fixed dosing and weight-tiered dosing, minimum anti-AChR concentration (nadir), maximum percentage change from baseline anti-AChR, and AUEC (area under the curve) for percentage change from baseline anti-AChR were assessed for the overall MG population and stratified by weight tier.
L1361: According to the Applicant, the simulations demonstrated that in the overall MG population (Figure 27C, Figure 28C, and Table 89), the 2-fixed dose regimen achieved a median maximum anti-AChR reduction from baseline of 67.0%. This reduction was comparable to that achieved with the \approx 7 mg/kg weight-tiered dosing (67.2%) and slightly lower than the \approx 10 mg/kg weight-tiered dose (70.3%). The maximum percentage change from baseline anti-AChR remained consistent across all regimens, as confirmed by simulations based on anti-AChR nadir and AUEC. Among patients weighing \ge 100 kg, the 2-fixed dose regimen achieved the smallest median maximum percentage anti-AChR reduction of 62.8%.
L1362: To compare the efficacy between 2-fixed dosing and weight-tiered dosing, the changes from baseline in MG-ADL score on day 43 were simulated for both regimens using the total IgG-MG-ADL and AChR-MG-ADL models. Both models yielded similar predictions for MG-ADL response.
L1363: Based on the Applicant's simulation, in the overall MG population, the 2-fixed dose regimen achieved a mean predicted MG-ADL score change from baseline of -3.31, which was the same as that achieved with the \approx 7 mg/kg weight-tiered dosing (-3.31) and slightly lower than that for the \approx 10 mg/kg weight-tiered dose (-3.47) (Figure 27D, Figure 28D and Table 89). Across all weight tiers, all dose regimens were predicted to achieve a mean change from baseline MG-ADL score of at least -3.08, and distribution of the MG-ADL change from baseline values largely overlapped in all regimens (Table 89).

L1385: ## **Simulations for Missed or Delayed Dose**
L1386: The Applicant conducted simulations using the PK-total IgG model to predict the effect of delayed or missed doses on rozanolixizumab plasma concentration profile and total IgG response. The reference dosing regimen was weekly dosing of 560 mg rozanolixizumab for 6 weeks. Scenarios where 1 dose was delayed by 1 to 4 days or skipped were included in the
L1387: simulations, with all other doses administered on the planned days, resulting in a shorter interval between consecutive doses the longer the delay.
L1388: Figure 29 and Figure 30 illustrate the effect of a missed dose, or a delay of 1 to 4 days for the second, fourth, and sixth dose on rozanolixizumab concentrations and total IgG levels.
L1389: **Figure 29. Simulations of Missed or Delayed Dose Scenarios for Rozanolixizumab Plasma Concentration Versus Time Following 6 Weekly Doses of 560 mg Rozanolixizumab**
L1390: Source: The Applicant's popPK report CL0534 report addendum, Page 22, Figure 10
L1391: Note: Stratification by the number of delayed days is done on the X-axis and by the dose number affected by the delay on the Y-axis. The reference scenario (administering all doses as scheduled) is colored in blue and scenarios exploring missed or delayed doses are in red in each stratification. The dose following a delayed dose is taken as originally planned, making the time shorter between the delayed dose and the next. Solid lines represent medians and shaded areas represent the 95th prediction intervals.
L1392: Figure 30. Simulations of Missed or Delayed Dose Scenarios for Percent Change from Baseline In Total IgG Versus Time Following 6 Weekly Doses of 560 mg Rozanolixizumab
L1393: Source: The Applicant's popPK report CL0534 report addendum, Page 23, Figure 11
Note: Stratification by the number of delayed days is done on the X-axis and by the dose number affected by the delay on the Y-axis. The reference scenario (administering all doses as scheduled) is colored in blue and scenarios exploring missed or delayed doses are in red in each stratification. The dose following a delayed dose is taken as originally planned, making the time shorter between the delayed dose and the next. Abbreviations: IgG, immunoglobulin G
L1394: The simulation results indicate that a delay of 1 to 4 days in dosing would lead to an incremental increase in peak plasma concentrations compared to scheduled dosing at week 2 or week 4.
L1395: The highest predicted median C_{max} was observed for the 4-day delayed dose at 39 mg/L, while scheduled dosing resulted in a median C_{max} of 17 mg/L. However, in all delayed dose scenarios, the C_{max} values did not surpass the median C_{max} attained with the weight-tiered dosing of \approx10 mg/kg (39 mg/L).
L1396: Delaying dosing up to 4 days resulted in temporary, smaller reductions in total IgG compared to scheduled dosing, but the reduction recovered after 2 additional scheduled doses. The simulation predicted that skipping a dose would have a greater impact on IgG reduction than the delayed-dose scenarios.
L1397: **Reviewer's Comments:** The reviewer was able to replicate the Applicant's findings by executing their final PK/PD model. However, since a substantial proportion of rozanolixizumab plasma concentrations fall below the LLOQ, the impact of excluding these samples on the performance of the PK/PD model remains uncertain. It was observed that at the doses of \approx 1 mg/kg, \approx 4 mg/kg, \approx 7 mg/kg, and \approx 10 mg/kg, 100%, 90%, 71%, and 53% of rozanolixizumab plasma concentrations, respectively, fell below the lower limit of quantification (LLOQ) (Table 84).
L1398: The exclusion of these samples may result in a notable degree of shrinkage towards typical model predictions for the predicted rozanolixizumab concentrations used in driving the PK/PD relationship, especially when modeling PK alone. This effect becomes particularly pronounced in studies involving low doses in MG patients. Additionally, the estimation of TMDD-related parameters may also be affected by this nonrandom censoring. As a result, we lack confidence in the model's predictive ability.
L1399: According to the Applicant's proposed draft label, subjects weighing \ge 100 kg would receive a fixed dose of (b)^{(4)} mg, (b)^{(4)} compared to the \approx 7 mg/kg and \approx 10 mg/kg doses administered in efficacy studies. Although the Applicant concluded that these dose differences would not translate into clinically relevant differences in total IgG levels and the MG-ADL clinical endpoint, it remains uncertain whether the Applicant's PK/PD model possesses sufficient sensitivity to accurately predict changes in total IgG and MG-ADL at lower exposures. Furthermore, anti-AChR Ab data at \approx 1 mg/kg is not available, since this dose was administered only in healthy volunteers. For subcutaneous administrations with \approx 1 mg/kg, all PK samples were below limit of quantification and with \approx 4 mg/kg, majority of PK samples were below LLOQ. Consequently, considering that subjects weighing over 100 kg comprise 23% of the phase 3 study population, we have concerns regarding the potential issue of underdosing. For subjects weighing over 100 kg, the efficacy of rozanolixizumab at the proposed fixed dose regimen of (b)^{(4)} mg cannot be reliably predicted. Therefore, we recommend a dose of 840 mg for subjects weighing over 100 kg, as assessed in the MG0003 study.

L1453: ## 14.6. Pharmacogenetics

L1455: ## 15. Study Design, MG0004 and MG0007
L1456: ## Design, Study MG0004
L1457: Study MG0004 was a 52-week, open-label extension (OLE) study of MG0003. This study was replaced by MG0007.
L1458: ## Objectives
L1459: ## Primary Objective, Study MG0004
L1460: To evaluate the long-term safety and tolerability of rozanolixizumab in subjects with gMG who have moderate to severe symptoms and are seropositive for anti-AChR or anti-MuSK antibodies.
L1461: ## Primary Endpoints, Study MG0004
L1462: - Occurrence of treatment-emergent adverse event (TEAEs)
- Occurrence of TEAEs leading to permanent withdrawal of study drug
- Other safety endpoints included:Adverse events of special monitoring (severe headache, severe gastrointestinal (GI) disorders and opportunistic infections)
- Vital signs values and change from baseline
- 12-lead electrocardiogram values and change from baseline
- Clinical laboratory parameters (hematology, biochemistry, and urinalysis).
L1463: ## Secondary Objective, Study MG0004
L1464: To evaluate the long-term efficacy of rozanolixizumab in subjects with gMG who have moderate to severe symptoms and are seropositive for anti-AChR or anti-MuSK antibodies
L1465: ## Secondary Endpoints, Study MG0004
L1466: - Change from baseline at each scheduled assessment during treatment and observation periods in MG-ADL, myasthenia gravis composite (MG-C) score, quantitative myasthenia gravis (QMG), and use of rescue medication.
L1467: ## Eligibility Criteria
L1468: - Subjects must have completed the observation period in Study MG0003
- Subjects who discontinued study drug in MG0003 for reasons other than requiring rescue medication were not eligible for Study MG0004.
- Subjects who required IVIg or PEX rescue medication in the MG0003 observation period were not eligible for Study MG0004.
L1469: ## Basic Study Design, Study MG0004
L1470: Study MG0004 was a phase 3, multicenter, randomized, open-label extension (OLE) study with a weekly rozanolixizumab SC dosing regimen for 52 weeks either on site or at home followed by an 8-week observation period in adult subjects with gMG who completed the observation period in Study MG0003. Subjects were randomized 1:1 to receive rozanolixizumab either \approx7 mg/kg or \approx10 mg/kg dose based on body weight-tier similar to that in Study MG0003 (see Section 6.1).
L1471: Transition between Study MG0003 and Study MG0004: the final visit (visit 14) in Study MG0003 served as the first visit in MG0004.
L1472: Study MG0004 was replaced by MG0007, an OLE study with 6-week treatment cycles based on MG worsening. Subjects who completed a minimum of 6-week treatment in Study MG0004 were eligible to enroll in MG0007. An IDMC monitored the ongoing safety and efficacy of the Study.
L1473: Figure 37. Study Schematic
L1474: Source: MG0004 SAP September 23, 2021
L1475: ## Design, Study MG0007
L1476: Study MG0007 is an ongoing open label extension study to MG0003 and consists of repeated treatment cycles demanded by gMG worsening as defined in the protocol.
L1477: ## Objectives
L1478: ## Primary Objective, Study MG0007
L1479: To evaluate the safety and tolerability of 6-week treatment cycles with SC rozanolixizumab in subjects with gMG who have moderate to severe symptoms and are seropositive for anti-AChR or anti-MuSK antibodies
L1480: ## Primary Endpoints, Study MG0007
L1481: - Occurrence of TEAEs
- Occurrence of TEAEs leading to permanent withdrawal of study drug
L1482: BLA 761286
Rystiggo (rozanolixizumab)
L1483: ## **Other Safety Endpoints, Study MG0007**
L1484: - Occurrence of serious TEAEs
- Occurrence of TEAEs of special monitoring
- Vital signs values and change from baseline (blood pressure and pulse)
- 12-lead electrocardiogram values and change from baseline
- Laboratory parameters and change from baseline (hematology, clinical chemistry, and urinalysis).
L1485: ## **Secondary Objective, Study MG0007**
L1486: To evaluate the efficacy of 6-week treatment cycles with SC rozanolixizumab in subjects with gMG who have moderate to severe symptoms and are seropositive for anti-AChR or anti-MuSK antibodies
L1487: ## **Secondary Endpoints, Study MG0007**
L1488: - For each of the first three 6-week treatment cycles, change from baseline to day 43 in one treatment cycle in MG-ADL, MG-C score, and QMG
- Time between consecutive treatment cycles
L1489: ## **Eligibility Criteria**
L1490: ## **Key Inclusion Criteria**
L1491: - Adults aged \ge 18 years with gMG who are anti-AChR or anti-MuSK antibody positive
- Must meet one of the following criteria:Completed the observation period in Study MG0003
- Required but not received IVIg/PEX during the observation period of MG0003
- completed at least 6 visits in MG0004
- Body weight \ge 35 kg at baseline
- Subjects who discontinued study drug in MG0003 for reasons other than requiring rescue medication were not eligible for Study MG0004.
- Subjects who required IVIg or PEX rescue medication in the MG0003 observation period were not eligible for Study MG0004.
L1492: ## **Basic Study Design, MG0007**
L1493: Study MG0007 is an ongoing phase 3, multicenter, randomized, open-label extension (OLE) study of MG0003 to evaluate 6-week treatment cycles of rozanolixizumab in subjects with gMG. An IDMC monitored the ongoing safety and efficacy of the study.
L1494: ## BLA 761286
## Rystiggo (rozanolixizumab)
L1495: Subjects were randomized 1:1 to receive rozanolixizumab initial 6-week treatment cycle of either \approx 7 mg/kg or \approx 10 mg/kg dose based on body weight-tier similar to that in Study MG0003 followed by 16-weeks observation period (see Section 6.1, Figure 38).
L1496: Subjects are evaluated every 4 weeks. Subsequent 6-week treatment cycles are triggered in case of MG symptom worsening defined as an increase of 2 points on MG-ADL or 3 points on QMG scale between assessments, based on investigator's assessments.
L1497: The minimum time to start the next treatment cycle is 4 weeks following the last dose of the treatment period of the previous cycle. If a study participant requires treatment earlier than 4 weeks, IgG levels from the previous cycle are considered and discussed with the medical monitor. A new treatment cycle cannot be initiated until total IgG level is \ge 2 g/L.
L1498: **Figure 38. Study Design, MG0007**
L1499: Source: Protocol Figure 1-2.
Abbreviations: MG, myasthenia gravis
L1500: ## Transition Between Study MG0003 and Study MG0007
L1501: The rollover must be within 4 weeks after the end of study visit in MG0003 (Figure 39).
L1502: ## Transition Between Study MG0004 and MG0007
L1503: The rollover must be within 4 weeks after the premature end of treatment (at least six SC rozanolixizumab weekly dosing) or the end of study visit in MG0004 (Figure 39).
L1504: In case of more than four-week time interval from Study MG0003 or MG0004, a screening period of up to 4 weeks was applicable to confirm the eligibility for Study MG0007.

Of these, 88 subjects were assigned to rozanolixizumab \approx7 mg/kg group, and 77 subjects were assigned to rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg group.

L1515: ## Dose Modifications, Study MG0007
L1516: The dose adjustments to \approx 7 mg/kg or \approx 10 mg/kg were permitted at the beginning of each treatment cycle as per the investigator's discretion. Recommended dose modifications from \approx 10 mg/kg to \approx 7 mg/kg equivalent due to drug-related AEs included:
L1517: - Moderate to severe headaches
- Moderate to severe gastrointestinal disturbances
- Moderate to severe toxicities (\gegrade 2 common terminology criteria for adverse events (CTCAE v5.0))
- Recurrent hypogammaglobulinaemia with a serum total IgG level of &lt;2 g/L

L1531: ## 17. Clinical Safety
L1532: ## 17.1. Safety Data Pool S1, Studies MG0003, MG0004, MG0007
L1533: Safety Pool S1 consists of all rozanolixizumab-treated subjects who have undergone at least one treatment cycle, defined as receiving at least one infusion of rozanolixizumab in any 6-week treatment period (See Section 7.4).
L1534: For Safety Pool S1, the treatment-emergent adverse events were defined as:
L1535: - Study MG0003: Adverse event starting on or after the time of first rozanolixizumab administration up to and including 3 weeks (56 days) after the last dose in the treatment cycle.
- Study MG0004 (first 6 weeks of all subjects): Adverse event that was not present before the first administration of rozanolixizumab or any unresolved event already present before the first administration of rozanolixizumab that worsened in intensity following exposure to treatment, up to and including 8 weeks after the end of the Treatment Period or 8 weeks after the last rozanolixizumab administration in study subjects who discontinued the study or rozanolixizumab.
- Study MG0007: Adverse event starting on or after the time of first rozanolixizumab administration up to and including 8 weeks (56 days) after the last dose of each treatment cycle
L1536: ## 17.1.1. Subject Disposition, Safety Pool S1
L1537: A total of 196 subjects were included in Safety Pool S1 and based on the maximum dose received in the first treatment cycle, 95 and 101 subjects were included in rozanolixizumab \approx7 mg/kg and \approx10 mg/kg dose groups, respectively. Out of 196 subjects in Safety Pool S1, 59 (30.1%) were permanently discontinued (Table 94). The most common reason for treatment discontinuation was occurrence of TEAEs in both rozanolixizumab dose groups. Another common reason for treatment discontinuation was withdrawal by subjects in the \approx10 mg/kg dose group.

L1542: ## 17.1.2. Baseline Characteristics, Safety Pool S1, Study MG0003, MG0004, and MG0007
L1543: Demographics for the pivotal placebo-controlled Study MG0003 are described in Section 6.2.2.4, based on the submitted data. Study MG0003 enrolled 200 subjects, almost all during the COVID-19 pandemic (n=183 [91.5%]; the pandemic start date March 20, 2020) and the rest (n=17 [8.5%]) during the pre-COVID-19 pandemic period. All 71 subjects in MG0004 entered from Study MG0003 and most (n=63 [88.7%]) subjects were enrolled during the COVID-19 pandemic. All 165 subjects including 105 subjects from Study MG0003 and 60 subjects from Study MG0004, entered Study MG0007 during the COVID-19 pandemic. The demographics for the Safety Pool S1 from these phase 3 studies are described in Table 95. One hundred seventeen (59.7%) were female. Subjects 65 years and older comprised 24.5% and subjects 75 years and older comprised 8.2% of total subjects. Subjects in the &lt;50 kg body weight category at baseline were in low numbers (6.1%). Subjects with seropositivity for anti-AChR autoantibody and anti-MuSK autoantibody comprised 82.7% and 7.7%, respectively of total subjects when tested at baseline visit. A majority of subjects were Myasthenia Gravis Foundation of America (MGFA) disease Class IIIa followed by Class IIb and IIIb at baseline.

L1549: **Reviewer's Comments:** Overall, the baseline characteristics of subjects in the Safety Pool S1 is consistent with the demographics of subjects in Study MG0003. The sex imbalance favoring females is consistent with the natural history of the disease, as described in Section 2. The relative proportion of anti-AChR-gMG and anti-MuSK-gMG in the Safety Pool S1 is consistent with the disease prevalence in the intended patient population (Gilhus et al. 2019). There are fewer subjects in the &lt;50 kg body weight category similar to that in Study MG0003 and this may potentially impact the labeling for dosing in this particular group.
L1550: ## Impact of the COVID-19 Pandemic on Safety Pool S1, Studies MG0003, MG0004, MG0007
L1551: The impact of the COVID-19 pandemic was minor and affected a relatively small number of the subjects. A majority of the subjects (91.5% in MG0003, 88.7% in MG0004 and 100% in MG0007) enrolled were randomized during the COVID-19 pandemic (the first laboratory-confirmed case of COVID-19 in the United States was January 20, 2020; the WHO declared a pandemic on March 11, 2020). The pandemic impact on Study MG0003 is described in Section 6.2.2.4.1. There were no protocol deviations related to COVID-19 in Study MG0004. The pandemic impacted six (3.6%) subjects and a total of 11 visits including 8 visits due to confirmed COVID-19 infection (all visits not done) in Study MG0007.
L1552: ## Protocol Deviations, Safety Pool S1, Studies MG0003, MG0004, MG0007
L1553: No subjects were excluded from any analysis due to protocol deviation. Protocol deviations for MG0003 are described in Section 6.2.2.4.1. A total of 18 (10.9%) subjects had an important protocol deviation in Study MG0007 including, exclusion criteria deviation (n=10 [6.1%]), procedural noncompliance (n=3 [1.8%]), withdrawal criteria deviation and treatment noncompliance (n=2 [1.2%] each), and incorrect treatment dose (n=1 [0.6%]). A total of five (7%) subjects had an important protocol deviation due to procedural noncompliance in Study MG0004.
L1554: **Reviewer's Comments:** The protocol deviations affected 11% and 7% of the randomized subjects in Studies MG0007 and MG0004, respectively and none were excluded from any analysis. They were unlikely to have had an impact on the efficacy and safety analyses.
L1555: ## 17.2. Overview of Treatment-Emergent

## Studies MG0003, MG0004, MG0007
L1556: Table 96 shows a summary of treatment-emergent adverse events (TEAEs) in 196 subjects pooled in Safety Pool S1 from the Studies MG0003, MG0004, and MG0007. Of 196 subjects, 182 (92.9%) subjects experienced a TEAE with rozanolixizumab treatment. A majority (64.3%) of subjects had mild to moderately severe TEAEs. Serious adverse events occurred in 21.9% of the subjects. Rozanolixizumab was permanently discontinued in 14.8% of subjects due to TEAEs.

L1564: ## 17.3. Deaths in the Subjects Treated With
## Rozanolixizumab, Safety Population,
## Safety Pool S1, Studies MG0003, MG0004,
## and MG0007
L1565: A total of five deaths were reported in the OLE Study MG0007 (Table 97). There were no new deaths reported in 120-day safety update dated February 3, 2023. Three deaths occurred in the setting of infection. Two deaths were attributed to severe acute respiratory syndrome coronavirus 2 (SARS-CoV2) pneumonia in unvaccinated individuals. One case of multilobar pneumonia in which no pathogen was identified. A role for rozanolixizumab in the TEAEs of COVID-19 and pneumonia cannot be ruled out. The reviewer notes that pneumonia is the most common of all infections in gMG patients with gMG and occurs due to bulbar and swallowing muscle weakness and immunocompromised status (Prior et al. 2018; Kassardjian et al. 2020). Two deaths attributed to cardiac events occurred during follow-up period and before treatment in Study MG0007. Given the time to onset and the presence of medical comorbidities, these events were considered unrelated to rozanolixizumab.
L1566: The mortality rate observed in the Safety Pool S1 from Studies MG0003, MG0004, and MG0007 was 25.9 deaths per 1000 (5 deaths/193.05 person-years) and is similar to the mortality rate observed in pooled safety population for efgartigimod (30.5 deaths per 1000 subjects; Integrated Review.BLA761195.December 17, 2021).

L1574: ## 17.4. Serious Adverse Events in the Subjects Treated With Rozanolixizumab, Safety Population, Safety Pool S1, Studies MG0003, MG0004, and MG0007
L1575: In Safety Pool S1, a total of 43 (21.9%) subjects experienced serious adverse events (SAEs) on rozanolixizumab treatment (Table 98). As occurred in the double-blind, placebo-controlled treatment period, the most common SAE was MG worsening and crisis (n=16 [8.1%]). Other SAEs with frequency &gt;2 subjects included infections (4.1%) including COVID-19 (2%) and aseptic meningitis (0.5%), gastrointestinal disorders (2.6%), and neoplasms (1.5%).
L1576: ## MG Crisis
L1577: Table 99 summarizes four subjects who experienced MG crisis on rozanolixizumab treatment in Study MG0007. All subjects were receiving rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg dose treatment cycles. Three subjects were withdrawn from the study due to MG crisis and the use of rescue therapy. The precipitating event for MG crisis was not known in these subjects. In the fourth subject food impaction in esophagus resulted in severe dysphagia and MG crisis which resolved after the food bolus was removed endoscopically and the subject continued rozanolixizumab treatment according to the study design. None of the subjects in Studies MG0003 and MG0004 experienced MG crisis while receiving rozanolixizumab.
L1578: ## Worsening of Myasthenia Gravis
L1579: Table 92 summarizes 12 subjects who experienced MG worsening on rozanolixizumab treatment in Safety Pool S1. Three out of 12 subjects experienced MG symptom worsening during the double-blind, placebo-controlled treatment in Study MG0003 and are described in Section 7.6.2.3. The other nine subjects experienced MG worsening and their symptoms resolved during Study MG0007. Six out of nine subjects were withdrawn from Study MG0007 including five subjects due to MG worsening and one subject due to positive interferon gamma release assay (IGRA) on study entry. Five out of nine subjects required rescue therapy (IVIg or PEX). Two subjects had multiple episodes of MG worsening during Study MG0007. The triggering factors for MG worsening determined as per the investigator were SARS-CoV-2 vaccine (n=1), missing of doses due to exposure to COVID-19 positive person (n=1), and hypogammaglobulinemia (n=1).
L1580: ## Headache
L1581: Subject ID MG0003/ [REDACTED] (b) (6) experienced serious headache after the second rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg infusion in Study MG0003. The SAE is described in Section 7.6.2.3. The event was likely related to rozanolixizumab.
L1582: ## Seizure
L1583: The SAE occurred in subject MG0003/ [REDACTED] (b) (6) who was randomized to rozanolixizumab \approx7 mg/kg treatment group during Study MG0003. The event is described in Section 7.6.2.3.
L1584: ## Transient Global Amnesia
L1585: Subject MG0007/ [REDACTED] (b) (6) experienced the SAE 261 days after the first and seventh day after the most recent rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg infusions in MG0007. At the time of the SAE, the subject had received five rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg infusions in Study MG0003 followed by 23 rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg infusions in Study MG0007. The SAE symptoms included dizziness, tinnitus, and disorientation in time and space. The brain imaging and electroencephalogram were without any fresh ischemic changes and seizure activity, respectively. The study drug dose was not changed, and the event was considered resolved 7 days after the symptom onset. The subject had multiple risk factors such as cardiac arrhythmia, myocardial infarction, hypertension, and diabetes. The transient global amnesia was considered severe. The event was unlikely related to rozanolixizumab.
L1586: ## COVID-19 Infection
L1587: Four subjects experienced the SAE of COVID-19 infection while receiving rozanolixizumab in Study MG0007 and a role of rozanolixizumab in the TEAE cannot be ruled out.
L1588: - Subject MG0007/ [REDACTED] (b) (6) died due to COVID-19 infection after receiving the first infusion of rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg dose in Study MG0007. The subject was not vaccinated for SARS-CoV2. The subject had completed the 6-week treatment cycle of rozanolixizumab in Study MG0003.
- Subject MG0007/ [REDACTED] (b) (6) died due to COVID-19 pneumonia and respiratory failure after receiving 16 rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg doses in Study MG0007. The subject was not vaccinated for SARS-CoV2.
- Subject MG0007/ [REDACTED] (b) (6) was hospitalized for 3 days due to COVID-19 infection 37 days after the most recent rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg infusion in Study MG0007. The dosing regimen for rozanolixizumab was unchanged. The subject had completed the 6-week treatment cycle of rozanolixizumab in Study MG0003.
- Subject MG0007/ [REDACTED] (b) (6) was hospitalized for worsening COVID-19 infection after the first dose of rozanolixizumab \approx7 mg/kg in Study MG0007. The SAE resolved in 8 days after onset. This subject received placebo in Study MG0003. The study drug was interrupted due to COVID-19 infection. The subject continued in the study.
L1589: ## Other Serious Infections
L1590: - Subject MG0007/ [REDACTED] (b) (6) experienced SAE of appendicitis complicated by cholecystitis, peritonitis, and liver abscess 38 days after the 18th infusion of rozanolixizumab \approx7 mg/kg in Study MG0007. The subject was withdrawn from the study due to infection requiring hospitalization.
- Subject MG0007/ [REDACTED] (b) (6) experienced SAE of E. coli urosepsis 61 days after the first and 26 days after the most recent rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg infusion in Study MG0007. The subject was withdrawn from the study due to infection requiring hospitalization. The infection resolved in 9 days after onset.
L1591: ## Aseptic Meningitis
L1592: Subject MG0007/ [REDACTED] (b) (6) experienced an SAE of aseptic meningitis 36 hours after the first rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg infusion in Study MG0007. The subject received placebo in Study MG0003. The subject reported severe headache with a numerical rating scale 9/10, stiff neck, diarrhea, nausea, and vomiting associated with fevers 38.4°C. In the emergency room, she received ceftriaxone, acyclovir, and dexamethasone for a presumed diagnosis of meningitis. CT head was reported as normal. A lumbar puncture showed 509 cells/\muL (normal range: &lt;5 cells/\muL); mostly monocytes and granulocytes; glucose quotient slightly reduced; and proteins were normal. Multiplex polymerase chain reaction from cerebrospinal fluid (CSF) was negative for *Escherichia coli*, *Neisseria meningitidis*, *Haemophilus influenza*, *Listeria monocytogenes*, *Streptococcus agalactiae/pneumonia*, *cytomegalovirus*, *enterovirus*, *herpes simplex virus 1/2*, *varicella zoster virus*, *human herpesvirus 6*, *parechovirus*, and *cryptococcus neoformans/gattii*. The results for three COVID-19 tests, influenza test, tick-borne encephalitis,
L1593: Borrelia, and Epstein-Barr virus were negative. The subject was hospitalized. Laboratory test results for tuberculosis, Aspergillus antigen in CSF, and human immunodeficiency virus test were negative. A magnetic resonance imaging scan of the head showed meningeal enhancement in frontal ventricular system and around third ventricle. Her condition was diagnosed as aseptic meningitis. The headache improved to 4 out of 10, 2 days after onset. Antibiotic and antiviral therapy were stopped. The headaches resolved 5 days after onset and the subject was discharged from the hospital. The study drug was withdrawn due to aseptic meningitis and the subject discontinued from the study. The SAE was considered severe and related to rozanolixizumab.
L1594: There have been 2 additional cases of aseptic meningitis in studies evaluating rozanolixizumab for the treatment of myelin oligodendrocyte glycoprotein antibody disease (MOG-AD) (Module 2.7.4. Summary of Clinical Safety; see Section 7.7.1). These subjects received rozanolixizumab 560 mg or placebo. Both subjects developed symptoms 2 days after the first infusion and recovered 6 days and 12 days after onset, respectively. The pathophysiology of drug induced aseptic meningitis is not understood.
L1595: ## Acute Respiratory Failure
L1596: The SAE occurred in subject MG0003/ [REDACTED] (b) (6) who was randomized to rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg treatment during Study MG0003 in the setting of tracheal device dislocation and is described in Section 7.6.2.3.
L1597: ## Gastrointestinal Disorders
L1598: - Subject MG0007/ [REDACTED] (b) (6) experienced hematochezia and abdominal pain and SAE large intestinal polyp 133 days after the first and 21 days after the most recent rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg infusion in Study MG0007. She received placebo in Study MG0003. The subject was hospitalized, and the polyp was excised endoscopically. The pathology showed a low-grade tubular adenoma and high-grade tubulovillous adenoma. The study drug dose was not changed. The SAE of large intestinal polyp was unlikely related to rozanolixizumab given the time to onset, relatively short exposure to rozanolixizumab (12 weeks), and a prior history of positive occult blood test before the study entry.
- Subject MG0007/ [REDACTED] (b) (6) experienced SAE of MG crisis and esophageal food impaction (see MG crisis above). The SAE of esophageal food impaction due to a meat bolus likely caused MG crisis and these events were unlikely related to rozanolixizumab.
- Subject MG0007/ [REDACTED] (b) (6) experienced SAE of duodenal ulcer bleeding 3 days after receiving five SC infusions of rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg dose in Study MG0007. The subject reported vomiting 10 to 11 times per day and diarrhea 9 to 10 times per day. The subject was hospitalized due to dehydration and electrolyte abnormalities. The symptoms resolved nine days after onset. The study drug dose was reduced to \approx7 mg/kg due to the SAE. The SAE of duodenal ulcer bleeding was unlikely related to rozanolixizumab because the subject had a previous history of gastric ulcer perforation, duodenal stenosis, and a silent duodenal ulcer. A potential role of rozanolixizumab in the events of vomiting and diarrhea cannot be ruled out given the frequency of these events in the subjects treated with rozanolixizumab in the study, the suggestive time to onset and a positive rechallenge (the subject had experienced moderate diarrhea on the day of the first infusion).
L1599: Other gastrointestinal SAEs included gastritis and vomiting in one subject each receiving rozanolixizumab \approx7 mg/kg infusion during Study MG0003.
L1600: ## Neoplasms
L1601: - Subject MG0007/ [REDACTED](b) (6) experienced SAE of retroperitoneal neoplasm and thymoma, and adrenal disorder 263 days after the first and fourth day after the most recent rozanolixizumab \approx7 mg/kg infusion in Study MG0007. The subject had received a total of seven infusions in Study MG0007. The subject was randomized to placebo in Study MG0003. A CT scan showed abnormalities suggestive of two moderate thymomas, moderate focal lesion in the left kidney and adrenal gland, and moderate neoplastic infiltrations in retroperitoneal space. The study drug was withdrawn due to SAE and the subject discontinued from the study. Thymoma is a known association of gMG. The SAEs of a lesion in the left kidney and adrenal gland and retroperitoneal neoplasm were unlikely related to rozanolixizumab
- Subject MG0007/ [REDACTED](b) (6) experienced SAE of invasive ductal breast carcinoma before the first dose of rozanolixizumab in Study MG0007. The subject was randomized to placebo in Study MG0003. The study drug was withdrawn. The SAE was unlikely related to rozanolixizumab.
- Subject ID MG0003/ [REDACTED](b) (6) experienced oral herpes, metastatic squamous cell carcinoma in the inguinal region and femoral vein thrombosis 26 days after the first and fourth day after the fourth rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg SC infusion in Study MG0003 that led to treatment discontinuation. The SAE was unlikely related to rozanolixizumab because the subject had episodes of squamous cell carcinoma before enrolling into Study MG0003 and a relatively short duration of exposure to rozanolixizumab (4 weeks).
L1602: ## Pericarditis
L1603: - Subject MG0004/ [REDACTED](b) (6) experienced SAE of moderately severe pericarditis 4 days after three infusions of rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg in Study MG0004. The subject had received six infusions of rozanolixizumab \approx7 mg/kg in Study MG0003. Pericarditis was diagnosed based on echocardiogram and elevated C-reactive protein. The episode resolved in 6 days after onset. The study drug was interrupted due to SAE. The subject completed six infusions in MG0004 and rolled into Study MG0007. The subject had no prior history of cardiac disease. A potential role of rozanolixizumab in the SAE of pericarditis cannot be ruled out.
L1604: ## Myocardial Infarction
L1605: - Subject MG0007/ [REDACTED] (b) (6) experienced SAE of myocardial infarction 55 days after the most recent infusion of rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg in Study MG0007. The subject had cardiac risk factors such as hypertension, obesity, and a prior history of renal stent placement. The event was considered resolved on the same day after angioplasty and three stent placements. The study drug dose was considered interrupted due to SAE. The study drug dose was unchanged and the subject continued receiving rozanolixizumab in Study MG0007. The SAE of myocardial infarction was unlikely related to rozanolixizumab because the time to onset for the event was less suggestive and the subject had alternative etiologies of medical comorbidities.

L1610: Serious adverse events defined as any untoward medical occurrence that, at any dose that results in death, is life-threatening, requires hospitalization or prolongation of existing hospitalization, results in persistent incapacity or substantial disruption of the ability to conduct normal life functions, or is a congenital anomaly or birth defect.
L1611: Treatment-emergent adverse events defined in the three studies as: MG0003 - as any AE starting on or after the time of first rozanolixizumab administration up to and including 8 weeks (56 days) after the final dose. Adverse events starting before the date of the first administration of rozanolixizumab or after 8 weeks following the final dose of rozanolixizumab were not considered TEAEs, MG0004 - as any event that was not present before the first administration of rozanolixizumab or any unresolved event already present before the first administration of rozanolixizumab that worsened in intensity following exposure to treatment, up to and including 8 weeks after the end of the Treatment Period or 8 weeks after the last rozanolixizumab administration in study subjects who discontinued the study or rozanolixizumab, MG0007 - defined as any AE starting on or after the time of first rozanolixizumab administration up to and including 8 weeks (56 days) after the last dose of each treatment cycle.
L1612: Duration: MG0003 to 6 weeks of treatment followed by 8 weeks observation period to total 14 weeks, MG0004 - first 6 weeks of all study subjects, MG0007 - is treatment period per cycle is 6 weeks followed by an observation period of up to 16 weeks totaling up to 22 weeks per cycle and repeat cycles as needed. The maximum number of cycles is 10 by data cutoff date.

L1626: ## 17.5. Treatment-Emergent Adverse Events
## Leading to Treatment Discontinuation in
## the Subjects Treated with
## Rozanolixizumab, Safety Population,
## Safety Pool S1, Studies MG0003, MG0004,
## and MG0007
L1627: In Safety Pool S1, a total of 29 (14.8%) subjects discontinued rozanolixizumab treatment due to adverse events (Table 101). The most common TEAEs leading to treatment discontinuation were MG worsening including crisis (n=7), infections (n=6), and aseptic meningitis (n=1). The brief case summaries of these subjects are described in the SAE section above. One subject discontinued treatment due to headache. Five subjects discontinued treatment due to abnormal investigations including blood immunoglobulin G decreased (n=2), Interferon gamma release assay positive (n=2), and liver function test increased (n=1).
L1628: ## Headache
L1629: Subject MG0003/ [REDACTED] (b) (6) discontinued treatment after the first rozanolixizumab \approx7 mg/kg infusion in Study MG0003 due to severe headache. Four days after the first dose of rozanolixizumab treatment, the subject experienced pulsatile moderately severe headache, nausea, photophobia, phonophobia, and vertigo. She reported pyrexia (fevers; unspecified) during this event. The subject received ibuprofen and paracetamol. The event resolved 4 days after onset. There is no additional clinical information in the case narrative. Given the suggestive time to onset the adverse event (AE) of headache was likely related to rozanolixizumab.
L1630: ## Blood Immunoglobulin G Decreased
L1631: Two (1 percent) subjects MG0007/ [REDACTED] (b) (6) and MG0007/ [REDACTED] (b) (6) discontinued treatment due to hypogammaglobulinemia on rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg and \approx7 mg/kg treatment, respectively in Study MG0007. The lowest IgG levels were &lt;1.09 g/L and 1.81 g/L, respectively (normal reference: 7.57 to 19.41 g/L). No infections were reported during treatment cycles according to the available information in MG0007 case narratives. Serum IgG reductions are expected due to rozanolixizumab mechanism of action.
L1632: ## Interferon Gamma Release Assay (IGRA) Positive
L1633: Two (1%) subjects discontinued treatment due to IGRA test positive in Study MG0007. Subject MG0007/ [REDACTED] (b) (6) received six infusions of rozanolixizumab \approx7 mg/kg dose level and tested IGRA positive from the blood sample taken during the last visit at 99 days in Study MG0003. At the time of the test result the subject had received the first rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg infusion in Study MG0007. Quantiferon Gold Plus tuberculosis test was positive. Clinical presentation and follow up for the tuberculosis were not described in the case narrative. A potential role of rozanolixizumab cannot be ruled out however, the reviewer notes that the subject resides in a country with a high risk of tuberculosis. Subject MG0007/ [REDACTED] (b) (6) had a positive IGRA test
L1634: after the first rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg infusion in Study MG0007. Quantiferon Gold Plus tuberculosis (TB) was negative. The subject had received placebo in Study MG0003. The subject later developed MG worsening 43 days after the most recent rozanolixizumab infusion. The event of a positive IGRA test was unlikely related to rozanolixizumab because the subject received only one dose of rozanolixizumab and tested positive on study entry.
L1635: ## Abnormal Liver Function Tests
L1636: Subject MG0007/ [REDACTED](b) (6) discontinued treatment due to increased liver enzymes (aspartate aminotransferase [AST] 441 U/L [normal reference: 5 to 34 u/L], ALT 861 [normal reference: 0 to 55 u/L], lactate dehydrogenase 423 U/L [normal reference: 125 to 220 u/L]) which occurred after the first infusion of rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg in Study MG0007 and was attributed to cholelithiasis (gall stones). Serum bilirubin and alkaline phosphatase levels remained within reference range. The subject received ursodeoxycholic acid as a treatment to dissolve gall stones and the TEAE resolved in 36 days after onset. The subject had received five infusions of rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg in Study MG0003 without abnormalities in liver function tests. The TEAE of abnormal liver function was unlikely related to rozanolixizumab.

L1641: Treatment-emergent adverse events defined in the three studies as: MG0003 - as any AE starting on or after the time of first rozanolixizumab administration up to and including 8 weeks (56 days) after the final dose. L1644: ## 17.6. Treatment-Emergent Adverse Events, Safety Population, Safety Pool S1, Studies MG0003, MG0004, and MG0007
L1645: Table 102 lists the TEAEs in the Safety Pool S1 with a cutoff frequency of at least 5% in the either dose of rozanolixizumab arm consistent with the cutoffs used labeling for efgartigimod, eculizumab, and ravulizumab, the monoclonal antibodies approved for the indication of anti-AChR antibody seropositive MG.
L1646: Overall, the incidence of most frequent TEAEs in the rozanolixizumab treated subjects in Safety Pool S1 was consistent with that observed during the double-blind placebo-controlled treatment cycle in Study MG0003. The most common system organ class with TEAEs \ge5% frequency was nervous system disorders followed by infections, gastrointestinal disorders, general disorders, and administration site conditions (Table 95). The most frequently reported TEAEs were headache, infections including upper respiratory tract infections, diarrhea, pyrexia, injection site reactions, musculoskeletal pain, abdominal pain, and nausea. The most frequent abnormal
L1647: laboratory test results included decreased blood immunoglobulin levels. Serum IgG reductions are expected due to rozanolixizumab mechanism of action.
L1648: Relatively more subjects in the rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg dose group reported infections compared to the \approx7 mg/kg dose group. The incidence of other TEAEs were balanced between the rozanolixizumab treatment arms.

L1685: ## 17.7. 120-Day Safety Update (Cutoff Date
## October 7, 2022)
L1686: On February 3, 2023, the Applicant submitted 3 months of additional safety data from the OLE Study MG0007 between the original BLA submission data cutoff date July 8, 2022, and 120-day safety update data cutoff date October 7, 2022.
L1687: ## BLA 761286
## Rystiggo (rozanolixizumab)
L1688: No new subjects were included in this safety update. There were no new treatment-emergent deaths in this safety update. Three additional subjects discontinued treatment due to TEAEs of appendicitis and endocarditis in one subject each in rozanolixizumab \approx10 mg/kg group and intestinal adenocarcinoma in one subject in rozanolixizumab \approx7 mg/kg group. The description of endocarditis leading to death was included in the original BLA submission (Section 17.3). No new cases of aseptic meningitis were identified. Four additional subjects developed serious TEAEs since the original BLA submission including intestinal adenocarcinoma (n=1) and nephrolithiasis (n=1) in rozanolixizumab 7 mg/kg group, and MG (n=1) and appendicitis (n=1) in rozanolixizumab 10 mg/kg group.
L1689: Compared to original BLA submission, there were no clinically significant changes in the relative incidence of common TEAEs (\ge5% frequency) in the safety update. Most common TEAEs continued to be in the system organ class of nervous system disorders (headache, MG worsening), infections (upper respiratory tract infections, oral herpes, COVID-19), gastrointestinal disorders (diarrhea, nausea, abdominal pain, vomiting), and general disorders and administration site conditions.
L1690: There were two new TEAEs of decreased blood immunoglobulin which is expected given the mechanism of drug action; one event led to temporary drug discontinuation. The hematology parameters showed a decrease in the mean platelet counts during treatment period which returned to baseline by day 99. The mean change from baseline to day 43 in the platelet counts (10^9/L) was -11.2 in rozanolixizumab 7 mg/kg group (n=80) and -24.7 in rozanolixizumab 10 mg/kg group (n=77) which is comparable to the data in the original BLA submission. The reviewer notes that the maximum lowest mean platelet count was 248.6 \times 10^9/L on day 43 in the subjects treated with rozanolixizumab for either dose, which was still within the normal reference range 150 to 400 \times 10^9/L, therefore reduction in the platelet counts were not clinically significant in the subjects receiving repeat treatment cycles of rozanolixizumab.
L1691: The mean lipid levels remained stable over time. The reductions in serum albumin levels remained less than 5% of baseline values and were not considered clinically meaningful.
L1692: No clinically significant changes were identified in vital signs and 12-lead electrocardiograms.

L1701: ## 22. Data Integrity-Related Consults (Office of Scientific Investigations, Other Inspections)
L1702: As further described in the clinical inspection summary report from the Office of Scientific Investigations, the clinical sites of Drs. Bril, Druzdz, and Pascuzzi, and the Applicant, UCB Inc., were inspected in support of this BLA and covered protocol MG0003. The study appears to have been conducted adequately, and the data generated by these sites and submitted by the Applicant appear acceptable in support of the respective indication. The procedures used to maintain the blind even when blinded staff primed the infusion line appear adequate.
L1703: Primary efficacy data, Myasthenia Gravis-Activities of Daily Living (MG-ADL), and secondary efficacy data, Quantitative Myasthenia Gravis (QMG), were verified; no discrepancies were identified between source and Applicant's data for the double-blind treatment phase of the study. Discrepancies in efficacy data were identified during observation period due to incorrect scores entered into the eCRF at Dr. Bril's site. One subject had an increase in prednisone dose during observation period which was considered a protocol deviation at Dr. Pascuzzi's site. According to the Applicant, there was one incident of accidental unblinding caused by an employee during observation period. However, these discrepancies should not impact the overall efficacy analysis because these events occurred after the subjects had completed the double-blind treatment period which was the timepoint of interest for the primary and secondary efficacy endpoints in Study MG0003. Additionally, there was no evidence of under-reporting of adverse events.
L1704: ## 23. Labeling: Key Changes and Considerations
L1705: This Prescribing Information (PI) review includes a high-level summary of the rationale for major changes to the finalized PI as compared to the Applicant's draft PI (Table 104). The PI was reviewed to ensure that PI meets regulatory/statutory requirements, is consistent (if appropriate) with labeling guidance, conveys clinically meaningful and scientifically accurate information needed for the safe and effective use of the drug, and provides clear and concise information for the healthcare practitioner.
L1706: **Table 104. Key Labeling Changes and Considerations**
L1707: Full PI Sections1	Rationale for Major Changes to Finalized PI2 Compared to Applicant's Draft PI
BOXED WARNING	Not applicable.

1 INDICATIONS AND USAGE	No substantial changes.
L1708: - A “Recommended Vaccination” subsection (2.1) was added based on the recommendation in the Guidance for Industry, Dosage and Administration Section of Labeling for Human Prescription Drug and Biological Products — Content and Format, that if there is important information about administering other drugs before initiating the subject drug, that information should be included in this section.
- In the recommended dosage table (Table 1), the following revisions were made:(b) (4) was removed given the typical body weight of adults, for which the drug will be indicated.
- A column for “Volume to be Infused” was added to complete the dosing information to help mitigate medication errors.
- A new weight category of “100 kg and above” with a recommended dosage of 840 mg was added. For patients in this weight category, the efficacy of rozanolixizumab at the proposed fixed dose regimen of (b) (4) mg cannot be reliably predicted; therefore, the recommended dosage for patients weighing over 100 kg was revised to 840 mg, as evaluated in Study MG0003.
- To provide additional guidance on the administration of subsequent cycle, the following statement was added: “The safety of initiating subsequent cycles sooner than 63 days from the start of the previous treatment cycle has not been established.”
- The Applicant had proposed a subsection titled “(b) (4)”. The information proposed under this subsection was moved to the appropriate respective locations in the Dosage and Administration section.
- Under the “Preparation and Administration Instructions” subsection (2.3), the following revisions were made:Information was reordered to better align with the steps to be taken for preparation and administration of the drug.
- Figures and text that is standard medical practice for healthcare providers was removed.
- The time recommended to allow the vials to reach room temperature was revised from “(b) (4)” to “approximately 30 minutes” because it was confirmed that 30 minutes is enough time for the vials to reach room temperature.
- Text was added to inform healthcare providers that the dose should be administered within 4 hours of puncturing the vial (removing the contents) or be discarded. Lack of microbial data does not support a longer time period. In addition, text was added to inform healthcare providers to administer the drug immediately after priming.

L1723: ## 24.1. Postmarketing Requirements
L1724: The following postmarketing requirements (PMRs) were required and agreed upon with the Applicant:
L1725: ## PMR 4427-1
L1726: Conduct a worldwide descriptive study that collects prospective and retrospective data in women exposed to rozanolixizumab during pregnancy and/or lactation to assess risk of pregnancy and maternal complications, adverse effects on the developing fetus and neonate, and adverse effects on the infant. Infant outcomes will be assessed through at least the first year of life. The minimum number of patients will be specified in the protocol.

L1734: ## 24.2. Postmarketing Commitments
L1735: The following postmarketing commitments were agreed upon by the Applicant:
L1736: ## PMC 4427-3
L1737: Develop a two-tiered reference standard system consisting of primary and working reference standards to support the rozanolixizumab product lifecycle. The primary and working reference standard will be created from representative DS batches which have passed all release specifications. The first new reference standards will be qualified against the current reference standard [REDACTED] (b) (4) according to a predefined protocol. The results for qualification of new primary and working/secondary reference standards will be submitted in a Prior Approval Supplement. Alternatively, protocols for qualification and requalification of future primary and working/secondary reference standards may be submitted as a Prior Approval Supplement, which may facilitate a reduced reporting category for implementation of new reference standards.
L1738: ## PMC Milestones
L1739: - Final Report Submission: 2/2024
L1740: ## PMC 4427-4
L1741: Perform a shipping validation study under real time shipping conditions (i.e., temperature, mode of transport, shipping duration, and shipping containers and packing representative of the minimum and maximum load) using rozanolixizumab finished product in the final commercial container closure and packaging systems to verify the impact of shipping on the product quality of rozanolixizumab finished product. The shipping validation data will be submitted in accordance with 21 CFR 601.12.

L1801: LAURA A JAWIDZIK
06/26/2023 06:35:10 PM
L1802: Figure 1 illustrates the Study MG0003 Design, a randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled trial. The study consists of three main phases:
Screening Period (up to 4 weeks): 1:1:1 randomization.
Randomized Treatment Period (Day 1 to 6 weeks): Participants are randomized to receive either Rozanolixizumab \approx 10\text{mg/kg}, Rozanolixizumab \approx 7\text{mg/kg}, or Placebo.
Observation Period (8 weeks): Follows the randomized treatment period.
Rescue Therapy Protocol:
If rescue therapy is necessary (e.g., disease worsening), participants must discontinue the study drug, receive IVIg/PEX treatment, complete all study visits, and move into the Observation Period.
If rescue therapy is necessary, participants either rollover into the OLE study to receive rozanolixizumab, OR receive IVIg/PEX treatment and complete the Observation Period. Participants who receive rescue therapy cannot enroll into the OLE study.
Total duration per study participant: up to 18 weeks.
L1803: Kaplan-Meier Plot of Time to MG-ADL Response (RS). The plot shows the percentage of responders over time (days) for three groups: Placebo (N=67), RLZ ~7mg/kg (N=66), and RLZ ~10mg/kg (N=67). The RLZ groups show significantly higher response rates earlier than Placebo. At Day 43, the Placebo group has approximately 48% response, while the RLZ groups have approximately 70% response.
L1804: Figure 4 is a line graph showing the Mean IgG percent change from baseline (Y-axis, ranging from -50 to 50) versus Day after dose (X-axis, ranging from 0 to 100) for Anti-AChR Positive Patients. The graph compares Placebo, Rozanolixizumab 10mg/kg, and Rozanolixizumab 7mg/kg treatments (TRTA).
The Placebo group (Red line) shows a significant increase in IgG levels, starting at 54% at Day 0, peaking at 47% around Day 25, and reaching 50% at Day 100.
The Rozanolixizumab 10mg/kg group (Blue line) shows a decrease in IgG levels, starting at 54% at Day 0, dropping to -42% at Day 25, and recovering to 44% at Day 100.
The Rozanolixizumab 7mg/kg group (Green line) shows a decrease in IgG levels, starting at 54% at Day 0, dropping to -41% at Day 25, and recovering to 47% at Day 100.
Overall, the Placebo group shows a substantial increase in pathogenic IgG levels, while both Rozanolixizumab groups show a decrease or stabilization, suggesting efficacy in reducing pathogenic IgG levels.
L1805: Figure 5 is a line graph showing the Mean IgG percent change from baseline (Y-axis, ranging from -50 to 100) versus Day after dose (X-axis, ranging from 0 to 100) for Anti-MuSK Positive Patients. The graph compares Placebo, Rozanolixizumab 10mg/kg, and Rozanolixizumab 7mg/kg treatments (TRTA).
The Placebo group (Red line) shows a slight increase in IgG levels, starting at 4% at Day 0, peaking at 8% around Day 25, and reaching 6% at Day 100.
The Rozanolixizumab 10mg/kg group (Blue line) shows a decrease in IgG levels, starting at 4% at Day 0, dropping to -3% at Day 25, and recovering to 3% at Day 100.
The Rozanolixizumab 7mg/kg group (Green line) shows a decrease in IgG levels, starting at 4% at Day 0, dropping to -3% at Day 25, and recovering to 3% at Day 100.
Overall, the Placebo group shows a slight increase in pathogenic IgG levels, while both Rozanolixizumab groups show a decrease or stabilization, suggesting efficacy in reducing pathogenic IgG levels.
L1806: Figure 6: PK Simulation of Six Weekly SC Infusion of Rozanolixizumab for Different Dose Regimens Across Body Weight Groups. The figure consists of two stacked plots showing Rozanolixizumab Concentration (ug/mL) versus Time after dose (day) for a 35-50 kg subject. The top plot shows concentration over 40 days, and the bottom plot shows concentration over 40 days. The legend indicates three dose regimens: ~7 mg/kg (red line), ~10 mg/kg (green line), and 2-fixed dose (blue line). The concentration peaks are highest for the ~10 mg/kg regimen, followed by the 2-fixed dose regimen, and lowest for the ~7 mg/kg regimen.

L1839: Figure 37 is a schematic diagram illustrating the study design for Study MG0004. The study is divided into two parallel arms, both receiving weekly rozanolixizumab infusions for 52 weeks, followed by an 8-week observation phase.
The timeline is marked by weekly visits (Blue = Site Visit, Purple = Home Visit).

L1840: Diagram illustrating the Study Design, MG0007.
The study begins on Day 1 with the administration of Rozanolixizumab (~7mg/kg or 10mg/kg). This is followed by an Observation Period. The total duration per treatment cycle is up to 22 weeks, consisting of 6 weeks of treatment followed by 16 weeks of observation.
After the initial treatment cycle, subsequent cycles will be given based on MG symptom worsening.
Repeat the 6 + 16-week cycles as needed.
L1841: Figure 39 is a flowchart illustrating the entry schema for Study MG0007. Participants are sourced from Lead-in studies MG0003 and MG0004.
From MG0003: Participants who completed MG0003 or required rescue therapy in the Observation Period of MG0003 enter MG0007.
From MG0004: Participants who completed at least 6 scheduled visits in MG0004 for rozanolixizumab treatment enter MG0007.
Within MG0007, participants proceed through an Initial Fixed Treatment/Observation Cycle, followed by an Observation Period of Initial Fixed Cycle, leading to Treatment/Observation Cycle(s) based on MG worsening.
L1842: Figure 40 is a flowchart detailing subject entry from Study MG0003 (Pivotal) to the Open label extension Study MG0007.
Study MG0003 (N=200) included: RLZ\approx7mg/kg (n=66), RLZ\approx10mg/kg (n=67), and Placebo (n=67).
Subjects eligible for entry into MG0007 are Completers or participants requiring rescue therapy during the MG0003 observation period (N=105).
These 105 subjects were distributed into two groups for the Open label extension (MG0004):
N=71: RLZ\approx7mg/kg (n=35) and RLZ\approx10mg/kg (n=36).
N=60: Participants who have completed \ge 6 treatments visits in MG0004.
These two groups (N=71 and N=60) combined to form the OLE Study MG0007 (N=165), which included: RLZ\approx7mg/kg (n=88) and RLZ\approx10mg/kg (n=77).
